BAIRA members said the MoU (memorandum of understanding) between the two countries gave Malaysia the job of finalising the agencies, which let one group trade illegally, and Prothom Alo reported that a ring of private agencies in Malaysia assisted the ring <sup>[N38 (2024)](#n38)</sup>. BCSM said on 14 May 2025 that the 2021 MoU gave Malaysia the job of selecting agencies, which let a Malaysian company and some Bangladeshi agencies form a syndicate that pushed the cost of sending a worker to Tk 5.5 lakh <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. The expatriates' welfare adviser, Asif Nazrul, said on 20 August 2025 that the agreement the previous government signed itself says Malaysia will select the agencies, that it cannot be changed by force, and that Bangladesh had told Malaysia in every meeting that all recruiting agencies should get the chance, as in other countries <sup>[N129](#n129)</sup>.

The list grew in three steps to 100, and it is alleged that the ring's leaders took a large sum from each agency to put its name in <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147), [N165](#n165)</sup>; in a memorandum of 5 May 2025, BAIRA members put that price at Tk 5 crore an agency <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>. The agencies inside received at least Tk 1.5 lakh "syndicate fee" per worker while sitting idle, and part of it went to the ring's controllers in Malaysia, Prothom Alo reported (how it was charged: see [The processing agency: the toll gate](#the-processing-agency-the-toll-gate)) <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported that no other country sending workers to Malaysia (14, by its count in 2024 and July 2026; 11, by its count in September 2026) had such a syndicate arrangement <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147), [N62](#n62), [N76](#n76)</sup>.

Officials named on both sides: several UN experts said in a statement of 19 April 2024 that Bangladeshi migrant workers were being cheated in Malaysia and alleged that government officials of both countries were involved with the ring, and Bangladesh's expatriates' welfare secretary, Md Ruhul Amin, said on 21 April that the Ministry was reviewing it <sup>[N167 (2024)](#n167)</sup>. The ACC's summary of the allegations, reported in August 2024, said high-ranking officials of both the Bangladeshi and Malaysian governments were involved with the syndicate, and did not separately name Swapan, known as its ringleader <sup>[N168 (2024)](#n168)</sup>. Malaysia's High Commissioner in Dhaka, Haznah Md Hashim, said on 12 March 2024 that syndicates active in both countries were one cause of the problem, and on 29 May 2024 that there may be syndicates beyond the control of both governments <sup>[N38 (2024)](#n38), [N75 (2024)](#n75)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 22 July 2026 noted that the UN human rights office (OHCHR) had issued a statement on the plight of Bangladeshi workers in Malaysia <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>.

<a id="how-the-2026-list-was-made"></a>

#### 1.3.10 How the 2026 list was made

A Malaysian committee chose the 25 with no involvement of the Bangladesh government, the Ministry said; 17 of them had never sent a worker to Malaysia, by Prothom Alo's analysis of BMET data; and Prothom Alo reported allegations that Amin and Swapan again decided who joined <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. How it was made:

- **28 October 2025:** after the third Bangladesh-Malaysia Joint Working Group meeting, in Dhaka on 21 and 22 May, Malaysia sent Bangladesh its "recruiting agent selection criteria", and the Ministry said the same criteria would apply in future to Bangladesh, India, Nepal, Pakistan and Myanmar <sup>[N169](#n169)</sup>. The ten conditions for a private agency, as the Ministry published them <sup>[N169](#n169)</sup>:
  - at least five years of satisfactory operation since licensing;
  - proof of sending at least 3,000 workers abroad in the last five years;
  - experience in at least three destination countries in that time;
  - valid licences from all relevant Bangladeshi authorities;
  - a good-conduct certificate from the sending country's authority;
  - no record of forced labour, trafficking, labour-law breaches, extortion, money laundering or other financial crime;
  - its own training and assessment centre with accommodation;
  - testimonials from five different foreign employers;
  - a permanent office of at least 10,000 square feet, run for at least three years;
  - proof of lawfully following destination countries' recruitment conditions, Malaysia's included.

  The Ministry asked licensed agencies able to meet them to apply with supporting papers by 7 November 2025, and said Bangladesh would ask Malaysia to list every agency that met them without exception <sup>[N169](#n169)</sup>.
- **19 November 2025:** Asif Nazrul said Bangladesh had objected to several of the conditions and asked for the deadline to be extended, telling Malaysia that if they were kept there would again be a syndicate with only a few agencies able to send workers, and that Malaysia had promised to relax them <sup>[N170](#n170)</sup>.
- Malaysia set 10 conditions; 163 agencies first qualified, and after Malaysia relaxed three conditions and then one more at Bangladesh's request, the Ministry under the interim government sent a final list of 423, Prothom Alo reported <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>.
- **9 April 2026:** the two governments' joint statement said Malaysia had begun introducing an AI-based, technology-driven recruitment system for all source countries, meant to cut middlemen and migration cost and bring the cost to workers to zero in line with the ILO (International Labour Organization) "employer pays" principle; Bangladesh said it fully supported such a digital platform <sup>[N50](#n50)</sup>.
- **30 July 2026:** Mahdi Amin, the Prime Minister's spokesperson, said Bangladesh had asked Malaysia to run the first phase of recruitment through BOESL, and that under the MoU valid until December 2026 Malaysia would set the final number of Bangladeshi agencies on its own vetting, from the list of 423 <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.
- **21 August 2026:** Malaysia published the 25 on FWCMS <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>.
- **28 August 2026:** the Ministry's notice said a high-level joint committee of Malaysia's human resources, foreign and home ministries, its anti-corruption commission and law-enforcement agencies vetted the agencies, with not the slightest involvement of the Bangladesh government <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>. It said that because the MoU signed in 2022 gives Malaysia's government alone the job of choosing Bangladeshi agencies and runs until 31 December 2026, it is not easy for Bangladesh to change Malaysia's conditions, and that it hopes a new MoU after that date will protect Bangladesh's interest in approving agencies <sup>[N171](#n171)</sup>.
- **September 2026:** Malaysia approved 25 agencies each for Nepal and Bangladesh and 10 each for India, Pakistan and Myanmar, while everyone on the lists sent by countries such as Sri Lanka and the Philippines was approved; the agency owners' associations of four countries protested in a joint statement, and Nepal has begun investigating its list of 25, Prothom Alo reported <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>.

<a id="objections-and-allegations"></a>

#### 1.3.11 Objections and allegations

In order:

- **28 May 2024:** S M Rafiq, managing director of United Export Limited, itself a syndicate member, wrote to the Prime Minister's Office that the main figures in sending money from Bangladesh to Malaysia were Swapan and his Malaysian partner Amin <sup>[N153 (2024)](#n153)</sup>.
- **22 August 2024:** in a letter to the Chief Adviser, BAIRA member Mostafa Mahmud alleged that Amin and Ruhul Amin had also built a Malaysia syndicate in 2017 and that over the two rounds the ring looted Tk 17,544 crore <sup>[N153 (2024)](#n153)</sup>.
- **September 2024:** Iftekharuzzaman, executive director of TIB (Transparency International Bangladesh), said workers from neighbouring countries go to Malaysia at the employer's cost while Bangladesh's rules are different, and that a ring in Malaysia acts as the representative of the Bangladeshi ring <sup>[N153 (2024)](#n153)</sup>.
- **3 October 2024:** at a BAIRA press conference Fakhrul Islam read a statement naming Swapan as the syndicate's main ringleader, built with former MPs and ministers including Benzir Ahmed, Nizam Hazari, Masud Uddin Chowdhury and Awami League leader Mohiuddin Mohi, and saying no action had been taken against them <sup>[N115 (2024)](#n115)</sup>. BAIRA's eight demands included ending Malaysia's power to select Bangladeshi agencies, letting the employer choose the agency, a central online system from the Ministry so all agencies could send workers at low or no cost, and sending the workers stranded by the syndicate again at low cost and free of it <sup>[N115 (2024)](#n115)</sup>.
- **5 February 2025:** at a human chain outside the Ministry, BAIRA leaders said the Amin-Swapan ring was manoeuvring to form a syndicate again around the reopening; a memorandum signed by 12 members, including Riazul Islam, Noman Chowdhury, Khandaker Abu Ashfaq and Md Fakhrul Islam, alleged it had extorted Tk 1,07,000 from each worker and demanded strict legal action against the two <sup>[N172](#n172)</sup>.
- **11 March 2025:** BAIRA members asked Asif Nazrul that no ring be allowed to form again, saying that under the previous government MPs and ministers had formed one and plundered the market <sup>[N173](#n173)</sup>.
- **April 2025:** agency owners told Prothom Alo that the old syndicate was trying to re-form, dropping agencies owned by Awami League leaders and bringing in people from other parties, and that at least two lists of agencies for it had been found; Ruhul Amin called it a rumour created by one group, and Amin could not be reached <sup>[N29](#n29)</sup>. BAIRA members alleged that last time the ringleaders took Tk 5 crore from each member agency in advance as a security deposit, and that this time they would take RM50 lakh (Tk 12.5 crore), to be adjusted once sending starts <sup>[N29](#n29)</sup>. The Ministry's joint secretary (employment wing), A Z M Nurul Haque, said there was no scope for a syndicate and amending parts of the MoU would be discussed with Malaysia; Andy Hall, a UK migration and human-rights campaigner, said on 7 April 2025 that moves to form a syndicate were again under way and the clauses handing control to it must be amended <sup>[N29](#n29)</sup>.
- **21 April 2025:** 453 agency owners signed a memorandum to the Ministry <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>. It said that although Malaysia takes workers from 14 countries it selects specific agencies for none of them, that the syndicate's seed was sown in the MoU that gave Malaysia's government the job of selecting Bangladesh's agencies, and that the ringleaders' claim that Malaysia would not take workers without a syndicate was untrue <sup>[N157](#n157)</sup>. Afterwards, former BAIRA joint secretary general Mohammad Fakhrul Islam said the Ministry's senior secretary had promised to work on amending the MoU; BNP Dhaka district president and BAIRA member Khandaker Abu Ashfaq said some were trying to add new people to the old syndicate and no one in the BNP believed in one <sup>[N157](#n157)</sup>.
- **24 April 2025:** at a human chain under the banner of BAIRA's general members outside Probashi Kallyan Bhaban in Eskaton, Dhaka, members said a vested-interest group was obstructing the reopening with false allegations of human trafficking and money laundering in Malaysia <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>.
- **5 May 2025:** BAIRA members' memorandum to the Chief Adviser's office alleged that the ringleaders, Amin Nor and the fugitive Swapan, and their Awami League associates were forming a syndicate with some influential political leaders, and that agencies, made members for Tk 5 crore each last time, were this time being charged Tk 14 crore each; it asked that Malaysia be asked to scrap the disputed software <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>. It proposed instead that BMET build a database of interested workers, agencies take workers from it, each worker deposit the government-fixed cost through a bank, and BMET supervise the whole process; the 453 agency owners had made the same demand to the Ministry on 21 April 2025 <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>.
- **19 May 2025:** before a press conference called by a BAIRA faction at the Dhaka Reporters Unity, Mohammad Fakhrul Islam and others were injured in fighting; he alleged the attackers were linked to the old syndicate and came on the orders of Swapan and Kazi Rafiq <sup>[N174](#n174)</sup>. His faction demanded that two MoU clauses be scrapped or amended so that Malaysian employers, not Malaysia's government, could choose Bangladeshi agencies; at a rival press conference Mezbah Uddin Sayeed, a director of Instance International Limited, said there had been no syndicate and the name was hype, and Al Akaba Associates' proprietor Atikur Rahman Atik called the other faction Awami League collaborators trying to close the market <sup>[N174](#n174)</sup>.
- **20 August 2025:** Asif Nazrul said that when recruitment is given to particular agencies workers are cheated, migration cost rises and many kinds of corruption occur <sup>[N129](#n129)</sup>. Tasneem Siddiqui, acting executive director of RMMRU, said that despite various pressures the interim government had not let a syndicate form <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>.
- **18 December 2025:** a High Court bench of Justice Fatema Najib and Justice Fatema Anwar issued a rule, on a writ by Afia Overseas and two other agencies, asking why the Ministry's 18 November 2025 notice setting the 10 criteria should not be declared without lawful authority, and gave the Ministry and BMET's director general 10 days to reply <sup>[N175](#n175)</sup>. The petitioners' lawyer, Md Atikur Rahman, said the criteria would get a particular group selected, create a monopoly and exclude ordinary agencies, against an Appellate Division judgment and the Competition Act <sup>[N175](#n175)</sup>.
- **7 January 2026:** RMMRU's report "Trends in labour migration from Bangladesh 2025" said trying to reopen Malaysia again through the old syndicate's leaders without bringing them to justice was unacceptable <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>. It said India has 192 recruiting agencies, Pakistan 464, Nepal 416 and Sri Lanka 248, while Bangladesh's, once limited to 850 to 900, were raised to 2,300 by the previous government, with 252 new licences approved in 2025 and, Tasneem Siddiqui added, 300 more in process <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>.
- **March 2026:** Fakhrul Islam told Prothom Alo that the Malaysia market cannot be opened through a ring <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>.
- **30 July 2026:** Mahdi Amin said Bangladesh had told Malaysia that nobody involved in corruption as part of the syndicate would be on the new agency list, and that the Malaysian authorities would confirm this shortly <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.
- **22 and 23 August 2026:** the opposition National Citizen Party (NCP) and Jamaat-e-Islami issued statements of concern <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. The NCP's spokesperson, Asif Mahmud, said the process, criteria and basis on which only 25 were chosen from the 423 were still not clear <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. Jamaat feared the arrangement could push a worker's cost to Tk 6 to 7 lakh, and said Bangladesh has more than two thousand valid recruiting agencies <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>.
- **September 2026:** several BAIRA members told Prothom Alo that the earlier syndicate agencies and the firms of ministers or MPs are not directly on the list, but the old ringleaders are behind the unfamiliar agencies, and that workers may have to spend Tk 6 to 7 lakh <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>. Shariful Hasan, head of BRAC's migration programme, said the choice of 25 proved the syndicate is stronger than the two governments and that workers are again being recruited through Amin Nur's system <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>. The state minister for expatriates' welfare, Md Nurul Haque, said the Ministry would set a cost ceiling, and that if any agency accused of trafficking or corruption is on the list, Malaysia would be told and its licence withdrawn <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>. In a memorandum to the expatriates' welfare secretary on 16 September, BAIRA members demanded equal opportunity for all valid and eligible agencies including the 312 listed ones, an end to the syndicate and monopoly, a significant cut in migration cost, full transparency, opening up all eligible medical centres, and a one-stop service through BOESL <sup>[N69](#n69)</sup>. At a Jamaat seminar on 17 September, the opposition leader and Jamaat Ameer, Shafiqur Rahman, said the 25 appeared to be "master agencies" with associates under them, demanded the government explain the criteria when some had no previous experience, and said "we are hearing" that Tk 15 to 22 crore was being invested to get in, which he said could not happen without the government's involvement <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>. He alleged that the syndicate built in Malaysia under the previous government by a man he called Dato Sri Amin was still operating, and that some leaders still met him secretly <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What were agencies asked to pay to get onto the 2026 list: RM50 lakh (Tk 12.5 crore) <sup>[N29](#n29)</sup>, Tk 14 crore <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>, or Tk 15 to 22 crore <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>?

<a id="who-sent-the-workers-in-2022-24"></a>

#### 1.3.12 Who sent the workers in 2022-24

Prothom Alo reported in July 2026 that agencies of former MPs, Awami League leaders and a city corporation councillor, and many firms new to the sector, sent large numbers of workers after getting into the syndicate <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>. The agencies it named in May 2024, with what they sent and what they said:

- **Five M International:** the top single sender, with clearances for 8,592 workers, though BMET said it had sent about 2,500 workers to the Middle East since it was founded in 2015; its managing director, by BAIRA's website, is Lt Gen (retd) Masud Uddin Chowdhury, then an MP <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>. He went into business after retiring in 2014 and later obtained a recruiting licence <sup>[N177](#n177)</sup>. His reply: see [The processing agency: the toll gate](#the-processing-agency-the-toll-gate).
- **Oishi International** (second) and **New Age International** (third): relevant sources claimed to Prothom Alo that both were active syndicate members <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.
- **Snigdha Overseas Limited,** of Feni Awami League MP Nizam Uddin Hazari: licensed in December 2018, it sent only 100 workers abroad in three and a half years, then about 8,000 to Malaysia in a year and a half after joining the syndicate, the fourth-largest; its executive director, Shahela Parvin, said various agencies sent workers through it and it took nothing beyond the government-fixed cost <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.
- **Ahmed International,** of Dhaka-20 MP Benzir Ahmed, BAIRA president when the syndicate was formed: 238 workers abroad before the market opened, then 7,849 to Malaysia, the fifth-highest <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.
- **Orbitals Enterprise and Orbitals International,** in the names of Kashmeri Kamal and Nafisa Kamal, wife and daughter of the then finance minister, A H M Mustafa Kamal: 9,861 workers between them <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.
- **Catharsis International,** Swapan's agency: 7,102 workers by BMET's count <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.
- **BNS Overseas Limited,** of Ishtiaque Ahmed, son of BAIRA president Mohammad Abul Bashar; **PR Overseas,** of his son-in-law Golam Rakib (licensed October 2021, more than 4,000 workers sent); and **Sadia International,** of Shamim Ahmed Chowdhury, elected BAIRA secretary general in the last election and later resigned: all three joined the syndicate, though the Nur Ali-Abul Bashar panel had won BAIRA's 2022 election campaigning against it; Bashar said the ring listed the two family firms for money to damage his image, and his son said no money changed hands <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.
- **Imperial Overseas** (licensed March 2021): 344 workers sent by July 2022, more than 7,000 in the next year and a half; two agencies confirmed to Prothom Alo that they sent workers through it by paying the syndicate fee <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.
- **BM Travels Limited,** of Dhaka North ward 39 councillor Md Shafiqul Islam: 7,225 clearances, with no earlier Malaysia experience <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.
- **Ananya Apurba Recruiting Agency,** of Dhaka South Awami League joint general secretary Mohiuddin Ahmed (licensed July 2022): 2,600 clearances <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>.

**Their replies, 4 June 2024.** At a briefing that day, Nizam Uddin Hazari said there is no such word as syndicate, that Malaysia selected the agencies and his was chosen "coincidentally", and that he was not involved in sending workers or visa trading and only did processing; Benzir Ahmed said he had not gone to lobby Malaysia; and BAIRA president Mohammad Abul Bashar said the two governments' agreement let Malaysia choose the agencies and the MPs had a right to do business <sup>[N110 (2024)](#n110)</sup>. The written statement of the 101-agency panel said the 101 included the government agency BOESL and Md Noor Ali's agency, Unit Eastern Private Limited, and accused a group led by Noor Ali of a media smear campaign ahead of the BAIRA election despite doing business in Malaysia under various names <sup>[N110 (2024)](#n110)</sup>.

<a id="the-cases-against-the-syndicate"></a>

#### 1.3.13 The cases against the syndicate

The ACC has filed or approved cases against dozens of the 2022-24 agencies for charging up to five times the cap, and in July 2026 the Ministry cancelled 49 licences, Catharsis among them <sup>[N178](#n178), [N179](#n179), [N180](#n180)</sup>. The cases themselves: see [The ACC and CID cases](#the-acc-and-cid-cases); The Paltan trafficking case; Licences withdrawn. Complaints, inquiries and court orders, in order:

- **2017:** several agencies filed two separate complaints, with the ACC and with Malaysia's MACC (Malaysian Anti-Corruption Commission); BAIRA member Mostafa Mahmud said the ACC did not proceed because the accused were from the private sector, and alleged that Amin Nor used influence to stop the Malaysian investigation <sup>[N75 (2024)](#n75)</sup>.
- **March 2024:** a statement on 20 March by BCSM demanded punishment of those responsible for the closure; Tasneem Siddiqui, founding chair of RMMRU, said the agencies involved in past irregularities should have been held accountable and that the same recruitment process was repeated when the market reopened in 2022 <sup>[N38 (2024)](#n38)</sup>.
- **May 2024:** Prothom Alo reported that the Ministry had never acted despite repeated allegations of syndicate-forming and overcharging; on 11 May the state minister, Shafiqur Rahman Chowdhury, said Malaysia had set the list of agencies and the complaints were being looked into <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>. On 31 May the Ministry was not investigating separately and wanted to discuss the allegations at the two countries' joint technical committee, for which it had written to Malaysia; no date was fixed <sup>[N75 (2024)](#n75)</sup>.
- **June 2024:** the Ministry's probe committee on the irregularities and the stranded workers, headed by additional secretary (employment wing) Noor Md Mahbubul Haque, was told to report within seven working days, got five more on 11 June, and was collecting information from the immigration department, 101 recruiting agencies and BAIRA <sup>[N120 (2024)](#n120)</sup>. On 11 June the state minister said no syndicate would be accepted, the government wanted every agency to be able to work, and legal action would be taken against agencies whose workers got no work in Malaysia <sup>[N120 (2024)](#n120)</sup>.
- **June to July 2024:** Supreme Court lawyer Tanvir Ahmed filed a writ in the High Court attaching two 2 June 2024 press reports (one headed that 30,000 young men's dreams were broken and Tk 20,000 crore looted, the other that more than 3,000 workers got no ticket) and seeking a three-member inquiry committee; on 30 June the bench of Justices Mustafa Zaman Islam and S M Masud Hossain Dolon gave the state one week to report what a committee it had heard of had investigated and what action had been taken <sup>[N181 (2024)](#n181)</sup>. On 15 July the deputy attorney general, Tushar Kanti Roy, said the six-member committee's report had come <sup>[N182 (2024)](#n182)</sup>. On 16 July the bench ordered the authorities to file an update every three months on the steps taken over the mismanagement that left workers with final clearance unable to go, and issued a rule asking why their inaction should not be declared illegal and why the money those workers deposited should not be returned with profit and compensation paid <sup>[N183 (2024)](#n183)</sup>.
- **18 August 2024:** the ACC began an inquiry, by a three-member team led by deputy director Mohammad Nurul Huda, into four former MPs who traded through the syndicate, Nizam Uddin Hazari, Masud Uddin Chowdhury, Benzir Ahmed and A H M Mustafa Kamal, all in hiding since the government fell on 5 August, Mustafa Kamal reportedly in Singapore <sup>[N168 (2024)](#n168)</sup>.
- **21 August 2024:** the High Court bench of Justices Naima Haider and Sashanka Shekhar Sarkar, on a writ filed on 15 July by S M Rafiq, proprietor of United Export Limited, ordered the ACC and Bangladesh Bank's BFIU (Financial Intelligence Unit) to investigate the allegations of irregularity, corruption and money laundering and report within 90 days <sup>[N184 (2024)](#n184)</sup>. Its rule asked why a full policy should not be ordered to ensure workers pay only the government-fixed Tk 78,990, and why the agencies through whose negligence more than 17,000 workers could not go in time should not compensate them <sup>[N184 (2024)](#n184)</sup>.
- **2024-25:** police and the ACC began investigating the recruitment process, a complaint was made against the syndicate under the human trafficking law, and in October 2024 police asked Malaysia to arrest and return two migration businessmen accused of trafficking workers, money laundering and embezzlement, Mohammad Rashed Alam Bhuiyan, a Dhaka University associate professor and RMMRU researcher, wrote in Prothom Alo on 2 June 2025 <sup>[N117](#n117)</sup>. RMMRU's December 2024 report said the government had started legal proceedings against four MPs over the syndicate and had written to Malaysia asking it to hand over the two ringleaders <sup>[N164 (2024)](#n164)</sup>. In April 2025 Prothom Alo reported that the ACC was investigating the four former MPs, but that no action had yet been taken against the syndicate's main figures, Swapan and Amin <sup>[N29](#n29)</sup>.
- **23 April 2025:** in a letter reported by Bloomberg News, Azman Mohd Yusof, secretary general of Malaysia's human resources ministry, called on Bangladesh to review and withdraw "unsubstantiated allegations" of wrongdoing in labour migration between the two countries, to improve Malaysia's rating in the annual US trafficking report, Rashed Alam Bhuiyan wrote <sup>[N117](#n117)</sup>.
- **12 August 2025:** BCSM, in a statement signed by its chair Tasneem Siddiqui and co-chair Syed Saiful Haque, said news reports said three former MPs linked to the syndicate, including the family of former finance minister A H M Mustafa Kamal (Lotus Kamal), were being freed from the allegations, and that Malaysiakini had reported Bangladesh agreed to Malaysia's request to suspend the investigation into the syndicate; it demanded no impunity, a fresh and judicial investigation, and public reporting of the ACC's progress <sup>[N185](#n185)</sup>.
- **9 April 2026:** at a meeting in Putrajaya the two governments discussed concerns about the ongoing human-trafficking cases, and Bangladesh reaffirmed its commitment to rule of law, due process, accountability and timely justice, their joint statement said <sup>[N50](#n50)</sup>.

<a id="the-acc-and-cid-cases"></a>

#### 1.3.14 The ACC and CID cases

- **11 March 2025:** the ACC filed cases against 32 owners and officials of 12 agencies, alleging they took five times the government-fixed Tk 78,990 from 67,380 Malaysia-bound workers and embezzled Tk 1,128 crore, its director general, Md Akhtar Hossain, told a press conference <sup>[N186](#n186)</sup>. The extra sums alleged, by agency:
  - Orbital Enterprise (owner Kashmiri Kamal; Mustafa Kamal is accused in both Orbital cases): Tk 100.99 crore from 6,029 workers; Orbital International (owner Nafisa Kamal): Tk 50.17 crore from 2,995 <sup>[N186](#n186)</sup>;
  - Snigdha Overseas Limited (accused: owner and former MP Nizam Uddin Hazari, his wife Nurjahan Begum and five staff): Tk 111.50 crore from 6,657; Binimoy International (accused: owner Abdus Sobhan Bhuiyan, former Chauddagram upazila chairman, and his wife Taslima Akter): Tk 91.42 crore from 5,458 <sup>[N186](#n186)</sup>;
  - FiveM International (accused: owner and former Feni MP Lt Gen (retd) Masud Uddin Chowdhury, his daughter Tasnia Masud and two others who worked there): Tk 119.33 crore from 7,124; Unique Eastern Private Limited (accused: owner Md Noor Ali, his wife Selina Ali, daughter Nabila Ali and former secretary Khondaker Shawkat Hossain): Tk 63.45 crore from 3,788 <sup>[N186](#n186)</sup>;
  - Catharsis International Limited (accused: owner Mohammad Ruhul Amin and his wife Lutfur Nesa Shelly): Tk 130.43 crore from 7,787; Ahmad International (accused: owner and former Dhaka-20 MP Benzir Ahmad): Tk 143.92 crore from 8,592; BM Travels Limited (accused: owner and former Dhaka North councillor Shafiqul Islam and his wife Mousumi Akter): Tk 135.56 crore from 8,093 <sup>[N186](#n186)</sup>;
  - BNS Overseas Limited (accused: owner and Jubo League leader Ishtiaque Ahmed Saikat and his wife Nasrun Nesa): Tk 70.60 crore from 4,215; Rubel Bangladesh Limited (accused: owner Muhammad Mojibul Haque Rubel and his wife Kamrun Nahar Hiramoni): Tk 47.65 crore from 2,845; The Ifti Overseas (accused: owners Md Rubel and Borhan Uddin Panna): Tk 63.60 crore from 3,797 <sup>[N186](#n186)</sup>.
- **25 May 2025:** at the ACC's request, Dhaka senior special judge Md Zakir Hossain barred from leaving the country ten people linked to the Malaysia market whom the ACC said were trying to flee while it investigated overcharging and money laundering: Nizam Hazari and his wife Nurjahan Begum, Snigdha Overseas owners Sheikh Abdullah and M Amirul Islam, Catharsis International's managing director Mohammad Ruhul Amin, Unique Eastern's managing director Noor Ali, Nasir Uddin Ahmed and Khondoker Shawkat Hossain, BM Travels' chairman Shafiqul Islam, and BNS Overseas' managing director Ishtiaque Ahmed <sup>[N187](#n187)</sup>.
- **20 August 2025:** the CID (the police Criminal Investigation Department) said the Dhaka Metropolitan Sessions Judge's Court had, on its application, ordered the attachment of about Tk 500 crore of property of Ruhul Amin (Swapan), whom it called the chief of the Malaysia manpower syndicate, and of linked people and firms <sup>[N188](#n188)</sup>. The CID alleged that he embezzled Tk 8,000 crore through the syndicate and bought houses and land with it; the attached property included 231 katha of land in seven deeds in Bashundhara, Banani and Uttara in the name of Catharsis International, deed value Tk 15 crore 55 lakh 33 thousand <sup>[N188](#n188)</sup>. It said inquiries were continuing against the syndicate's other members and money-laundering action would be taken against them too <sup>[N188](#n188)</sup>.
- **28 August 2025:** a CID sub-inspector, Md Moniruzzaman, filed a case under the Money Laundering Prevention Act at Banani police station against 33 people of the syndicate, among them three former MPs, a former minister's wife and Swapan; it says an inquiry into laundering of money taken by fraud found preliminary evidence of their involvement <sup>[N189](#n189)</sup>. The accused include Masud Uddin Chowdhury, his wife Jesmin Masud and daughter Tasnia Masud, Benzir Ahmed, Nizam Uddin Hazari, Kashmeri Kamal and Ruhul Amin <sup>[N189](#n189)</sup>. The case statement alleges that the accused, in collusion, took money from 9,372 workers sent through Five M International, that Five M took Tk 1.5 lakh a worker, and that Tk 1,07,510 of it was laundered, more than Tk 100 crore in all, a figure that may change during investigation <sup>[N189](#n189)</sup>.
- **14 September 2025:** the ACC filed cases against the top officials of 13 more agencies over Tk 1,159 crore <sup>[N190](#n190), [N178](#n178)</sup>. The case names 31 chairmen, managing directors and directors and alleges that, while holding posts in BAIRA, they formed a syndicate and took five times the government-fixed Tk 78,990 from Malaysia-bound workers, its director general Akhtar Hossain said <sup>[N191](#n191)</sup>. The 13 are Akash Bhraman, Winner Overseas Limited, Shahin Travels, Navira Limited, Adib Air Travels and Tours, United Manpower Consultancy Limited, Greenland Overseas, PR Overseas Limited, Jaharat Associates Limited, Apurba Recruiting Agency, Jannat Overseas, Midway Overseas Limited and South Point Overseas Limited <sup>[N191](#n191)</sup>. The accused include A K M Mosharraf Hossain and Mohiuddin Ahmed, directors of Apurba Recruiting Agency; Mohammad Rafiqul Haldar Bhuiyan, chairman of Jannat Overseas; and Manzur Kader, chairman of Midway Overseas and South Point Overseas <sup>[N191](#n191)</sup>. With this case the ACC had taken legal action in 25 cases against 64 accused over about Tk 2,288 crore, counting the earlier 12-company cases over about Tk 1,128 crore, Prothom Alo reported <sup>[N191](#n191)</sup>.
- **4 November 2025:** the ACC approved six cases against 11 owners and officials of six agencies, alleging that on average five times the Tk 78,990 cap was collected from 31,331 Malaysia-bound workers and Tk 525.23 crore embezzled and laundered, its director general said on 6 November <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>. By agency:
  - Amin Tours and Travels (owner Ruhul Amin): Tk 124.45 crore extra from 7,430 people; Merit Trade International Limited (chairman Hasan Ahmed Chowdhury Kiron): Tk 58.44 crore from 3,489; Sadia International (Shamim Ahmed Chowdhury alias Noman): Tk 55.63 crore from 3,321 <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>;
  - Imperial Resource Limited (chairman Md Iqbal Hossain, managing director Badruddoza Chowdhury): Tk 135.69 crore from 8,101; RRC Human Resource Service Limited (chairman Mohammad Moniruzzaman, managing director Md Alamgir Kabir): Tk 87.13 crore extra from 5,202; Thanex International Limited (former manager Abdullah Shahed, director Md Joynal Abedin Nomani, current manager Shamser Ahmed): Tk 63.89 crore extra from 3,788 <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>.
- **13 November 2025:** the ACC filed a case against five agencies, GMG Trading Private Limited, The GMG Associate Limited, QK Quick Express Limited, MEF Global Bangladesh Limited and Dhamasi Corporation Limited, and their chairmen, directors and managing directors, alleging they took about Tk 314 crore 34 lakh 72 thousand 500 extra from about 18,767 Malaysia-bound people, on average five times the Tk 78,990, its director general (prevention) Akhtar Hossain said <sup>[N192](#n192)</sup>.
- **21 June 2026:** a Dhaka special judge, Md Sabbir Foyez, granted four days' remand of Masud Uddin Chowdhury (seven were sought) in the ACC case alleging that he and others, abusing government power, got themselves appointed as agents for sending workers to Malaysia, illegally collected Tk 119 crore 32 lakh 70 thousand from workers against the Tk 78,990 fee, and laundered it <sup>[N193](#n193)</sup>. He had been remanded several times in various cases and has been in jail since his arrest on 23 March 2026 (see [The Paltan trafficking case](#the-paltan-trafficking-case)) <sup>[N193](#n193)</sup>.

<a id="the-paltan-trafficking-case"></a>

#### 1.3.15 The Paltan trafficking case

- **3 September 2024:** Altab Khan, owner of a firm named Afia Overseas, filed a case at Paltan police station, Dhaka, alleging embezzlement through a syndicate and human trafficking over Malaysia recruitment, naming 103 accused, after the fall of the Awami League government <sup>[N177](#n177), [N194](#n194), [N195 (2024)](#n195)</sup>. The accused include former expatriates' welfare minister Imran Ahmad, former senior secretary of the expatriates' welfare ministry Ahmed Munirus Salehin, former Feni MP Nizam Hazari, former Dhaka-20 MP Benzir Ahmed, and Masud Uddin Chowdhury as accused no. 3, according to the case documents <sup>[N194](#n194)</sup>.
- **October 2024:** the Detective Branch (DB) of Dhaka Metropolitan Police arrested Imran Ahmad in Banani, Dhaka, on the night of 20 October, and on 21 October Magistrate Mehera Mahbub allowed three days' remand, against the 10 days police sought <sup>[N195 (2024)](#n195)</sup>. The remand petition, by investigating officer Sub-Inspector Nazmul Hasan, alleges that when the market opened on 1 October 2022 Altab Khan obtained demand letters from Malaysian companies through his own licence, but at the Ministry Imran Ahmad and its secretary told him the workers had to be sent through the 100 licences of their syndicate, and Tk 1.5 lakh per worker was then demanded for visa processing <sup>[N195 (2024)](#n195)</sup>. It says Khan was threatened after pointing out that the government-to-government arrangement allowed Tk 78,990 per worker, and that Tk 24,000 crore was laundered through the syndicate <sup>[N195 (2024)](#n195)</sup>. It also alleges that Imran Ahmad helped the ring by having his wife's elder brother's son open an app named "Ami Probashi", and that his ring created discrimination among businesses although there are more than two thousand recruiting agents <sup>[N195 (2024)](#n195)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Did the Malaysia market reopen to Bangladeshi workers in July 2022 <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup> or on 1 October 2022, as the remand petition says <sup>[N195 (2024)](#n195)</sup>?

- **15 July 2025:** the case was investigated first by Paltan police and then by the CID, which filed a final report calling it false and recommending action against the complainant; he filed a no-confidence petition, and the court ordered the DB to reinvestigate <sup>[N194](#n194)</sup>. The CID's supervising officer, Additional Special SP Mostafizur Rahman, said 76 of the 103 accused were on bail and the rest absconding, so they could not be questioned, that the complainant could not produce enough evidence, and that an extortion section in the case also got a final report, which the court accepted; Altab Khan said the CID had compromised with the accused and never told him the result <sup>[N194](#n194)</sup>.
- **December 2025:** after the DB began reinvestigating, accused no. 12, S M Rafiq, gave a confessional statement in court, which a DB officer said contains information that human trafficking and embezzlement of a huge sum took place in the name of sending workers to Malaysia <sup>[N194](#n194)</sup>.
- **March 2026:** the DB arrested Masud Uddin Chowdhury at his home in Baridhara DOHS, Dhaka, at midnight on a Monday, Prothom Alo reported on 24 March, and a court granted five days' remand in the Paltan trafficking case of 3 September 2024 <sup>[N177](#n177), [N194](#n194)</sup>. A DB additional commissioner said he faces 11 cases in Feni and Dhaka, including at Paltan, Banani, Kotwali, Mirpur and Hatirjheel police stations, on charges including murder, attempted murder and human trafficking, and that the ACC and the CID are investigating several allegations; at the hearing the public prosecutor said he had made off with Tk 24,000 crore in the name of sending people abroad <sup>[N177](#n177)</sup>. A law-enforcement source said the ACC had applied to show him arrested in its Tk 119 crore case, with an order due on 9 April 2026; a DB joint commissioner said information from him was being verified, and sources said he was being asked how he used the state machinery to start the business after retiring and allegedly build the syndicate <sup>[N196](#n196)</sup>. DB Joint Commissioner Mohammad Nasirul Islam said a ring was built around sending workers to Malaysia under the previous government, that Masud Uddin Chowdhury was a powerful member of it, and that he was being questioned about the ring and the source of his power <sup>[N194](#n194)</sup>.

<a id="licences-withdrawn"></a>

#### 1.3.16 Licences withdrawn

- **19 July 2026:** the Ministry withdrew the licences of 49 agencies, including agencies of former Awami League MPs, that Prothom Alo linked to the syndicate; the notification says charge sheets against them under sections 406, 420, 385, 386, 427 and 34 of the Penal Code 1860 had been filed and accepted in court <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>. Among them are Orbitals Enterprise and Orbital International, Ahmed International, Snigdha Overseas Limited, Five M International, Unique Eastern Private Limited of Noor Ali, and Catharsis International <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>. The full list: [Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4).
- **September to October 2026:** the Attorney General, Md Ruhul Quddus Kajal, said one agency challenged the withdrawal by writ and the High Court issued a rule and stayed the notification on 15 September; the Appellate Division's chamber court stayed that order on 30 September; 11 more agencies then got a stay from a vacation bench in a second writ, which the chamber court also stayed <sup>[N197](#n197)</sup>. Prothom Alo names New Age International among the agencies that filed writs <sup>[N197](#n197)</sup>. On 4 October 2026 the chamber judge, Justice Md Rezaul Haque, stayed a High Court order that had suspended the notification, so the withdrawal of the 49 licences remains in force <sup>[N197](#n197)</sup>.

Each agency's record, including these cases, is in the `agencies` folder.

<a id="the-broker-and-the-village"></a>

#### 1.3.17 The broker and the village

Most workers (78% in a 2023 survey) reach the agency through a sub-agent, sometimes through five or six levels of brokers <sup>[N198 (2023)](#n198), [N10 (2018)](#n10)</sup>. The broker is usually a friend, neighbour, relative or returnee, and by October 2026 brokers were already collecting passports, and the owner of one associate agency said he had already collected over 1,000 <sup>[N199 (2022)](#n199), [N7](#n7)</sup>. Across Bangladesh's visa trade, when a visa comes through relatives, BAIRA's secretary general says, the money changes hands mainly among them and the agency only does the paperwork <sup>[N200 (2024)](#n200)</sup>. Only a sub-agent registered with BMET may now recruit ([Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying") <sup>[27 (2023)](#o27), [28](#o28)</sup>.

Cases:

- **November 2023, Meherpur:** the Gangni men held in Kuala Lumpur (see [Which employer](#which-employer)) said three cousins, Suruj Ali and Awal of Sahebnagar and Abdullah of Mohammadpur, took Tk 4 to 5 lakh from each, promising construction work at Tk 45,000 to 50,000 a month within 40 days of arrival; they alleged that 54 of them were taken on 6 November 2023 to an agency office on Road 4, Banani, Dhaka, kept there some days, flown out on 11 November, and had their passports taken by agency people after landing <sup>[N146 (2024)](#n146)</sup>. Abdullah told Prothom Alo he was a computer operator at the Dhaka travel agency involved, said everyone was expected to have work by 28 February and those who did not would be sent home, and did not answer whether money would be refunded; a Kazipur union member said more than a hundred people from the area went through a Dhaka travel agency and none got work <sup>[N146 (2024)](#n146)</sup>.
- **June 2024, Akhaura:** Tanvir Mia, 22, of Debgram, Brahmanbaria, for whom Tk 6.5 lakh had been paid to a broker and his brother in Malaysia for a supermarket job, had two flights cancelled, and on 31 May 2024 fell from the train taking him home from Dhaka into the Meghna and went missing <sup>[N113 (2024)](#n113)</sup>. His family said they had trusted the broker because he had sent 30 people to Malaysia three days earlier, and his father had sold 130 decimals of land for the trip and his own business <sup>[N113 (2024)](#n113)</sup>. After Tanvir's death his father said the brokers had not contacted the family and had fled their homes <sup>[N201 (2024)](#n201)</sup>.
- **July 2024, Jashore:** in Bankra, on the Kopotakkho river where Monirampur and Jhikargachha upazilas meet, some local men who tried to go to Japan illegally through brokers were taken through Thailand and the jungle into Malaysia, came home after three years and then began sending people to Malaysia themselves; the bazar committee's general secretary said at least 10 of the area's multi-storey buildings are known as "Malaysia buildings" <sup>[N202 (2024)](#n202)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a village broker take per Malaysia worker?
> - Does the worker, the agency or both pay him?

<a id="what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep"></a>

### 1.4 What you pay, and what you earn and keep

No cap had been set for 2026 <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup> until the minister told parliament on 15 November 2026 that the cost had been fixed at Tk 1,54,000 <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. Both governments say the employer pays, yet people are told to keep Tk 6 to 7.5 lakh ready, and an MP told parliament the same day that it costs Tk 7 to 8 lakh <sup>[N203](#n203), [N7](#n7), [N28](#n28), [N4](#n4)</sup>. Much less than the headline wage reaches home: a 2024 permission letter leaves food to the worker, and over 40% of income goes on the mess (shared lodging), transport and food <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29), [N14 (2023)](#n14)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a plantation worker take home after food in 2026?

<a id="the-cap"></a>

#### 1.4.1 The cap

Until mid-November 2026 no cap was set for 2026 private-agency recruitment: on 5 November the agencies proposed at most Tk 2.5 to 3 lakh and the minister said the private-agency cost could fall in that range, and in October the state minister said nobody had followed the old cap <sup>[N6](#n6), [N23](#n23), [N7](#n7)</sup>. On 15 November 2026 the expatriates' welfare minister, Ariful Haque Chowdhury, told parliament that the cost of sending a worker to Malaysia had been cut and fixed at Tk 1,54,000, and that strict action would be taken against anyone who collects more or breaks the government's arrangements <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. In April 2026 both governments said employers will bear the full cost <sup>[N203](#n203)</sup>. Malaysia officially follows an "employer pays" policy for recruitment costs, but under the current system it is still hard to implement, and the money changing hands in the Bangladeshi recruitment market runs to several hundred crore ringgit while the real cost of recruitment is far lower, The Edge reported in June 2026 <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>. The old cap, in the Ministry's July 2022 order, was Tk 78,990 for the worker's costs inside Bangladesh, with the employer paying everything in Malaysia, airfare both ways included ([Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying") <sup>[10 (2022)](#o10), [16 (2021)](#o16)</sup>. Some 2024 permission letters set less, Tk 41,240 or Tk 38,710 ([Step 1, item 6: Find and check the permission letter for your job](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6)) <sup>[30](#o30), [31 (2024)](#o31)</sup>. For the BOESL zero-cost route, see [Status](#status).

> **Need to know**
> - Is the Tk 1,54,000 the minister announced on 15 November 2026 <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup> a cap on what a private agency may take, in which order or notice is it set, and what does it cover?

<a id="what-people-really-pay"></a>

#### 1.4.2 What people really pay

For 2026, brokers collecting passports tell people to keep Tk 7 to 7.5 lakh ready, and a listed agency's owner says no less than Tk 6 lakh <sup>[N7](#n7), [N28](#n28)</sup>; members of BAIRA (Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) said in September 2026 that workers may have to spend Tk 6 to 7 lakh, and the opposition party Jamaat-e-Islami said on 23 August 2026 it feared the cost could reach that (see [Who controls it](#who-controls-it)) <sup>[N76](#n76), [N67](#n67)</sup>. An MP, Atikur Rahman Mujahid, told parliament on 15 November 2026 that sending a worker to Malaysia currently costs Tk 7 to 8 lakh, and asked whether a separate parliamentary committee could check that workers go at the fixed Tk 1,54,000 <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>.

In 2022-24 the average was Tk 5.44 lakh, with press and workers' groups giving Tk 3.5 to 6 lakh <sup>[N11](#n11), [N12](#n12), [N204](#n204)</sup>. BAIRA's joint secretary general, Fakhrul Islam, said in March 2024 that though a worker was supposed to go for Tk 80,000, nobody could go for less than Tk 4.5 lakh <sup>[N38 (2024)](#n38)</sup>. Other figures for that round:

- **May 2024:** the managing director of PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government's bank for migrants), Mojibur Rahman, said people going through recruiting agencies were spending Tk 4 to 5 lakh <sup>[N74 (2024)](#n74)</sup>.
- **31 May 2024:** at least 10 Malaysia-bound passengers at Dhaka airport told Prothom Alo they had paid Tk 5 to 7 lakh each to the firms sending them <sup>[N112 (2024)](#n112)</sup>.
- **June 2024:** Prothom Alo put the average at Tk 5.5 lakh against the Tk 78,990 cap <sup>[N160 (2024)](#n160)</sup>.
- **August 2025:** BCSM said that, by the government's white paper on the economy, press reports and ACC allegations, each worker sent in 2022, 2023 and 2024 was charged five to six times the government-fixed cost, and that a syndicate of ten operated in 2017 and 2018 without anyone ever being tried <sup>[N185](#n185)</sup>.
- **June 2026:** The Edge reported that the problem peaked in 2023, when many workers spent up to USD 6,600 each (about Tk 8 lakh, as Prothom Alo printed it) to reach Malaysia and then found that the jobs promised did not exist <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>.

Workers call the price the চুক্তি (deal) struck with the broker: "went on a Tk 5 lakh deal" <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>.

<a id="where-a-tk-55-lakh-deal-goes"></a>

#### 1.4.3 Where a Tk 5.5 lakh deal goes

Official fees are about Tk 15,000; the rest pays for the visa trade (the demand letter, the syndicate toll and agents) and usually the airfare, the visa and the medical <sup>[N11](#n11), [N8](#n8), [10 (2022)](#o10), [N125](#n125), [N206](#n206)</sup>. The 2022-24 pieces reported per worker <sup>[N11](#n11), [N8](#n8), [N158](#n158), [N9 (2024)](#n9), [32](#o32), [10 (2022)](#o10)</sup>:

- the demand letter: Tk 1.6 to 2 lakh to middlemen in Malaysia;
- the syndicate toll: Tk 1.07 to 1.52 lakh;
- agents in Bangladesh: about Tk 1.42 lakh, of which an unauthorised middleman takes Tk 50,000 to 1 lakh;
- official fees: about Tk 15,000
  - medical Tk 8,000
  - welfare fee Tk 5,500
  - insurance Tk 1,000
  - clearance Tk 250
  - source tax Tk 800 to 1,200

Together these come to about Tk 4.2 to 5.1 lakh <sup>[N11](#n11), [N8](#n8), [N158](#n158), [32](#o32), [10 (2022)](#o10)</sup>. The deal usually also covers the airfare (Tk 45,000 in BOESL's own 2025 costing), the visa and the medical, though the MoU puts all three on the employer <sup>[N125](#n125), [N206](#n206), [16 (2021)](#o16)</sup>. Even official items get padded, such as the medical ([Step 2: Medical check in Bangladesh (Bio-Medical), once your VDR is approved](#step-2-medical-check-in-bangladesh-bio-medical-once-your-vdr-is-approved)) <sup>[N207](#n207)</sup>; Prothom Alo reported in March 2024 that a business in medical tests ran under the same ring of 100 agencies that took Tk 1.5 lakh a worker, and that many who paid Tk 4 to 5 lakh on average found no job on arrival <sup>[N38 (2024)](#n38)</sup>. When each part is paid: see [How it is paid](#how-it-is-paid).

**The syndicate's share.** In the August 2025 money-laundering case filed by the CID (the police Criminal Investigation Department), Five M International is alleged to have taken Tk 1.5 lakh a worker, of which Tk 1,07,510 was laundered (see [The ACC and CID cases](#the-acc-and-cid-cases)) <sup>[N189](#n189)</sup>. How the toll was taken in: see [How the toll was collected](#how-the-toll-was-collected). In total:

- BAIRA members said in March 2024 that the ring alone took Tk 1.5 lakh a worker, more than Tk 6,000 crore on over four lakh workers, and that the corridor made Tk 18,000 crore of business in the previous year and a half <sup>[N38 (2024)](#n38)</sup>.
- People in the sector told Prothom Alo in May 2024 that sending about 4.5 lakh people in a year and a half made Tk 24,000 crore of business, about Tk 20,000 crore of it above the government-fixed fee, including more than Tk 6,500 crore of syndicate fee at Tk 1.5 lakh a head <sup>[N147 (2024)](#n147)</sup>; Prothom Alo repeated the Tk 24,000 crore and the Tk 5,44,000 average against the Tk 78,990 cap in July 2026 <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>.
- Prothom Alo reported in September 2024 the allegation that about Tk 5,000 crore was laundered to Malaysia as syndicate fee at Tk 1 lakh a worker and another Tk 3,750 crore through the visa trade, at least Tk 8,750 crore in all, a large share of the USD 1.12 billion (about Tk 11,000 crore) Bangladesh Bank recorded as remittance from Malaysia in the 2023-24 fiscal year <sup>[N153 (2024)](#n153)</sup>.

For 2026, BAIRA members said that on top of a syndicate fee of RM5,500 (Tk 1,68,000), buying each demand letter, that is each visa, will cost RM8,000 to 9,000 (Tk 2,65,000 to 2,75,000), so more than Tk 4,30,000 a worker will go to Malaysia <sup>[N76](#n76), [N8](#n8)</sup>.

<a id="how-it-is-paid"></a>

#### 1.4.4 How it is paid

Stages: the deal is paid in stages, for visa processing, the medical and other costs, much of it to a broker <sup>[N208](#n208), [N206](#n206)</sup>. A delay raises the price: a Cumilla man's Tk 3.6 lakh became Tk 5.7 lakh <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, how much of the চুক্তি is paid with the passport, how much when the calling visa comes, and how much before the flight or the BMET card?

Receipts: few are given. Down payments for the passport and medical carry none, and workers get receipts for little of the rest <sup>[N209 (2024)](#n209), [N208](#n208)</sup>. Before flying, some workers are made to sign, on video, that they paid only the official fee; in 2025 police cited those signatures to close a trafficking case (see [Done to workers](#done-to-workers)) <sup>[N210](#n210), [N133](#n133)</sup>.

<a id="how-they-borrow"></a>

#### 1.4.5 How they borrow

Nearly all borrow (96% in a 2024 survey of Malaysia workers), most (82%) with two or more loans <sup>[N13 (2024)](#n13)</sup>. A typical package is a land mortgage (জমি বন্ধক), cattle sold and loans from relatives: a Natore farmer mortgaged his 16 decimals for Tk 1 lakh and sold four cows for Tk 4.5 lakh to pay a Paltan agency Tk 5.5 lakh, then could not fly <sup>[N209 (2024)](#n209)</sup>. Informal loans are usually paid straight to the broker, so nothing records them <sup>[N13 (2024)](#n13), [N208](#n208)</sup>. Workers in Malaysia borrow at 10% a month to renew their permits; a microcredit NGO in Bangladesh may charge at most 24% a year, the cap set by the MRA (Microcredit Regulatory Authority) <sup>[N205](#n205), [N211 (2024)](#n211)</sup>. The government's PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank) loan comes only after the visa, and many can't find its guarantors ([Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying") <sup>[33](#o33), [N212 (2023)](#n212)</sup>.

Cases:

- **November 2023, Meherpur:** the wife of one of the Gangni men held in Kuala Lumpur (see [Which employer](#which-employer)) said he went after selling one decimal of their homestead land and borrowing Tk 3 lakh at high interest <sup>[N146 (2024)](#n146)</sup>.
- **About 2023, Abdus Salam:** his mother, Taslima Begum, said in October 2024 that she borrowed Tk 3.5 lakh at interest from various people to send him to Malaysia about a year and a half earlier through her brother Abu Taher; he could not work for his first six months and sent some money within a year, and she had repaid Tk 1.5 lakh including interest and still owed lenders Tk 2 lakh <sup>[N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What monthly rate does a village moneylender (মহাজন) charge on a Malaysia loan in 2026?
> - What are the usual terms of a land mortgage taken for one: the amount per decimal, who works the land, and when it is returned?

<a id="what-the-job-pays-a-correct-payslip"></a>

#### 1.4.6 What the job pays: a correct payslip

A correct payslip shows basic pay of at least RM1,700 a month (about Tk 52,000), plus overtime at least 1.5 times the hourly rate, 2 times on a rest day and 3 times on a public holiday, up to 104 hours a month ([Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[11 (2024)](#o11), [34 (2023)](#o34), [N213](#n213)</sup>. The deductions allowed <sup>[35](#o35), [36](#o36), [N214 (2020)](#n214), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>:

- 2% for EPF (Employees Provident Fund, Malaysia's retirement fund), about RM34 on the basic, compulsory since October 2025 wages and matched by the employer's 2%;
- 0.5% for SOCSO (Malaysia's social security);
- rent of at most RM100 a month;
- no more than half the wage in total.

Both EPF shares are paid back in full when you leave for good ([Leaving at the end of the job](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job)) <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>. The levy, the pass renewal and the FOMEMA medical are the employer's to pay <sup>[9](#o9), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. At a Pahang plywood factory in 2026, workers on 8-hour contracts got RM65 a day for 12-hour days: the minimum day rate for normal hours alone, so four hours a day went unpaid <sup>[N215](#n215), [11 (2024)](#o11)</sup>. What workers are made to pay instead: see [What goes unpaid](#what-goes-unpaid).

<a id="what-goes-unpaid"></a>

#### 1.4.7 What goes unpaid

43% got less than promised in the 2024 survey <sup>[N13 (2024)](#n13)</sup>. The usual losses:

- Overtime unpaid or paid at a flat rate (the Pahang case, see [What the job pays: a correct payslip](#what-the-job-pays-a-correct-payslip)) <sup>[N215](#n215)</sup>.
- Wages held for months, often before the company closes: up to eight months at Kawaguchi, nearly three at Aerofoam in 2026 <sup>[N216](#n216), [N217](#n217)</sup>.
- The employer's costs taken from pay: the levy, the yearly renewal, hostel charges, uniforms (RM500 in one case) and "flight-ticket deposits" <sup>[N218](#n218), [N205](#n205), [N206](#n206), [N217](#n217)</sup>.
- Time with no job, earning nothing while the loan runs <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>.

Workers who ask for the promised pay have been fired or told to do more overtime <sup>[N14 (2023)](#n14)</sup>. How to claim: If you aren't paid.

<a id="money-sent-home"></a>

#### 1.4.8 Money sent home

Most send through banks, and legal transfers earn the government's 2.5% incentive (how: see [Sending money home](#sending-money-home)); over 16% use hundi (an unlicensed money agent), which is an offence in Bangladesh <sup>[N219 (2023)](#n219), [N220](#n220), [N221 (2022)](#n221)</sup>. Hundi survives because it converts at the kerb rate, which in 2022 was well above the bank rate, because the hundi man comes to the mess, and because an employer holding your passport shuts you out of banks <sup>[N222 (2022)](#n222), [N12](#n12), [N219 (2023)](#n219)</sup>. The risk: families could not cash money received in agent and MFS (mobile money) accounts frozen for hundi, and Malaysian Immigration arrests hundi operators working behind restaurants and shops <sup>[38](#o38), [N221 (2022)](#n221), [N223 (2023)](#n223)</sup>.

Remittances from Malaysia were USD 102 crore in 2021-22, 112 crore in 2022-23, 160 crore in 2023-24 and 280 crore in 2024-25, then USD 26 crore in July 2025 and 27 crore each in August and September, Prothom Alo reported <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>. By Bangladesh Bank data they grew 35% in 2023-24, which people in the sector put down to more workers going; by BMET data, 2023, with more than three and a half lakh workers, was the highest year for departures to Malaysia between 2004 and 2023 <sup>[N224 (2024)](#n224)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Was the 2023-24 remittance from Malaysia USD 1.12 billion <sup>[N153 (2024)](#n153)</sup> or USD 160 crore (1.6 billion) <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>?

<a id="how-long-the-debt-takes"></a>

#### 1.4.9 How long the debt takes

At the 2022-24 price of about Tk 5.44 lakh the cost equals at least a year's wages; counting living costs, repayment takes from 21-22 months to three years <sup>[N225 (2024)](#n225), [N14 (2023)](#n14), [N9 (2024)](#n9)</sup>. The 2026 asking price of Tk 6 to 7.5 lakh is higher (see [What people really pay](#what-people-really-pay)) <sup>[N7](#n7), [N28](#n28)</sup>. Meanwhile 73% spend half or more of their monthly pay on repayments <sup>[N13 (2024)](#n13)</sup>. Every month without work or pay, and every renewal the worker is made to pay, adds to it <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>. Two cases:

- Farhad Hossain, killed on a Seremban construction site in July 2025 two and a half years after going to Malaysia on a Tk 5 lakh loan: his father said the loan had still not been fully repaid <sup>[N226](#n226)</sup>.
- Mominul Islam of Chuadanga, a shop worker at Times Square, Kuala Lumpur, said in December 2025 that he borrowed Tk 5 lakh to go, earns RM2,200 (Tk 66,000) a month, and has already repaid the loan <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.

<a id="what-ties-you-to-the-employer"></a>

### 1.5 What ties you to the employer

Your work pass ties you to one employer for everything. The PLKS (Pas Lawatan Kerja Sementara, the work pass; the "permit", পারমিট) names one employer; only that employer can bring you in, renew the pass and send you home, and work for anyone else breaks it <sup>[5](#o5), [9](#o9), [39 (2006)](#o39), [N205](#n205)</sup>. The standard contract is 3 years, renewable, with 90 days' probation, and the air ticket home is due only after the contract is completed <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29), [8](#o8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do outsourcing or shell companies still sponsor Bangladeshis on PLKS to work anywhere in 2026?
> - What does such a PLKS "visa" sell for?

- **Enter:** only your employer can bring you in. The employer holds the quota and applies for your VDR (Visa Dengan Rujukan, the calling visa approval); you wait in Bangladesh until it is approved, and can check its status yourself on Immigration's MyIMMs site ([Step 3, item 2: Track the approval](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-2)) <sup>[9](#o9), [5](#o5)</sup>. The employer must collect you at the airport within 6 hours of landing ([After you arrive, item 1](#after-you-arrive-item-1)) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
- **Stay:** the FOMEMA medical in Malaysia decides whether you can stay. The employer has 30 days after arrival to take you to a FOMEMA clinic, and the PLKS is issued only if you pass; if you fail, you are sent home on a Check-Out Memo with the debt unpaid <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Since December 2023 the check is yearly <sup>[N227](#n227)</sup>. FOMEMA has rejected workers for diabetes, high blood pressure and high cholesterol, and neither worker nor employer gets the report ([After you arrive, item 2](#after-you-arrive-item-2)) <sup>[N227](#n227), [N228 (2024)](#n228)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Does any agency refund part of the package when a worker fails FOMEMA, and is this ever written into the চুক্তি?

- **Renew:** only your employer can renew the pass, once a year, up to 10 years (see [How long you can stay and renewing](#how-long-you-can-stay-and-renewing)) <sup>[9](#o9), [5](#o5)</sup>. Workers call it ভিসা রিনিউ (visa renewal) <sup>[N149 (2024)](#n149)</sup>. The official cost in manufacturing, construction or services is about RM2,055 (Tk 63,000): the RM1,850 levy, RM60 for the pass, RM125 processing and RM20 visa, all payable by the employer <sup>[24](#o24), [9](#o9)</sup>. Workers report paying RM2,500 to 3,200 a year (Tk 77,000 to 98,000) <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>.
- **Quit:** to quit, you give 2 months' notice or pay 2 months' wages, and pay your own fare home; Immigration normally cancels the pass at once <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [34 (2023)](#o34), [N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Once Immigration cancels a quitting worker's pass, how many days does he have to leave?

- **Change employer:** you can't change employer on your own; only the employer can apply, and only if it closes, goes bankrupt, is restructured or dies ([If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer)) <sup>[5](#o5), [41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>. A Kuala Lumpur migrant lawyer calls a legal job change almost impossible and a main reason so many end up undocumented <sup>[N230 (2019)](#n230)</sup>.
- **Leave:** only your employer can send you home. The employer applies for the Check-Out Memo and since 2025 must be present at the exit point ([Leaving at the end of the job](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job)) <sup>[9](#o9), [N231](#n231)</sup>. Its RM500 security bond for you is refunded only if you leave on that memo and forfeited if you run <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. That bond is why employers hold passports and demand deposits: at a Pahang plywood factory in 2026, workers had to pay RM2,500 before going home on annual leave <sup>[N215](#n215)</sup>. Missing the agreed return date counts as running away <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Can a worker get the Check-Out Memo without the employer?

<a id="which-law-covers-you"></a>

#### 1.5.1 Which law covers you

The pass falls under the Immigration Act, so any breach of its conditions voids it at once <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. The work falls under the Employment Act 1955 in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan, and separate Labour Ordinances in Sabah and Sarawak <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. In Sarawak a foreign worker is bound by a Labour Department licence to the job, workplace and licence holder named on it <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>.

<a id="your-passport-in-practice"></a>

#### 1.5.2 Your passport in practice

The law forbids the employer to keep it ([After you arrive, item 4](#after-you-arrive-item-4)) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [44 (1997)](#o44)</sup>, but employers routinely take passports on arrival "for safekeeping" without a receipt <sup>[45](#o45), [N218](#n218)</sup>. In an IOM (International Organization for Migration) study of 40 workers who became undocumented, every one had their passport held by the employer or agency <sup>[N232 (2023)](#n232)</sup>. Held passports are ransomed: the Kuala Lumpur company that gave about 600 Bangladeshis no work asked Tk 1.7 to 1.8 lakh each for theirs in 2024 <sup>[N144 (2024)](#n144)</sup>. Some have got passports back by complaining to the authorities <sup>[N217](#n217)</sup>.

<a id="what-running-away-or-an-expired-pass-does-to-you"></a>

#### 1.5.3 What running away or an expired pass does to you

- **Running away (lari, Malay for "run").** The employer reports you, you are blacklisted, the bond is forfeited and the pass cancelled; some employers file false reports near the contract's end to avoid the fare home, or after a complaint (If your employer reports you absent (lari)) <sup>[24](#o24), [N229 (2021)](#n229), [45](#o45)</sup>. You become what Malaysia calls PATI (Pendatang Asing Tanpa Izin, foreigner without permission) and Bangladeshis call অবৈধ (illegal) <sup>[N233](#n233), [N144 (2024)](#n144)</sup>.
- **An expired pass** makes you an overstayer: fine, jail or a compound, then removal and a blacklist (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39), [46](#o46), [N234 (2022)](#n234)</sup>. Most undocumented workers got there this way: all 40 in the IOM study arrived legally and left their jobs because of exploitation, and recruitment debt kept them in Malaysia afterwards <sup>[N232 (2023)](#n232)</sup>. Selim Reza of North South University wrote in June 2026 that most undocumented Bangladeshis in Malaysia are not involved in crime but lost legal status through employer fraud, company closure, visa-renewal complications or administrative weakness <sup>[N101](#n101)</sup>.
- **Buying your way back to legal.** No legalisation programme is open; the last, RTK (the Labour Recalibration Programme), ended on 31 December 2023 <sup>[N235](#n235)</sup>. Past rounds were a market: agents charged RM6,000 to 10,000 against an official maximum of RM3,900, and rings sold fake renewals and PLKS stickers at RM2,500 to 6,000 <sup>[N236 (2018)](#n236), [N237 (2023)](#n237), [N238 (2023)](#n238)</sup>. The only official way out is home under PRM 2.0, with a compound of RM300 for breaking pass conditions or RM500 for overstaying ([Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. Some can't use it (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers), [Step 3: Go to Immigration](#step-3-go-to-immigration), Who can't use PRM 2.0) <sup>[N239](#n239), [N240](#n240)</sup>. What the governments have said since:
  - **15 May 2025:** asked to legalise undocumented Bangladeshis, Malaysia replied that it does so from time to time, last in the previous year, but cannot for those whose visas have expired; Bangladesh asked it to consider them too, since workers often cannot renew because of the employer, and Malaysia said it would consider it <sup>[N40](#n40)</sup>.
  - **2 August 2025:** the adviser Asif Nazrul said some Bangladeshis in Malaysia had messaged him complaining that undocumented workers there could not become legal while more people were being brought from Bangladesh <sup>[N241](#n241)</sup>.
  - **December 2025:** Tasneem Siddiqui, founding chair of RMMRU, said that instead of showcase manpower export the government should push to regularise undocumented Bangladeshis, as Malaysia has done before, and that if the market is again handed to a syndicate, workers will not in practice be able to join jobs <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.
  - **22 June 2026:** at the two Prime Ministers' talks, according to the Prime Minister's spokesperson Mahdi Amin, the two sides discussed humanely giving legal status to Bangladeshis in irregular status or in prison in Malaysia, or returning them safely home; no programme was announced <sup>[N58](#n58)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does a worker reported as lari (not deported) stay blacklisted?
> - Can a worker blacklisted on a lari report go home under PRM 2.0, and if not, what is his way out?
> - Did Malaysia run any legalisation in 2024, as it told Bangladesh in May 2025 <sup>[N40](#n40)</sup>, or was RTK, which ended on 31 December 2023, the last <sup>[N235](#n235)</sup>?

<a id="what-goes-wrong-most-and-what-people-wrongly-believe"></a>

### 1.6 What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe

Money owed is the commonest complaint: unpaid, late or cut wages, then jobs that don't exist or don't match the promise <sup>[45](#o45), [N242](#n242), [N243](#n243)</sup>. Before departure the loss is money taken and no departure, and most complainants get back less than half of it (see [If an agency or broker takes your money](#if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money)) <sup>[48 (2024)](#o48), [N244 (2024)](#n244)</sup>. Employers sent 20,331 Bangladeshi workers home in 2022 and 23,065 in 2023, by Malaysia's Home Ministry's count <sup>[N81](#n81)</sup>. On 22 June 2026 Malaysia's Prime Minister, Anwar Ibrahim, referring to allegations of exploitation and abuse of Bangladeshi workers in Malaysia, said exploiting workers, treating them badly and using them only for self-interest could not be accepted, and that both countries must lead in making recruitment transparent <sup>[N60](#n60)</sup>.

Most workers go on what they were told rather than on a contract, so a false belief goes unchecked until it costs them: OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a Bangladeshi migrants' organisation) finds 75% leave without having seen a contract <sup>[N245](#n245)</sup>.

<a id="what-people-believe-and-the-facts"></a>

#### 1.6.1 What people believe, and the facts

- **"The talks mean the market is open."** Selim Reza of North South University's Centre for Migration Studies wrote on 30 June 2026 that before the Prime Minister's June visit much of the media and some government officials gave the impression that reopening was decided and only a formal announcement remained, but that the visit's main result was agreement in principle on transparent, fair, lower-cost recruitment, not an immediate opening <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>. Where it stands now: see [Status](#status).
- **"I've paid the full price, so a job is waiting."** Not guaranteed: quotas are issued beyond real need, so many who paid found no job (see [The quota](#the-quota); see [Which employer](#which-employer)) <sup>[N135 (2023)](#n135), [N136 (2023)](#n136)</sup>; in the case of the 161 promised construction jobs, the employer also asked RM6,000 to return their passports <sup>[N143 (2024)](#n143)</sup>. Activists and researchers estimate 100,000 to 200,000 of about 800,000 Bangladeshis in Malaysia are jobless, unpaid and in debt <sup>[N246 (2024)](#n246)</sup>. Before paying the bulk, the employer can be checked on SSM (Malaysia's company registry) and the calling visa on MyIMMs ([Step 1, item 7: Check the employer exists](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-7); [Step 3, item 2: Track the approval](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-2)) <sup>[49](#o49), [50](#o50), [5](#o5)</sup>.
- **"Passing the medical in Dhaka means I'm in."** FOMEMA in Malaysia decides, every year, and failing it means going home with the debt (see [What ties you to the employer](#what-ties-you-to-the-employer)) <sup>[5](#o5), [N227](#n227)</sup>.
- **"Go on a tourist visa; the work permit gets done there."** Travel agents sell exactly this: a Paltan travel agency owner promised work permits, sent two people on visit visas, held them and demanded Tk 5 lakh from their families <sup>[N247](#n247)</sup>. A visit pass allows no work and can't become a work pass inside Malaysia; the worker must be outside Malaysia while the employer applies ([Changing visa type](#changing-visa-type)) <sup>[51](#o51), [9](#o9)</sup>. In one July 2025 operation 123 Bangladeshis holding tourist visas were refused entry at KLIA (Kuala Lumpur International Airport) <sup>[N248](#n248)</sup>.
- **"Malaysia will legalise us again."** When a rumour of an "RTK 3.0" spread in January 2025, Immigration's Director General said no decision had been taken, and rings sold fake places in past rounds <sup>[N249](#n249), [N250 (2024)](#n250)</sup>. What the governments have said since: see [What running away or an expired pass does to you](#what-running-away-or-an-expired-pass-does-to-you).
- **"A student visa lets you work."** Prospective students are routinely told this <sup>[N251](#n251)</sup>. Students may work only in breaks of over 7 days, up to 20 hours a week, in a few listed jobs, and those breaking the rules are deported (see [Student visa (Student Pass), Can you work?](#can-you-work)) <sup>[52](#o52), [53](#o53), [N251](#n251)</sup>. In August 2025 a 2-minute-19-second TikTok video by Haim Hilman Abdullah, chairman of Kedah state's committee on industry, investment, higher education, science, technology and innovation, claimed that 10,000 Bangladeshi students in Malaysia would get jobs <sup>[N252](#n252)</sup>. On 23 August 2025 Malaysia's Higher Education Minister, Zambry Abd Kadir, said reports that a "Graduate Pass" would be given to 10,000 Bangladeshi students to let them work were untrue and baseless, that no such agreement was made during Chief Adviser Muhammad Yunus's visit, and that Malaysia had given no consent; he called Haim Hilman's claim irresponsible <sup>[N252](#n252)</sup>.
- **"The approved agency list is on Facebook."** Posts in late 2025 that Malaysia had approved 25 Bangladeshi agencies were false; Malaysia had told neither the High Commission nor the Ministry (see [Done to workers](#done-to-workers)) <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>.

<a id="irregular-routes-and-where-an-irregular-stay-ends"></a>

#### 1.6.2 Irregular routes, and where an irregular stay ends

- **Overland through Thailand: it ends in arrest, in Thailand or in Malaysia.** Brokers fly people to Cambodia; they cross into Thailand on foot, are moved in stages to the south, and are brought into Malaysia across the Kelantan border <sup>[N253](#n253), [N254](#n254)</sup>. The price is Tk 7 lakh (April 2026) or up to RM15,000 (July 2026), more than the legal route <sup>[N253](#n253), [N254](#n254), [N11](#n11)</sup>. Caught on the way, they are charged in Thailand: in April 2026, 21 Bangladeshis found hidden in a resort in southern Thailand were charged there with illegal entry <sup>[N253](#n253)</sup>. Caught after crossing, they go to an immigration depot: in July 2026, 15 caught in a Cheras transit house on the day they crossed were taken to the Putrajaya depot <sup>[N254](#n254)</sup>.
- **By sea from Teknaf and Cox's Bazar.** A Prothom Alo editorial of 7 September 2025 said Cox's Bazar has become a regular sea route for human trafficking, used from 21 points in Teknaf, Ukhia, Moheshkhali, Cox's Bazar Sadar and other upazilas, and that besides Rohingya from the camps, traffickers lure a section of young Bangladeshis onto it <sup>[N255](#n255)</sup>. The gangs become active after the monsoon and before winter, when the sea is calm, and such incidents rise in winter <sup>[N255](#n255), [N256](#n256)</sup>. The editorial said deaths in the Thai jungle on the way to Malaysia, torture by traffickers and deaths when food and water ran out on trawlers have been reported, and that more than 657 Rohingya died on sea voyages in 2024 <sup>[N255](#n255)</sup>. Rohingya pay syndicates Tk 3,52,000 to 3,85,000 (USD 3,200 to 3,500) each, AFP reported in November 2025, citing Malaysian media, and the boats are often weak with no life jackets, or many passengers don't wear them <sup>[N257](#n257)</sup>. UNHCR (the UN refugee agency) and IOM said on 16 July 2026 that about 300 Rohingya and Bangladeshis had been reported dead or missing in the Andaman Sea and Bay of Bengal so far in 2026, on a route to Malaysia, Indonesia and Thailand that has become the world's deadliest sea route for refugees and migrants, after about 900 Rohingya dead or missing in 2025 <sup>[N258](#n258)</sup>. Cases:
  - **June 2023 to April 2025:** teenagers and young men from Teknaf and Ukhiya, some aged 16, lured by brokers with promises of well-paid jobs in Malaysia, set off by sea without telling their families and were detained in Myanmar for illegal entry, serving 22 months in prison <sup>[N259](#n259)</sup>. Families learned of the arrest only about a month after it; 20 were brought back on the navy ship BNS Samudra Avijan from Yangon, arriving at Chattogram on 15 April 2025, a year after the families submitted papers to the government <sup>[N259](#n259)</sup>.
  - **10 May 2025:** the BGB (Border Guard Bangladesh) rescued 14 teenagers and young men from a house in Uttar Lambori, Teknaf, where, it said, they had been locked up to be trafficked to Malaysia by sea and held hostage to extort money from their families; no one was arrested <sup>[N260](#n260)</sup>. Two of them, Mohammad Kasem and Mohin Uddin of Cox's Bazar town, said a CNG auto-rickshaw driver tricked them into the house while they were visiting Inani; they were beaten, Tk 1 lakh each was extorted from their families, and they were held for 18 days while the captors tried to sell them to brokers for the sea route <sup>[N260](#n260)</sup>.
