  - **September 2025:** two young men from Munshiganj Sadar, Amit Hasan and Manik Mia, said a Cox's Bazar resident, Md Nur, invited them to visit, took them to his house in Marichya, Ukhia, on 2 September, then put them in an auto-rickshaw for a "trip to Teknaf" and handed them to traffickers, who held them in a hill den to be taken to Thailand by sea and beat them if they tried to phone home; they were rescued 20 days later <sup>[N261](#n261)</sup>. On 15 September the BGB's Ramu sector commander, Colonel Md Mohiuddin Ahmed, said 47 traffickers had been arrested during trafficking to Malaysia by sea and 21 more who fled were being sought <sup>[N262](#n262)</sup>. Operations in Teknaf that month: the BGB detained 12 traffickers and rescued 11 people on 16 September; the Coast Guard rescued 66 on 18 September; RAB (Rapid Action Battalion) and the BGB arrested three armed traffickers and rescued 84 on 22 September; five kidnapped people were rescued from the Kachchhapia hills on 23 September <sup>[N263](#n263)</sup>.
    - The 66 of 18 September, 44 of them women and children, were held in a den deep in the hills of Kachchhapia, Baharchhara union; the Coast Guard said most were Rohingya, some lured with promises of a better life in Malaysia and others kidnapped and held for ransom <sup>[N264](#n264)</sup>.
    - The 84 were rescued in a roughly 12-hour operation dated 21 September in the first report, 66 of them from the Rohingya camps; officials said two had been taken there by force and the rest brought by lures, many with promises of jobs in Malaysia and Thailand and some women with promises of marriage, and the Teknaf-2 BGB commander said the traffickers fired at the forces and tried to use some victims as shields <sup>[N261](#n261)</sup>. One young woman rescued said she had been married by mobile phone on 15 September to a Rohingya man in Malaysia, who told her to meet some people to go by sea; no money was asked of her, and her "husband" was to pay something once she reached Malaysia <sup>[N261](#n261)</sup>. By 22 September, 65 traffickers had been arrested and 161 people rescued in Teknaf that year, law-enforcement sources said <sup>[N261](#n261)</sup>.
    - On 25 September the BGB rescued three men from Khuniapalong Marichya, Ramu, Robiul Alam (18), Md Tazim (18) and Md Ismail (30), from a hill in Baraitali, Teknaf; it said they had been lured with the promise of being taken to Malaysia by sea via Thailand, that ransom was being sought from their families, and that they were threatened with being sold to a trafficking group in Malaysia if it was not paid <sup>[N265](#n265)</sup>.
  - **October 2025:** the Coast Guard rescued eight women and children near Jummapara hill in Baharchhara on 1 October, and at midnight that day a Coast Guard and Navy team rescued 21 people, mostly Rohingya, held for trafficking to Malaysia by sea near Karachipara ghat <sup>[N263](#n263)</sup>. On 3 October the Coast Guard and Navy rescued 38 more (18 women, 12 men, 8 children, mostly Rohingya) from the hills next to Kachchhapia government primary school; by then law-enforcement agencies had arrested 70 gang members and rescued 239 people in Teknaf in 2025 <sup>[N256](#n256)</sup>.
  - **November 2025:** over 100 migrants went missing when a boat sank near the Thailand-Malaysia sea border; by 9 November Malaysia's maritime enforcement agency (MMEA) had rescued 12 alive, one of them Bangladeshi, and recovered one body near Langkawi, and Kedah police said the boat had left Buthidaung in Myanmar with about 300 aboard, who split into three boats of about 100 near the coast, as the organisers had taught them, to evade the Malaysian authorities <sup>[N257](#n257), [N258](#n258)</sup>.
  - **7 December 2025:** the Coast Guard rescued seven men and women from Jaliapalong, Ukhia, from a den in the Baharchhara hills and detained three men with a homemade firearm; an officer said they had been brought to be trafficked to Malaysia by sea with promises of jobs for the men and marriage for the women, and were held while the traffickers tried to extract money <sup>[N266](#n266)</sup>.
  - **3 January 2026:** the Navy ship BNS Swadhinata chased and stopped a wooden boat about 30 miles south-west of St Martin's Island carrying people illegally to Malaysia, rescued 263 including women and children, and detained 10 gang members; the boat had no basic life-saving equipment and not enough food or water <sup>[N267](#n267)</sup>.
  - **16 January 2026:** Sumaiya Akter, 18, was shot dead in Noakhaliapara, Baharchhara, when armed men from the hills attacked a den where a gang had gathered people to send to Malaysia by sea, a local union member said; the district police spokesman said coastal Baharchhara has become the main base of trafficking gangs <sup>[N268](#n268)</sup>.
  - **8 April 2026:** a Malaysia-bound trawler that left the Teknaf coast with at least 250 Bangladeshi and Rohingya men and women sank in the Andaman Sea in strong wind, rough sea and overloading; nine were rescued, and the Coast Guard said no trace of the missing or the wreck had been found <sup>[N269](#n269)</sup>.
  - **Late June 2026:** two boats carrying more than 500 people, mostly Rohingya including some from camps in Bangladesh, left Rakhine and are feared lost, UNHCR and IOM said <sup>[N258](#n258)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Was the RAB and BGB operation that rescued 84 people in Teknaf on 21 September 2025 <sup>[N261](#n261)</sup> or 22 September <sup>[N263](#n263)</sup>?

- **Out of Malaysia by speedboat to Indonesia: it can end in arrest by Indonesia's navy.** At the end of August 2026 Indonesian navy units detained 16 migrant workers, eight of them Bangladeshi, who had crossed from Malaysia by twin-engine speedboat and were landing at Pantai Purnama in Dumai, Riau province <sup>[N270](#n270)</sup>.

- **Through the airport, on a tourist visa: if spotted, it ends in refusal at the airport and a flight back.** Travellers refused entry are sent back within 24 hours <sup>[N271](#n271)</sup>. A "counter setting" syndicate sold entry packages on Facebook and Telegram: agents booked dates with immigration officers by WhatsApp, and the group, arriving as tourists, was sent to a "special counter", with officers taking RM200 to RM2,500 per worker; the MACC (Malaysian Anti-Corruption Commission) arrested 60 people over it in 2024, 49 of them immigration officers <sup>[N272 (2024)](#n272)</sup>. On 17 March 2025 Malaysia's Border Control and Protection Agency (AKPS) detained 15 Bangladeshis at KLIA who wore cricket uniforms and showed an organiser's letter for a 21 to 23 March tournament; AKPS found the letter fake, no such event, and a named "sponsor" who knew nothing of it, and said it believed they were part of a syndicate entering for another purpose <sup>[N273](#n273)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - What does a tourist-visa entry arranged for work in Malaysia sell for in Dhaka in 2026, and what is the street word for it?

- **Overstaying, or staying on after running away: it ends in arrest, the depot and removal.** Undocumented work is there to be had: Prothom Alo reported in December 2025 that because Malaysia sets a minimum wage, many businesses hire undocumented workers to save costs, Bangladeshis the largest group among them, mostly in construction, services and manufacturing <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>. Detention is in a crowded immigration depot, where 465 detainees died in 2021 to 2025, and removal comes with no appeal (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39), [N274](#n274), [N275](#n275)</sup>. Deportees pay their own tickets or their families or embassy do, and they are blacklisted <sup>[N276](#n276)</sup>. Forged papers bought to stay carry jail and caning (see [Tried by workers](#tried-by-workers)) <sup>[N277](#n277), [55](#o55)</sup>. Crackdowns follow crises: a 21 May 2020 OHCHR statement, cited in a Prothom Alo column of 3 April 2026 by Mohammad Jalal Uddin Sikder of North South University, said that during COVID-19 migrant workers in Malaysia were blamed for spreading the virus, detained, and many sent back <sup>[N278](#n278)</sup>.

<a id="visas-not-open-to-bangladeshis"></a>

## 2 Visas not open to Bangladeshis

- **Visa-free visit:** Bangladeshis need a visa <sup>[3](#o3), [6](#o6)</sup>
- **ASEAN (Association of Southeast Asian Nations) visa-free visit of under a month:** ASEAN nationals only (except Myanmar) <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>
- **14-day visa-free visit:** Myanmar only <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- **Visa exemption for Indian nationals:** India only <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>
- **Visa exemption for Chinese nationals:** China only <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- **eNTRI (electronic travel registration):** Indian nationals only <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>
- **Visa on arrival:** Chinese and Indian nationals only <sup>[57](#o57)</sup>
- **Transit without visa (120 hours):** Suspended <sup>[58](#o58), [57](#o57)</sup>
- **Multiple-entry visa for an ordinary tourist or family visit:** Limited to eligible nationalities and to fly-and-cruise, business, medical and wedding trips <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- **Autogates (automatic passport gates) for visitors:** 10 countries only, not Bangladesh <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>
- **Foreign domestic helper (housemaid) pass:** Bangladesh is not an approved source country <sup>[9](#o9), [19](#o19)</sup>
- **Graduate Pass (one year's stay after a Malaysian degree):** Bangladesh is not among the 32 eligible nationalities <sup>[53](#o53), [60](#o60)</sup>
- **Visa exemption for diplomatic and official passports:** Not for ordinary passports <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>
- **Malaysia-Thailand Border Pass:** Malaysian citizens of the border states only <sup>[62](#o62)</sup>
- **APEC (Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation) Business Travel Card:** Malaysian citizens only <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>
- **Work & Holiday Visa:** Malaysian and Australian nationals aged 18 to 30 only <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>
- **Section 66 endorsement (to live or work in Sabah):** Malaysian civil servants and their dependants only <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>

<a id="work-visa-company-worker-plks"></a>

## 3 Work visa: company worker (PLKS)

Closed to new workers from 31 May 2024 and reopening since October 2026; where the restart stands is in Background ([Is it open now, and who can go?](#is-it-open-now-and-who-can-go)) <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [7](#o7), [N3](#n3)</sup>. There is no legal route for women in general jobs or for jobs in Sabah (see [The channels](#the-channels)) <sup>[N20](#n20), [N77](#n77)</sup>.

The PLKS is valid 12 months at a time and renewed by the employer <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Bangladeshis may work only in manufacturing, construction, agriculture, plantation and services, except sectors where intake is frozen <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Jobs in Sabah and Sarawak fall under those state governments <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. You must be 18 to 45 when the employer applies <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

How the job order passes from the employer through the agencies to you is in Background (see [How a job reaches you](#how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut), and who takes a cut); the order of events is under [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "How long".

> **Need to know**
> - What is the most a private agency may take from a worker since the October 2026 reopening <sup>[N279](#n279), [N280](#n280)</sup>?
> - Does the employer still pay the worker's airfare?
> - What terms will the new MoU due on 31 December set?

<a id="who-pays-what"></a>

### 3.1 Who pays what

**On paper the employer pays almost everything; in practice workers have paid lakhs.**

**The employer** pays the levy, the visa, the pass and a deposit <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. Under the Bangladesh-Malaysia MoU (valid to 31 December 2026) the employer also pays all Malaysian-side costs, including your airfare both ways, and BMET clears you only once that is ensured ([Step 6, item 4: The ticket](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-4)) <sup>[30](#o30), [10 (2022)](#o10), [14](#o14), [29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>. What the new MoU will set from 1 January 2027 is not known, and whether zero-cost BOESL workers pay their own airfare is disputed (see [Status](#status)) <sup>[14](#o14), [N28](#n28)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Was a new MoU signed on 31 December 2026, and does it keep the rule that the employer pays all Malaysian-side costs?

**The worker** pays the Bangladesh-side fees, each in the step that owns it <sup>[10 (2022)](#o10)</sup>:

- OEP registration: Tk 200 ([Step 1, item 1: Register on the Overseas Employment Platform (OEP)](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1)) <sup>[66](#o66), [2](#o2)</sup>;
- the medical: Tk 8,000, no more ([Step 2: Medical check in Bangladesh (Bio-Medical), once your VDR is approved](#step-2-medical-check-in-bangladesh-bio-medical-once-your-vdr-is-approved)) <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>;
- the passport: Tk 4,025 to Tk 10,350 by validity and speed ([Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates)) <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>;
- police clearance: Tk 1,500 ([Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates)) <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>;
- pre-departure orientation: Tk 200 at Feni Technical Training Centre ([Step 6, item 1: Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-1)) <sup>[1](#o1), [69](#o69)</sup>;
- at BMET clearance ([Step 6, item 3: BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-3)): Tk 5,500 welfare fee, Tk 1,000 insurance, Tk 250 clearance fee, and source tax of Tk 800 (unskilled) or Tk 1,200 (skilled) <sup>[70](#o70), [21](#o21), [1](#o1)</sup>.

**The cap and what workers really pay.** The 2022 cap on everything the worker pays in Bangladesh, agency charge included, was Tk 78,990; 2022-24 workers reportedly paid an average Tk 5.44 lakh <sup>[10 (2022)](#o10), [N11](#n11)</sup>. Details are in Background ([What you pay, and what you earn and keep](#what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep)) <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>.

**BOESL's zero-cost places:** what they include is disputed (see [Status](#status)); the Ministry says no worker may be made to bear extra cost for the job <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-find-a-real-job"></a>

### 3.2 Step 1: Find a real job

There are three routes to a job, and all start with OEP registration ([Step 1, item 1: Register on the Overseas Employment Platform (OEP)](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1)) <sup>[16 (2021)](#o16), [30](#o30)</sup>:

- **A BOESL circular** ([Step 1, item 3: Apply for government (BOESL) jobs](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)): zero-cost by BOESL's account, few places <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
- **A private agency** ([Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4)): only the 25 processing agencies can process the visa <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
- **A relative's employer** ([Step 1, item 5: Or through a relative or friend in Malaysia](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5)): still goes through those agencies <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

Whatever the route, the checks that tell a real job from a fake are [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), items 4, 6 and 7, [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "The trap" and [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying".

1. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1"></a>**Register on the Overseas Employment Platform (OEP),** BMET's website for job-seekers. Agencies must pick workers from BMET's database of registered job-seekers, and BMET clears no one outside it <sup>[16 (2021)](#o16), [30](#o30)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[71](#o71), [66](#o66), [2](#o2), [N281](#n281)</sup>:

   - your NID (national ID card);
   - a phone that can receive codes;
   - a bKash, Nagad or other mobile-money account with Tk 200;
   - your passport details, education and work skills.

   1. In Google Chrome, open [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration) <sup>[66](#o66), [71](#o71)</sup>.
   2. Type your name in English exactly as on your NID (no dots; spaces where the NID has them), your mobile and NID numbers, and your date of birth, division and district as on the NID <sup>[71](#o71), [66](#o66)</sup>. Type the captcha numbers, accept the terms and press "যাচাই করুন" (Verify) <sup>[71](#o71)</sup>.
   3. Type the code (OTP) sent to your phone, press Submit, then "সংরক্ষণ করুন ও এগিয়ে যান" (Save and continue) <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.
   4. Fill in the form, then press "পেমেন্ট করতে এগিয়ে যান" (Proceed to payment), "পেমেন্ট করুন" (Pay), Mobile Banking and your service; enter your account number, the code and your PIN to pay the Tk 200 government fee <sup>[66](#o66), [2](#o2)</sup>.
   5. Download and print your registration card, which carries your BMET number; you can download it again at [oep.gov.bd/employee-card](https://www.oep.gov.bd/employee-card) with that number, without logging in <sup>[66](#o66), [72](#o72), [N281](#n281)</sup>.
   6. Take 2 printed copies of the registration slip to give your fingerprints <sup>[2](#o2)</sup> at any DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office, BMET's district office), TTC (Technical Training Centre) or IMT (Institute of Marine Technology) <sup>[73 (2023)](#o73)</sup>. DEMO addresses, phones and emails: [bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922e139933eb65569e2af6c](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922e139933eb65569e2af6c) <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Does giving fingerprints at a DEMO, TTC or IMT cost anything?
      > - What hours do DEMOs, TTCs and IMTs take fingerprints?
      > - What does the worker get back after giving fingerprints?

   **Check it is real:** register at employee.oep.gov.bd, the address BMET's district office gives <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>; use no middlemen and pay fees only through the proper channel, against a receipt <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. **If it fails:** if "Verify" shows an error, check your name, NID number and date of birth match the NID exactly <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>; otherwise call the OEP hotline, 09614445271 <sup>[72](#o72), [75](#o75)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-2"></a>**Learn a trade if you need one.** BMET's TTCs teach 55 trades <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>; every TTC, with its principal's name, address, phone and email, is listed at [bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922dd2c933eb65569e13cd6](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922dd2c933eb65569e13cd6) <sup>[76](#o76), [77](#o77)</sup>. Each TTC posts admission notices with courses, minimum education, dates and fees; the Bangladesh-Korea TTC (BKTTC) in Dhaka, for example, posts them at bkttc.dhaka.gov.bd <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>. BKTTC's 3- or 6-month courses (360 or 720 hours), in trades such as welding and plumbing, end with an NSDA (National Skills Development Authority) skills certificate <sup>[79](#o79), [78](#o78)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[78](#o78), [66](#o66)</sup>:

   - your OEP registration;
   - the course's minimum education (at BKTTC, JSC, the Junior School Certificate, for welding);
   - a current, clear photo in your OEP profile.

   1. Log in at [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp) with your mobile number and the code sent to it <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.
   2. Open the training portal (ট্রেনিং পোর্টাল), then Training, then Trade/Skills; choose your division, district and centre and press Search <sup>[66](#o66), [78](#o78)</sup>.
   3. Pick the trade, press Apply, check your photo matches your face now, then press Continue, "Process to Enroll" and Confirm; "My Course" shows your applications <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.
   4. Sit the admission test on the date in the notice; BKTTC posts results on its notice board and website and sends them by SMS <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>.
   5. Pay the fee against a money receipt (BKTTC: Tk 748 for 3 months, Tk 1,484 for 6 months); courses run by funded projects are free <sup>[78](#o78), [1](#o1)</sup>. At BKTTC, regular trainees with at least 80% attendance get Tk 150 a day for transport and food when funds are allocated <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>.

   **Scams:** if anyone offers a TTC place or exam pass for money, tell the nearest law-enforcement officer <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. To complain about a TTC:

   1. Complain to its principal (phone and email on BMET's TTC list, [Step 1, item 2: Learn a trade if you need one](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-2)) <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>.
   2. Then complain to BMET's Director (Employment), diremi@bmet.gov.bd, 01711111544 <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

   The government's migrant call centre, 16135, takes calls on any migration problem <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

   **Construction jobs:** Malaysia requires foreign construction workers to hold a construction skills certificate (SKKP) from their own country, checked by CIDB (Malaysia's Construction Industry Development Board), and to finish an online safety and health course (eSICW) <sup>[20 (2024)](#o20)</sup>. The CIDB skill test runs at Khulna TTC (Teligati, Khulna; 041-2870047 or 01716966627; ttckhu@gmail.com) and is to start in Dhaka, Chattogram and Rajshahi <sup>[21](#o21), [77](#o77)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does the CIDB skill test still run at Khulna TTC?
   > - How does a worker register for the CIDB skill test?
   > - What is the CIDB skill test fee?
   > - What certificate does a worker get after passing the CIDB skill test?

3. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3"></a>**Apply for government (BOESL) jobs.** BOESL posts Malaysia job circulars on [boesl.gov.bd](https://www.boesl.gov.bd/) <sup>[81](#o81)</sup>. Its portal, brms.boesl.gov.bd, has been closed since 11 October 2026 for a technical fault; follow its notice board and [facebook.com/boesl.gov.bd](https://www.facebook.com/boesl.gov.bd/) <sup>[82](#o82), [83](#o83)</sup>. Each circular sets its own rules; the example below is its 5 November 2026 zero-cost circular for DHL Supply Chain (Malaysia), for men, at RM1,700 basic pay a month <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. It had two posts <sup>[N282](#n282)</sup>:

   - **Operations Assistant:** at least SSC (Secondary School Certificate); English reading, writing and communication; physical fitness for warehouse work; one to two years' experience; forklift, power pallet truck or reach truck skills a plus.
   - **Technical Skill Workers:** at least HSC (Higher Secondary Certificate) and good knowledge of warehouse network cabling, racking and IT equipment; electrical vocational, polytechnic or degree holders preferred.

   It offered a three-year renewable contract at RM1,700 (about Tk 52,000) a month, eight hours a day, six days a week, with overtime as the company needs and Malaysian labour law allows; the employer provides housing, first aid and transport <sup>[N282](#n282)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>:

   - a passport valid for at least 18 to 24 months;
   - your OEP registration;
   - your education and experience details;
   - for the interview:
     - 4 colour passport photos on white;
     - colour copies of every passport you have held;
     - original education and experience certificates;
     - your NID;
     - a CV showing computer skills (MS Excel), numeracy, any driving licence and any overseas or warehouse experience.

   1. Check the circular's posts, age limit (22 to 35 in the example), education, skills and window <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
   2. Apply online through the circular's link (in the example, [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration)) with your passport number, education and experience, for one post only, within the window; incomplete or wrong applications are rejected <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
   3. A software lottery picks a shortlist, which BOESL posts on its website; check your name there <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. In the example BOESL ran the lottery electronically on 8 November 2026 at the Probashi Kallyan Bhaban auditorium, with Ministry representatives, BOESL officials and journalists present; it picked 300 of 4,029 applicants (200 for Operations Assistant, 100 for Technical Skill Workers) for the final 112 places <sup>[N5](#n5), [N282](#n282)</sup>.
   4. If shortlisted, bring the documents above to the interview, where the employer's representative tests you and chooses <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>; a driving licence and work experience count in your favour <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. In the example the Ministry said on 12 November 2026 that a nine-member delegation from Malaysia would hold practical tests and interviews of the 300 from that day to 15 November, that BOESL would not interfere in the selection and that the candidates the employer picks are final <sup>[N98](#n98)</sup>; the employer's representatives are to choose the final 112 <sup>[N282](#n282)</sup>.

   In the example, workers pay nothing at any stage <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Even selection does not guarantee a job <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. UN experts said thousands recruited through BOESL paid over five times the official fees <sup>[N283](#n283), [N210](#n210)</sup>. BOESL has no agents or sub-agents, never takes cash and takes money only after final selection, by bank pay order; gangs use its name to sell Malaysia jobs <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>. BOESL's managing director said on 12 November 2026 that if any broker, middleman or unauthorised person tries to take money at any stage of the recruitment, or any candidate takes part in an unethical transaction, strict legal action will be taken against both on proof <sup>[N98](#n98)</sup>. Office: Probashi Kallyan Bhaban (5th floor), 71-72 Eskaton Garden, Ramna, Dhaka; 02-58311838, 02-48319125, 02-48317515 or 01765411653; info@boesl.gov.bd <sup>[8](#o8), [84](#o84)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which BOESL Malaysia jobs charge the worker <sup>[N280](#n280)</sup>?
   > - How much do those BOESL Malaysia jobs charge the worker?
   > - Do all 10,000 zero-cost places go through BOESL circulars <sup>[N279](#n279)</sup>?

4. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4"></a>**Or go through a private agency.** Only agencies on Malaysia's list can recruit: open [fwcms.com.my/affiliates](https://fwcms.com.my/affiliates/#recruitment-agencies), choose Recruitment Agencies, then Bangladesh <sup>[26](#o26), [7](#o7)</sup>.

   You'll need: the agency's name and RL number (the recruiting licence number every licensed Bangladeshi agency has) <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.

   1. Find the agency on the FWCMS list <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
   2. On BMET's list, [oep.gov.bd/agencies](https://www.oep.gov.bd/agencies), check the same RL number shows status Active (not Expired, Cancelled or Suspended) and an unexpired validity date; note its registered address and office phone <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.
   3. Go to the agency at that address, not to a middleman's office <sup>[85](#o85), [1](#o1)</sup>.
   4. Check the demand letter, contract, job details, salary and migration cost <sup>[21](#o21)</sup> against the permission letter ([Step 1, item 6: Find and check the permission letter for your job](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6)).

   **What happens in practice:** for overcharging and ghost jobs, see [What you pay, and what you earn and keep](#what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep), Which employer, and [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "The trap" <sup>[N284 (2024)](#n284), [N11](#n11)</sup>. Some workers arrive with valid work permits to find no job ([After you arrive, item 1: Wait for your employer at the airport](#after-you-arrive-item-1)) <sup>[N285](#n285), [N243](#n243)</sup>.

   **Which agencies.** Only BOESL and the 25 processing agencies can process the calling visa and e-visa; the 312 associates cannot send workers directly (see [The processing agency: the toll gate](#the-processing-agency-the-toll-gate)) <sup>[7](#o7), [26](#o26), [N8](#n8)</sup>. In BMET's records from January 2004 to 23 August 2026, only eight of the 25 had sent workers to Malaysia, 1,612 between them; the other 17 sent none <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. Five of the eight sent 11, 74, 100, 150 and 202 workers, and the other three 436, 333 and 306 <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>. FWCMS and OEP give different addresses for several of the 25, including these; check the RL number on OEP <sup>[26](#o26), [85](#o85)</sup>:

   - Rifa International: Banani on FWCMS, Purana Paltan on OEP;
   - The Anchor Care: Savar DOHS on FWCMS, Dhamrai on OEP;
   - Brothers Trading & Contracting: Banani on FWCMS, Shantinagar on OEP;
   - Goodness Service: Banani on FWCMS, Naya Paltan on OEP.

   The eight, and anything reported about them:

   - **General Trading Company (RL0320)** and **Rifa International (RL0693):** among 57 agencies the ACC asked BMET about on 23 July 2026, in a case alleging illegal wealth from trafficking women abroad with forged documents <sup>[N8](#n8), [85](#o85)</sup>.
   - **United Gulf Services (RL1036):** Prothom Alo (September 2026) reports its owner, Laila Arzuman Banu, is the wife of Abdul Hai, whose Greenland Overseas was in the earlier syndicate and is now run by their daughter, Rehana Arzuman Hai; neither answered Prothom Alo's calls <sup>[N8](#n8), [85](#o85), [N76](#n76)</sup>; Greenland's owners are among those the ACC charged in September 2025 over Malaysia fees <sup>[N286](#n286)</sup>.
   - **The Anchor Care Global Migration and Consultant (RL1765):** 11 of the 371 workers it had cleared went to Malaysia <sup>[N8](#n8), [85](#o85)</sup>.
   - **Al-Shupto Overseas (RL0580)**, **Brothers Trading & Contracting (RL0671)**, **Country Employment Agency (RL1197)** and **Neighbour Associates (RL0746)**: nothing reported against them <sup>[N8](#n8), [85](#o85)</sup>.

   The 17 without a Malaysia record, as Prothom Alo printed them: Tania Trade, Satkhira International, A-Plus, Al Hayat Overseas, Valley Trade, Khan Jahan Ali Overseas, Bengal Human Resource, Earth Smart, At Taqwa, Motherland, Mangrove, Jewel Rinku Enterprise, Bangladesh One Overseas, Annesha Air, Chandpur International, Godness Services and Bhaluka Overseas <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>. Bangladesh One Overseas Limited had taken clearance for 795 workers in all, none for Malaysia <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>. Bhaluka Overseas, licensed in 2022, had taken clearance for 235 workers up to 23 August 2026, none for Malaysia; its proprietor, Md Abu Sohag Khan, told Prothom Alo he had earlier sent more than a thousand workers to Malaysia in the names of various other agencies, was selected automatically from Malaysia and paid no one for it <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>.

   Of the 17 without a Malaysia record, **Goodness Service (RL2068)** is also among the 57 in the ACC's inquiry, and by early October 2026 the High Commission had attested a demand under it for 28 workers for the NSK Trade City supermarket chain <sup>[N8](#n8), [N72](#n72), [85](#o85)</sup>. The FWCMS list still names associates whose licences are cancelled or suspended on OEP:

   - RS International (RL1428, under The Anchor Care): cancelled on 1 June 2026 over allegedly sending 30 young men to Russia's war front or risky work <sup>[26](#o26), [85](#o85), [N287](#n287)</sup>;
   - T S Overseas (RL1755, under Country Employment Agency): cancelled on 1 June 2026 over the same allegation <sup>[26](#o26), [85](#o85), [N287](#n287)</sup>;
   - Triple A Overseas (RL2630, under BOESL): suspended; see [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying" <sup>[26](#o26), [85](#o85)</sup>.

   **Licences withdrawn on 19 July 2026.** The Ministry withdrew the licences of 49 agencies that Prothom Alo linked to the 2022-24 syndicate; court challenges have not reversed it (see [The cases against the syndicate](#the-cases-against-the-syndicate)) <sup>[N165](#n165), [N197](#n197)</sup>. Seven are named in The cases against the syndicate <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>. The others, as Prothom Alo printed them: Shaheen Travels, Irving Enterprise, Hridoy Trade International, Rabbi International, Jahrat International, 4 Site International, Binimoy International, Akash Bhraman Limited, QK Quick Express Limited, United Export Limited, GMG Trading Private Limited, Sadia International, The Super Eastern Limited, Elegants Overseas Limited, Midway Overseas Limited, Madina Overseas, Al Khamis International, New Age International, Damashi Corporation Limited, Galaxy Corporation, Sultan Overseas, Provati International, Manispower Corporation, Amin Tours and Travels, ISMT Human Resource Development, Darbar Global Overseas, Natasha Overseas, Aparajita Overseas, Stanford Employment Private Limited, Jannat Overseas, BM Travels, BNS Overseas Limited, Rubel Bangladesh Limited <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>, Trans Asia Integrate Services Limited, Al Farah Human Resources and Consultancy, Brothers International, Thanex International Limited, JG Alfalah Management, Imperial Resources Limited, PR Overseas Limited, MEF Global Bangladesh Limited and Ananya Apurba Recruiting Agency <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>.

   Bangladesh has no official grading of agencies; the state minister says he could not confidently recommend one <sup>[N288](#n288)</sup>. Every licensed agency's record, including the 2022-24 syndicate agencies and their cases, is in the `agencies` folder.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which agencies place Bangladeshis in the promised job at a fair cost?

5. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5"></a>**Or through a relative or friend in Malaysia.** A relative can find you the job, but he can't send you himself. His employer applies for a visa in your name, with your passport copy and photo <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>; the trade calls this an "own visa" (নিজস্ব ভিসা). The recruiting agency and the processing agency ([Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4)) still handle it: the recruiting agency holds the demand letter, must pick you from BMET's database and gets your BMET clearance under its own licence; the processing agency ([Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4)) does the calling visa and e-visa and gives the recruiting agency a no-objection certificate <sup>[30](#o30), [7](#o7)</sup>. Employers can't name an associate agency as the approved agency, though they were reported in September 2026 to be getting the right to name the agency of their choice <sup>[N8](#n8), [N11](#n11)</sup>. In 2022-24 an agency outside the list running a named worker's case had to hold his power of attorney while a listed agency did the "visa handling" <sup>[N289 (2024)](#n289)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Will BMET clear a worker named by the employer whom the recruiting agency did not find itself?
   > - Does the agency then charge less?

   **Check you fit.** The age and sector rules (see Is it open now, and who can go?) apply, and the job must be in a sector the employer's own quota covers <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. New quotas were frozen from March 2023, and the High Commission began attesting new demand letters again only in October 2026 <sup>[N290 (2024)](#n290), [N72](#n72)</sup>.

   You'll need:

   - from the relative, photos of his own i-Kad and PLKS sticker;
   - the employer's full name, Malaysian registration number and work address;
   - the job and the basic monthly pay, which must be at least RM1,700 (about Tk 52,000), not counting overtime <sup>[11 (2024)](#o11), [40 (2022)](#o40), [N291](#n291), [N1](#n1)</sup>;
   - the names and RL numbers of both agencies;
   - a photocopy of the attested demand letter <sup>[85](#o85), [N24](#n24)</sup>.

   1. **Check the relative's own employer.** The i-Kad (foreign worker identity card) must be carried at all times, so he has it <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. The employer on it should be the company he says he works for and the employer on your offer. Some legal workers are on an outsourcing company's books and moved between client companies, or made to work informally for other firms, and only find out after arriving <sup>[9](#o9), [N152 (2023)](#n152)</sup>. Have the relative buy the employer's SSM Company Profile (RM15.40, [Step 1, item 7: Check the employer exists](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-7)) and photograph the business at the address it gives <sup>[49](#o49), [87](#o87)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Do the i-Kad and PLKS sticker print the employer's name?

   2. **Register in BMET's database** on OEP and give your fingerprints at a DEMO, as in [Step 1, item 1: Register on the Overseas Employment Platform (OEP)](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1); the fee is Tk 200 <sup>[66](#o66), [2](#o2), [88 (2022)](#o88)</sup>.
   3. **Check both agencies.** Find each on the FWCMS list and check its RL number shows Active on OEP ([Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4)) <sup>[26](#o26), [85](#o85)</sup>. Ask whose name is on your power of attorney; the agency must hand it and the demand letter to BMET before clearance <sup>[N289 (2024)](#n289), [30](#o30)</sup>.
   4. **Have the demand letter checked.** The agency keeps the original, which covers a batch of workers <sup>[30](#o30), [N72](#n72)</sup>. Ask for a photocopy showing the High Commission's attestation stamp, the employer, the number of workers and the agency it is addressed to; no unattested demand letter is accepted <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. Take it with the RL number to your DEMO or BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka: the check is free, by hand (Malaysia is not on BMET's online visa check) and takes 3 working days <sup>[21](#o21), [1](#o1)</sup>.

   5. **Wait for LULUS on MyIMMs.** Have the relative get the employer's company registration number and the application number, and check the status ([Step 3, item 2: Track the approval](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-2)) <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>. If he can't or won't get them, stop. Only the employer or its own appointed representative may deal with Immigration, and using an agent is an offence, so a relative who says he or "his agent" is handling Immigration is not describing the real process <sup>[90 (2021)](#o90)</sup>.
   6. **Don't hand over your passport ("submitting the book", বই জমা) or pay for a medical before the visa papers exist.** Brokers and agencies have taken passports and money with a promise of the medical; the Ministry says hand neither over without confirming approval <sup>[N292](#n292), [12](#o12), [N293](#n293)</sup>. The agency takes the VDR (calling visa) papers to the medical centre <sup>[N292](#n292)</sup>. The medical is Tk 8,000 and no more, at any FWCMS-listed centre you choose; no centre belongs to an agency, and the MiGRAMS slip is free <sup>[N294](#n294), [26](#o26)</sup>.
   7. **Pay in stages, against proof.**
      - *The agency's share:* only by cheque, bank draft or pay order into its own account, against a receipt <sup>[21](#o21), [1](#o1)</sup>. A permission letter lets the agency take its money only once the visa and flight are confirmed <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>. Get the contract and its BMET-attested Bangla translation before clearance ("Before paying") <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>.
      - *Money to the relative:* the Ministry warns against paying before approval is confirmed <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>, so pay in stages:

        1. Nothing large before LULUS shows on MyIMMs <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>.
        2. The next part after the visa is issued <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>.
        3. The rest after BMET clearance <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

        For every payment, get a dated note signed by the relative, with the amount and what it is for, and a witness's signature; a village court hears only claims backed by a receipt or other document, and without one a BMET complaint rests on your word <sup>[N295](#n295), [N244 (2024)](#n244)</sup>. Pay by bank transfer or mobile banking to his account in Bangladesh, never by hundi, which is illegal and leaves no record <sup>[N221 (2022)](#n221), [21](#o21), [N295](#n295)</sup>.

   **What it costs.** The cap, the market price and where the money goes are in Background (see [What you pay, and what you earn and keep](#what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep)). If the employer's agency is not one of the 25, one of them takes a fee on top, alleged at RM5,500 (about Tk 1.68 lakh) in 2026 <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does a relative in Malaysia charge in 2026 for a place on his employer's quota?
   > - How does that payment split between the boss, the relative and the two agencies?

   **After you land.** If you fail the FOMEMA medical (Malaysia's foreign worker health check) within 30 days, the employer sends you home on a Check Out Memo ([After you arrive, item 2: FOMEMA medical within 30 days](#after-you-arrive-item-2)) <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>, so put in the relative's signed note what he repays if that happens.

   **If it fails.** Taking money on a false promise of a job abroad is a crime in Bangladesh for anyone, relatives included: up to 5 years' jail and a fine of at least Tk 1 lakh, tried by a magistrate <sup>[91 (2023)](#o91)</sup>. The Tk 3 lakh security that compensates workers covers only a sub-agent registered with BMET, not a relative <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. Where to go:

   - BMET's online form, against a named person: it accepts "a person" and takes receipts and recordings as evidence <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>.
   - The Ministry (see [If an agency or broker takes your money, item 3: Or complain straight to the Ministry](#if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-3)); the 2013 Act requires an investigation within 30 working days <sup>[91 (2023)](#o91)</sup>.
   - A civil suit for compensation, which the 2013 Act allows above the village court's Tk 3 lakh limit <sup>[N295](#n295), [91 (2023)](#o91)</sup>.
   - Free legal advice and the village court (see [If an agency or broker takes your money, item 4: Courts and NGOs](#if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-4)).

   If you land and there is no job, see [After you arrive, item 1: Wait for your employer at the airport](#after-you-arrive-item-1).

   > **Need to know**
   > - How has a Bangladeshi court or BMET enforced an order against a relative who lives in Malaysia?

   **The zero-cost alternative** is BOESL's circulars ([Step 1, item 3: Apply for government (BOESL) jobs](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)) <sup>[8](#o8), [93](#o93)</sup>. Malaysia's own direct-hiring platform, TURAP, was approved by its Cabinet in April 2026 but has not launched <sup>[N296](#n296)</sup>. Malaysia's Human Resources Minister, Ramanan Ramakrishnan, proposed it to stop the exploitation of Bangladeshi workers at village level; it is built by Bestinet, the company that built FWCMS, and some stakeholders in Bangladesh and Malaysia suspect it is another way to keep recruitment concentrated in a few firms, Prothom Alo reported from The Edge on 16 June 2026 <sup>[N54](#n54), [23](#o23)</sup>.

6. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6"></a>**Find and check the permission letter for your job.** For each Malaysia job order the Ministry or BMET issues a permission letter naming the agency and its RL number, the employer, the jobs, salary, age range, hours, food, housing, airfare, contract length and the most the agency may take from each worker <sup>[94](#o94), [30](#o30), [95 (2024)](#o95), [96](#o96)</sup>. As of 7 November 2026, BMET's list had no Malaysia permission letter from the 2026 reopening; the newest was dated 27 February 2025 <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. So for a 2026 job this check can't be done online yet <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>:

   - the agency's RL number;
   - the employer's name and salary the agency gave you.

   1. Open [bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos) and search "Malaysia" (or মালয়েশিয়া) <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>.
   2. Open the entry with your agency's RL number; titles give the letter number and RL number, like "DN25-225 RL1274" <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>.
   3. Compare its employer, salary and maximum fee with what the agency told you <sup>[30](#o30), [95 (2024)](#o95), [96](#o96)</sup>. Its English "Recruitment Permission Letter" page shows a permission ID, the block visa number (Malaysia's approval reference for the group of visas), the visa issue date and the employer's name and city; check they match your visa and demand letter <sup>[30](#o30), [98 (2023)](#o98)</sup>.
   4. Take the demand letter or visa (if stamped, with a copy of the passport page) to your DEMO or BMET's head office, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000; BMET checks it free within 3 working days <sup>[21](#o21), [1](#o1), [99 (2021)](#o99)</sup>. If it is fake, complain as under [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money".

   > **Need to know**
   > - Will BMET post Malaysia permission letters from the 2026 reopening?
   > - Does BMET check a permission-letter number by hand, at the same counter as the demand letter?

7. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-7"></a>**Check the employer exists** through SSM e-Info, the online shop of Malaysia's Companies Commission (SSM, Suruhanjaya Syarikat Malaysia) <sup>[49](#o49), [87](#o87)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>:

   - the employer's full name or Malaysian registration number from the demand letter;
   - an email address;
   - a credit or debit card or Malaysian online banking (FPX).

   1. Register and log in at [ssm-einfo.my](https://www.ssm-einfo.my/) <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>.
   2. Top up your account; card and FPX payments clear instantly <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>.
   3. Search the name or number and buy the Company Profile, RM15.40 <sup>[50](#o50), [49](#o49)</sup>.
   4. Save the PDF at once; it stays in Transaction History for only 7 days <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>.
   5. Check the status, registered and business addresses and nature of business <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>; "Existing" means not being wound up, struck off or dissolved <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>. Compare name and address with the demand letter.

   **If it fails:** if you can't get the document, call the e-Info call centre, 1300 30 4636 <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What can a worker do if the company can't be found on SSM e-Info?
   > - What can a worker do if the company on SSM e-Info doesn't match the demand letter?

**How long.** In order:

1. The employer gets its quota approved by the Human Resources Ministry's One Stop Centre and pays the levy <sup>[5](#o5), [9](#o9)</sup>.
2. The High Commission in Kuala Lumpur attests the demand letter <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>.
3. The agency selects you from BMET's database <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>.
4. The employer applies for your VDR through FWCMS, which IMI aims to approve in 5 working days <sup>[5](#o5), [100](#o100)</sup>.
5. The medical <sup>[N292](#n292), [26](#o26)</sup>.
6. The High Commission of Malaysia in Dhaka issues the visa <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>.
7. BMET clears you in 3 working days <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
8. The flight, paid by the employer <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>.

These times are the fastest possible, not promises; delays raise the price (see [How it is paid](#how-it-is-paid)) <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>.

**What you get back** ([Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr); [Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight)) <sup>[89](#o89), [51](#o51), [7](#o7)</sup>:

- a calling visa (VDR) in your name for the named employer, shown as LULUS (approved) on MyIMMs;
- the visa issued in Dhaka;
- BMET clearance through the recruiting agency.

**The trap.**
- *Resold on arrival.* Thirty-six men who went with a contract naming their employer were each "sold" to another company for about Tk 2 lakh, at under half the promised pay <sup>[N297 (2024)](#n297)</sup>.
- *A-to-B (এটুবি) without telling you.* An A-to-B, "free" or "agent" visa means the company that brings you gives you no work <sup>[N293](#n293)</sup>. Agents put new workers on one without saying so, and a worker can't tell it from a direct-company visa <sup>[N293](#n293)</sup>. Working for anyone but the employer that applied is illegal <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
- *The free visa (ফ্রি ভিসা).* What it is and what it costs are in Background (see [Company visa, agent visa, free visa](#company-visa-agent-visa-free-visa)). A "renewal" through an agent can be a fake PLKS sticker: one ring sold them for RM5,000 to 6,000 <sup>[N238 (2023)](#n238)</sup>. Getting a pass by a false statement or holding a forged one is an offence <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. When renewals stop, you are undocumented: detained, deported at your own or your family's cost, and blacklisted from Malaysia, usually for 1 to 5 years, with your biometrics on file <sup>[N298 (2017)](#n298), [N234 (2022)](#n234), [N276](#n276)</sup>.
- *Passports held for ransom.* On arrival, agency people have taken passports "for the visa" and asked Tk 70,000 to 1 lakh to return them <sup>[N297 (2024)](#n297)</sup>. What the employer may do with your passport, and the penalty, are in [After you arrive, item 4: Keep your own passport](#after-you-arrive-item-4). In Bangladesh, anyone keeping a migrant's passport without lawful reason commits an offence under the 2013 Act <sup>[91 (2023)](#o91)</sup>.

**Before paying:**

- **Know what you should pay.** On 15 November 2026 the minister told parliament the cost of sending a worker had been fixed at Tk 1,54,000; which notice sets it, and whether it binds private agencies, is not yet published (see [The cap](#the-cap); What people really pay) <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. The old cap was not followed <sup>[N7](#n7)</sup>. The 2022 cap covered everything the worker pays in Bangladesh (passport, medical, registration, welfare fee, insurance, smart card, pre-departure training, clothing, agency service charge and other costs); the employer paid the security deposit, pass, insurance and air tickets <sup>[10 (2022)](#o10)</sup>. Your job's permission letter states its maximum, in some letters nothing <sup>[94](#o94), [31 (2024)](#o31)</sup>. The agency must display in writing at its office the migration cost fixed for your destination and the monthly wage <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>; compare both with your permission letter ([Step 1, item 6: Find and check the permission letter for your job](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6)).
- **Pay only by cheque, bank draft or pay order into the agency's own bank account, and keep every receipt** <sup>[30](#o30), [21](#o21)</sup>. Agencies and middlemen have started collecting passports from people who want to go <sup>[N7](#n7)</sup>; on 10 September 2026 a BMET mobile court sealed Triple A Overseas (RL-2630), fined it Tk 50,000 and seized passports it had collected with promises of Malaysian visas <sup>[N299](#n299)</sup>; the expatriates' welfare secretary said on 16 September that three boxes of passports were seized <sup>[N69](#n69)</sup>. The Ministry has warned against paying anything before the Malaysia process is finalised, or handing over a passport or money without confirming approval <sup>[N8](#n8), [12](#o12)</sup>.
- **If a local broker brings you the job.** An agency may use only a sub-agent registered with BMET, and must list its registered sub-agents on its own website <sup>[27 (2023)](#o27), [28](#o28)</sup>. A registered sub-agent has an office with a signboard showing his name, registration number and date and the agency's name and licence number, and his BMET registration certificate on display inside <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. The agency and the sub-agent are both liable for what he does, and a worker cheated by a sub-agent is compensated from the Tk 3 lakh security he lodges with the agency <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Has BMET, any DEMO or any agency yet published a list of registered sub-agents <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>?

- **Get the contract and a Bangla translation before you go.** The agency must sign a contract with you before BMET clearance and give you a Bangla translation attested by BMET <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>; check the contract is attested by the destination country's embassy <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. Keep your copy: a wage claim in Malaysia asks for it <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101)</sup>, and some employers swap it on arrival for one the worker can't read <sup>[45](#o45)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - How does a worker check the BMET attestation on the contract?
  > - What can a worker do if the agency won't hand over the contract?

- **If you need to borrow,** Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB), the government bank for migrant workers, lends up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral at 9% simple interest, repaid monthly over up to 3 years, and decides within 7 working days <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>. It lends only once you have a valid visa and does not arrange visas <sup>[N212 (2023)](#n212)</sup>.

  You'll need <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>:

  - the free form;
  - 3 recent photos;
  - copies of your NID, passport, visa and BMET card (BMET's smart card);
  - the contract if you have it;
  - 3 signed MICR cheque leaves from your own active bank account, with its statement;
  - a PKB savings account opened with at least Tk 500;
  - two guarantors, each with a photo, NID and proof of address <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>:
    - one well-off person, with 3 signed cheque leaves, a bank statement and proof of income;
    - one family member.

  Many applicants can't get the loan for lack of guarantors <sup>[N212 (2023)](#n212)</sup>. You and they must live permanently in the branch's area; you must be 18 to 60 with no loan in default <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.

  1. Apply at your area's PKB branch (list linked from PKB's loan page on mygov.bd), or online on the [mygov.bd](https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1726556874) migration loan page with "আবেদন করুন" (Apply) <sup>[102 (2019)](#o102)</sup>. You can apply only once until the loan is approved or refused <sup>[102 (2019)](#o102)</sup>.
  2. Online, you get application and tracking numbers by SMS and can check status with your mobile and tracking numbers <sup>[102 (2019)](#o102)</sup>.
  3. Pay 0.5% processing and 0.5% documentation fees plus VAT and Tk 900 per lakh for risk cover; bring 16 revenue stamps of Tk 10, and 3 adhesive stamps of Tk 100 per guarantor <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.
  4. PKB sends an SMS when the loan is approved <sup>[N212 (2023)](#n212)</sup>.

  PKB helpline: 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>; loans department: DGM, Loans and Advances, 01716-175560, migration@pkb.gov.bd <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>. To complain <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>:

  1. Write to PKB's grievance officer, GM (Admin and Accounts), +88-02-48321195, gm2@pkb.gov.bd (30 working days).
  2. Then the DMD, dmd@pkb.gov.bd (20).
  3. Then the MD, md@pkb.gov.bd (60).

  > **Need to know**
  > - Is the BMET card, which comes with emigration clearance, needed before PKB approves the loan <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>?
  > - What can a worker do if PKB refuses the loan?

<a id="if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money"></a>

#### 3.2.1 If an agency or broker takes your money

**Expect slow, partial recovery.** BMET cases take 4 months to a year, and in August 2023 the average award was about Tk 74,000 against Tk 3.5 to 5 lakh paid; workers with full documents get more back <sup>[N244 (2024)](#n244)</sup>. Keep proof of every payment from the start ([Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying") <sup>[30](#o30), [21](#o21)</sup>.

**If it doesn't send you, or sends you to a different job:**

1. <a id="if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-1"></a>**Complain to BMET.** It takes complaints from people cheated before going, after going or after coming back; it is free and meant to be settled within 30 working days <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

   You'll need:

   - a copy of your NID or passport <sup>[N295](#n295), [92](#o92)</sup>;
   - every receipt, bank slip or mobile-money record of what you paid <sup>[N295](#n295), [92](#o92)</sup>;
   - the contract, visa or work permit if you have them <sup>[N295](#n295), [92](#o92)</sup>;
   - any letters, messages, audio or video from the agency or broker <sup>[N295](#n295), [92](#o92)</sup>;
   - written down: the agency's name and RL number, the name and phone of whoever took the money, dates and amounts, and the employer's name and address <sup>[48 (2024)](#o48), [92](#o92)</sup>.

   1. Fill in BMET's complaint form against a recruiting agent, or write an application to BMET's Director General, with your proof <sup>[48 (2024)](#o48), [1](#o1), [99 (2021)](#o99)</sup>. Give your name, father's or husband's name, address, phone and email, the type of complaint (for example money misappropriation, or not sending workers abroad), everyone affected, the agency's address, and the details above <sup>[48 (2024)](#o48), [92](#o92)</sup>.
   2. Hand it in at BMET's complaint cell, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000, and get the acknowledgement receipt with your complaint ID <sup>[1](#o1), [99 (2021)](#o99), [48 (2024)](#o48)</sup>. The responsible officer is BMET's Director (Employment), 01711111544, diremi@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>; BMET's main number is 02-49357972 <sup>[99 (2021)](#o99)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Is the online form at ovijog.bmet.gov.bd, which takes evidence files and shows status, in service <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>?

   3. BMET investigates, hears both sides, usually in Dhaka, and recommends a decision to the Ministry, which decides <sup>[1](#o1), [N295](#n295)</sup>. If a guilty agency won't pay, BMET can take the money from its security deposit <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>.

   **What happens in practice:** most victims get back less than half of what they lost <sup>[N244 (2024)](#n244)</sup>. Workers who paid middlemen with no written agreement have only their testimony, passport and BMET card to show <sup>[N244 (2024)](#n244)</sup>. A complaint proved against a registered sub-agent can cost him his registration <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.

   **Cases:**

   - **2006 and 2010:** Lal Mia, 54, of Chhoto Amian village, Shalikha, Magura, said he paid a broker Tk 2.5 lakh in 2006 and Tk 3 lakh in 2010 to go to Malaysia, never went and never got the money back; across four attempts from 2002 to 2012, also for Saudi Arabia and Dubai, he paid about Tk 10.5 lakh, selling four bighas of farmland and his 12.5-decimal homestead, and in June 2026 he lived in a house on someone else's land, selling eggs by bicycle <sup>[N300](#n300)</sup>.
   - **February 2024:** after at least 63 young men from Gangni upazila, Meherpur, said they had been held without work in Kuala Lumpur, Meherpur's Deputy Commissioner said the district law-and-order committee had advised the families to file cases, but none was willing to <sup>[N146 (2024)](#n146)</sup>.

2. <a id="if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-2"></a>**If BMET does not settle it,** go up the ladder.

   You'll need <sup>[48 (2024)](#o48), [1](#o1)</sup>:

   - your complaint ID;
   - copies of everything you gave BMET.

   1. Write to BMET's Additional Director General (Employment), adgemp@bmet.gov.bd, 02-48321949 or 01720105492 <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
   2. Then write to the Ministry's Secretary, secretary@probashi.gov.bd, who has 1 month <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
   3. Then file on the Cabinet Division's Grievance Redress System at [grs.gov.bd](https://www.grs.gov.bd/), which has 3 months <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

3. <a id="if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-3"></a>**Or complain straight to the Ministry.** It is free; action starts within 3 to 5 working days, both sides are heard, and it is meant to be settled within 30 working days <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>:

   - an application;
   - documents proving the complaint;
   - your full address;
   - the agency's RL number and address.

   Send them to the Ministry's Enforcement-2 branch, sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255, or to the Ministry at Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000 <sup>[104](#o104), [105](#o105)</sup>.

   The branch also holds mobile courts (on-the-spot courts under the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013) on its own initiative, free, within 7 working days, giving immediate remedies to people cheated before or after going <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>. For advice, call the 24-hour migrant call centre, 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

4. <a id="if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-4"></a>**Courts and NGOs.** You can go to court alongside a BMET complaint, but many workers can't keep a case going without money or legal aid <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>; with NGO legal help, some have sued agents or brokers under the 2013 Act <sup>[N244 (2024)](#n244)</sup>, which since a 2024 amendment also covers sub-agents and other intermediaries <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>.

   You'll need: the same papers as for the BMET complaint <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>.

   - Free legal advice: the government legal aid helpline, 16699 (+8809612316699 from abroad), run by NLASO (National Legal Aid Services Organization), which has a legal aid office in each district <sup>[106](#o106), [107](#o107)</sup>.
   - A village court can order money owed under a contract, receipt or other document repaid, up to Tk 3 lakh <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>.
   - Migrant NGOs such as WARBE (Welfare Association for the Rights of Bangladeshi Emigrants) and the Ovibashi Odhikar Forum settle some disputes by village mediation and pass agency cases to BMET <sup>[N244 (2024)](#n244)</sup>; WARBE does not take fraud by local sub-agents <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which court and case type does a worker use against an agency or broker?
   > - What does a court case against an agency or broker cost?
   > - Where does a worker file at the village court?
   > - What fee does the village court charge?
   > - How does a worker contact WARBE?
   > - How does a worker contact the Ovibashi Odhikar Forum?

<a id="step-2-medical-check-in-bangladesh-bio-medical-once-your-vdr-is-approved"></a>

### 3.3 Step 2: Medical check in Bangladesh (Bio-Medical), once your VDR is approved

**Your visa approval comes first.** [Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr) is the employer's work while you wait; your next action is this medical, once the VDR is approved. Under the Ministry's 28 October 2026 instructions, which apply until further notice, the agency brings your VDR visa papers ([Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr)) to the medical centre <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. FWCMS's undated page says the Bio-Medical must come before the VDR application <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>; for workers leaving Bangladesh, follow the newer Ministry order. Hand over no passport or money for a medical before you have seen the VDR papers ([Step 1, item 5: Or through a relative or friend in Malaysia](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5)) <sup>[N292](#n292), [12](#o12)</sup>.

**Which centres are approved, in date order:**

- **28 August 2026:** the Ministry said Bangladesh had asked to change the approved medical centres, a joint delegation of Malaysia's health, human resources and home ministries had inspected every centre in Bangladesh, and Malaysia had decided to start with the remaining 16 centres on its existing list after dropping, at Bangladesh's request, controversial centres linked to the previous government <sup>[N171](#n171)</sup>.
- **15 October 2026:** the Ministry said medical checks would start through the centres listed on Malaysia's website, that a joint Malaysian delegation would give final approval after inspecting each centre, and that any new centre added would be published on that website <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>.
- **4 November 2026:** no medical centre was approved for the restart; the minister said the list of centres was still with Malaysia for vetting, and that centres then testing Malaysia-bound workers were doing so illegally <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>.
- **19 November 2026:** the Ministry said workers had started Malaysia medical tests and 51 medical centres had been approved from Malaysia to run them <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.

In October 2026 the Ministry advised workers not to do a medical until it issued formal instructions <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>; by 19 November 2026, the Ministry said, 51 medical centres had been approved by Malaysia and workers had started their tests <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>. A medical done before 31 October 2026 is not accepted <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where is the list of the 51 centres approved by 19 November 2026 published, and is it the Ministry's list that the 4 November instruction said to wait for <sup>[N19](#n19), [N3](#n3)</sup>?

FWCMS lists accredited medical centres in Bangladesh that do the "Bio-Medical" examination, and says the Bio-Medical is mandatory before an eVDR application can proceed <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. The Ministry's 28 October 2026 instructions speak of a medical test at an approved medical centre on the Ministry's list <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the Ministry's medical test the same as FWCMS's Bio-Medical, done once at one centre, or are they two checks?

1. <a id="step-2-medical-check-in-bangladesh-bio-medical-once-your-vdr-is-approved-item-1"></a>**Check the centre is approved:**

   1. Check the Ministry's list of approved centres on the Ministry's website <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. It is published after Malaysia approves the centres (Which centres are approved, above) <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>; any new centre is to be published on Malaysia's website <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>.
   2. At the door, look for the large, visible billboard every approved centre must put up at its entrance <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
   3. You can also look the centre up at [fwcms.com.my/affiliates](https://fwcms.com.my/affiliates/), under Accredited Medical Centres, Bangladesh (name and address of each) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
   4. You may use any approved centre, not only the one the agency names <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>.

   A centre that breaks the Ministry's instructions is removed from the list <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. Under the 28 October 2026 instructions a centre that disobeys may have its operations shut, and an agency may have its overseas recruitment suspended and its Ministry approval cancelled <sup>[N302](#n302)</sup>. Under the 19 November 2026 instructions a centre that breaks the conditions forfeits its security deposit and loses its licence, and a Ministry monitoring team oversees the centres <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.

   The FWCMS list alone is not enough: Human Diagnostic and Medical Centre, Box Culvert Road, Paltan, Dhaka, was still on it in November 2026 after a mobile court sealed it on 1 October for testing Malaysia-bound workers without approval <sup>[26](#o26), [N303](#n303), [N304](#n304)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - At what page address on the Ministry's website will the list of approved centres appear?
   > - What must the billboard at an approved centre's entrance show <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>?

2. <a id="step-2-medical-check-in-bangladesh-bio-medical-once-your-vdr-is-approved-item-2"></a>**Get the medical slip from your agency and test.** Your agency makes your MiGRAMS slip; no fee may be charged for it <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. A centre may issue medical slips only for the number of workers in the Malaysian employer's demand letter plus at most 5%, and may not issue more on its own or to send extra workers <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>. The agency gives the centre your papers and VDR visa papers ([Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr)) <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[12](#o12), [32](#o32)</sup>:

   - the medical slip and papers from your agency;
   - Tk 8,000, the fee for general workers.

   1. Pay Tk 8,000, no more, and keep the receipt for any complaint <sup>[32](#o32), [12](#o12)</sup>. Under the 19 November 2026 instructions the centre must charge nothing above Tk 8,000 and nothing in the name of MiGRAMS, must not deliberately declare a worker unfit to make him test again, must not make or use fake reports, and must report each day's tests online to the Ministry with each worker's name, passport number and visa number <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
   2. If you pass, your agency applies for your BMET clearance ([Step 6, item 3: BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-3)) <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. If you move to another agency, the first must send your medical report on without delay <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. Under the 19 November 2026 instructions, if a worker declared fit in Bangladesh is found unfit in Malaysia before being employed, the medical centre must bear all his related costs, including sending him home <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does the centre ask for your passport?
   > - How do you check a MiGRAMS slip is real?
   > - When FOMEMA finds a worker unfit whom a Bangladesh centre passed, who pays the fare home: the medical centre, as the 19 November 2026 instructions say <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>, or the employer, as [After you arrive, item 2: FOMEMA medical within 30 days](#after-you-arrive-item-2) says <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>?

   **Scams:** brokers and dishonest agencies offer unauthorised medicals to get passports and money, and have charged for medicals for jobs that never came <sup>[12](#o12), [N207](#n207)</sup>. Hand over nothing until the centre is on the Ministry's approved list, and test only after the agency gives you a MiGRAMS slip for a real job <sup>[12](#o12), [26](#o26), [32](#o32)</sup>.

   **If a centre overcharges, harasses you or tests without approval, or a broker took your passport or money for a medical:**

   1. Email the Ministry at secretary@probashi.gov.bd with the date, place, names of the people or centre, and evidence (receipt, photos, messages) <sup>[12](#o12), [32](#o32)</sup>. For example: "To the Secretary, Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment. On [date], [centre, address] charged me Tk [amount] for my Malaysia medical, against the fixed Tk 8,000. My agency is [name, RL number]. Receipt attached. Please take action and return the extra money. [Name, phone, NID]." <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>
   2. Send the same to the Ministry's enforcement branch, sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255, with your full address and the agency's RL number and address; free, action within 3 to 5 working days, settled within 30, and it also runs mobile courts ([Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money", route 3) <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.
   3. If an agency is involved, also complain to BMET as in [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money" <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
   4. If a broker kept your passport, file an online GD (general diary) at [gd.police.gov.bd](https://gd.police.gov.bd/) ([Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates)) <sup>[108](#o108)</sup>.
   5. Call the 24-hour migrant call centre, 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr"></a>

### 3.4 Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)

1. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-1"></a>**The employer applies; you wait in Bangladesh.** After quota approval and paying the levy, the employer applies online through FWCMS for your VDR (Visa Dengan Rujukan, visa with reference; FWCMS calls it the Calling Visa) with the Home Ministry approval letter, levy receipt, a security deposit or guarantee, and your passport copy and photo; you stay in Bangladesh until it is approved <sup>[9](#o9), [5](#o5), [23](#o23)</sup>. The deposit, visa fee, pass and levy are the employer's costs, but some deduct the levy from wages, often secretly <sup>[9](#o9), [N218](#n218)</sup>.

   **If the levy is taken from your wages,** use the routes under If you aren't paid:

   - the Labour Department's Working for Workers (WFW) app, 03-8000 8000 or jtksm@mohr.gov.my <sup>[109](#o109), [110](#o110), [101 (2017)](#o101)</sup>;
   - the High Commission's labour wing <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>;
   - the migrant call centre, 16135 <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-2"></a>**Track the approval.** You'll need the employer's company registration number (or an individual employer's Malaysian identity card number) and the application number of the employer's VDR application <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>. On IMI's [MyIMMs eServices](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main?pra) choose "Status Permohonan Pembantu Rumah Asing / Pekerja Asing" and enter both <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>:

   - BARU: processing;
   - LULUS: approved;
   - BAYAR: paid;
   - CETAK: visa sticker printed;
   - TOLAK: rejected;
   - BATAL: cancelled;
   - TANGGUH: deferred.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Who gives the worker the VDR application number?

   A complete eVDR (online VDR) should be approved within 5 working days for Peninsular Malaysia, 14 for Sabah, 30 for Sarawak <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

   **If the VDR is refused:** an agency that fails to send workers gets no further Ministry permissions until it repays them <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>; claim your money back through BMET and the Ministry as in [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money" <sup>[1](#o1), [104](#o104)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-3"></a>**After IMI approves, the visa itself is issued in Dhaka.** IMI makes the decision; the High Commission of Malaysia in Dhaka then issues the VDR entry visa on that approval <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>. Worker visa applications go to the High Commission's authorised visa application agencies, not to the High Commission itself <sup>[61](#o61), [51](#o51)</sup>. The Tk 600 visa fee and the RM105 (about Tk 2,500) processing fee are the employer's costs under IMI's rules and the Bangladesh-Malaysia MoU <sup>[51](#o51), [61](#o61), [9](#o9), [10 (2022)](#o10)</sup>. The visa agency adds its own charge, set at its discretion <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Who pays the visa agency's charge?
   > - Which agencies handle worker visas?

   To ask, contact the visa section: dhaka@imi.gov.my, +88 02 4108 1918, House 19, Road 6, Baridhara Diplomatic Enclave, Dhaka, Sunday to Thursday, 8.30 to 4.30 <sup>[61](#o61), [112](#o112)</sup>.

<a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates"></a>

### 3.5 Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates

Get the passport before [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job): the BOESL application ([Step 1, item 3: Apply for government (BOESL) jobs](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)) and the VDR application ([Step 3, item 1: The employer applies; you wait in Bangladesh](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-1)) need it <sup>[8](#o8), [9](#o9)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1"></a>**Get an e-passport valid long enough:** at least 18 to 24 months left <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>; the work pass can be renewed only while your passport has more than 12 months left <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>:

   - your original NID (compulsory over age 20);
   - for some jobs, technical certificates such as a driving licence.

   1. On [epassport.gov.bd](https://www.epassport.gov.bd/) choose your present-address district and police station to get your Regional Passport Office, and fill in the form exactly as on your NID; nothing needs attesting <sup>[114](#o114), [113](#o113)</sup>. A Bangladeshi abroad can correct the age on a reissued passport by at most 8 years, on the basis of the birth registration <sup>[N305](#n305)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Do brokers alter ages or names on passports for Malaysia jobs?
      > - What does an altered age or name do to PLKS renewal?
      > - What does an altered age or name do to EPF withdrawal?

   2. Pay only through A-Challan, DGePay or ShurjoPay (card, bKash, Nagad or Rocket), or at a bank through A-Challan <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>. A 48-page passport costs <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>:

      - 5 years, regular: Tk 4,025;
      - 5 years, express: Tk 6,325;
      - 5 years, super express: Tk 8,625;
      - 10 years, regular: Tk 5,750;
      - 10 years, express: Tk 8,050;
      - 10 years, super express: Tk 10,350.

   3. Enrol (photo and fingerprints) at that office with the printed appointment summary and form, your original NID, and the bank slip if you paid at a bank <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>.
   4. With an online-verified NID there is no police verification; delivery takes up to 15, 7 or 2 working days from enrolment (regular, express, super express) <sup>[116](#o116), [67](#o67)</sup>.

   **To complain:**

   1. Passport helpline 16445 (09666716445 from abroad, 24 hours) <sup>[117 (2023)](#o117)</sup>.
   2. Then the office's grievance redress officer, listed on its website (for example the Dhaka Secretariat office: 01538855234, rpo_secretariat@passport.gov.bd) <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.
   3. Then [grs.gov.bd](https://www.grs.gov.bd/) <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.

   **If it is lost or stolen:**

   1. File a GD quickly at the nearest police station <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>, or at [gd.police.gov.bd](https://gd.police.gov.bd/) or the Online GD app with your NID number, a mobile number (for an SMS code) and a live photo, entering what was lost, the district, the police station, and the time and place <sup>[108](#o108)</sup>. You get a digital GD copy with its number and the investigating officer's details (help: gd@police.gov.bd, 01320001428 on WhatsApp) <sup>[108](#o108)</sup>.
   2. Re-apply with copies of the old passport and the GD <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2"></a>**Police clearance only if asked;** it is not on IMI's VDR list or BMET's clearance lists <sup>[9](#o9), [30](#o30), [1](#o1)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do Malaysian employers or agencies still ask for a police clearance?

   If asked, you'll need <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>:

   - a passport valid at least 3 months;
   - a present address that is one of your passport's addresses;
   - a scan of the passport data page attested by a first-class gazetted officer.

   1. Apply at [pcc.police.gov.bd](https://pcc.police.gov.bd/) <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.
   2. Pay Tk 1,500 by treasury challan, code 1-7301-0001-2681, at any Bangladesh Bank or Sonali Bank branch, or online by card with a service charge <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.
   3. The police verify you at your present address; track it by texting PCC S and your reference number to 26969 <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.
   4. Collect it with a copy of your passport or application, or, if you are abroad, through someone with an attested authorisation letter giving their name and NID number <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.

   Helpdesk: Sunday to Thursday, 9 am to 5 pm, 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-3"></a>**Certificates,** each where it is asked for: the NSDA skills certificate from a TTC course ([Step 1, item 2: Learn a trade if you need one](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-2)), original education and experience certificates for a BOESL interview ([Step 1, item 3: Apply for government (BOESL) jobs](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)), and skill certificates for BMET clearance ([Step 6, item 3: BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-3)) <sup>[78](#o78), [8](#o8), [1](#o1)</sup>.
