<a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself"></a>

### 3.6 Step 5: Check the visa yourself

Before you fly, make three checks; after you land, one more.

1. <a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-1"></a>**Visa approval on MyIMMs shows LULUS.** You need the employer's company registration number and the application number; how to check is in [Step 3, item 2: Track the approval](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-2) <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-2"></a>**BMET checks the visa by hand.** Take the visa (with a copy of the passport page) or the demand letter to BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000 (02-49357972), or your DEMO; it is free and takes 3 working days ([Step 1, item 6: Find and check the permission letter for your job](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6)). BMET's online visa check doesn't cover Malaysia <sup>[21](#o21), [1](#o1), [99 (2021)](#o99)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-3"></a>**Your BMET clearance ([Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight))** shows at [portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check) <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>.

   The agency checks (FWCMS list and OEP status) are in [Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4) <sup>[26](#o26), [85](#o85)</sup>, and the permission letter in [Step 1, item 6: Find and check the permission letter for your job](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6) <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>. If you can't get a check done, ask the High Commission of Malaysia's visa section in Dhaka whether the visa is real ([Step 3, item 3: After IMI approves, the visa itself is issued in Dhaka](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-3)) <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-4"></a>**After arrival:** IMI's [ePass check](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/checkEpassValid?semakan) shows name, nationality, pass type and expiry when you enter the ePass number (like EP141011C23105313148), passport number and nationality; "Pas Tidak Sah" means not valid <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Where does a worker find their ePass number?

**If any check shows a fake visa, pass, job or clearance:** stop paying and keep your passport <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. Then:

- complain to BMET and the Ministry's enforcement branch as in [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money", with your passport copy, contract, visa, messages with the agency and proof of payment <sup>[1](#o1), [N295](#n295), [104](#o104)</sup>;
- ask the High Commission of Malaysia's visa section whether the visa is real ([Step 3, item 3: After IMI approves, the visa itself is issued in Dhaka](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-3)) <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>;
- report whoever took your money or passport through the online GD ([Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates)) <sup>[108](#o108)</sup>;
- call 16135 <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

<a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight"></a>

### 3.7 Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight

1. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-1"></a>**Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO)** at a BMET training institute (list with contacts on [BMET's website](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922dd2c933eb65569e13cd6)) <sup>[1](#o1), [76](#o76), [77](#o77)</sup>. You'll need your OEP registration and passport or NID number <sup>[21](#o21), [121](#o121)</sup>.

   1. Apply on OEP, then download the enrollment card at [training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-enrollment-card](https://training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-enrollment-card) with your passport or NID number and the captcha <sup>[21](#o21), [121](#o121)</sup>.
   2. Attend all 3 days and pay against a money receipt (Tk 200 at Feni Technical Training Centre, which says to pay no more) <sup>[1](#o1), [69](#o69)</sup>.
   3. Download your certificate at [training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate](https://training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate) with your passport or NID number; it is the only real one <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-2"></a>**Check the contract before clearance** (the agency must give you it and a BMET-attested Bangla translation: [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying") <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>. Get it at least 2 days before you go and check <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>:

   - the job, employer and address, and workplace;
   - length;
   - monthly pay, hours, overtime and leave;
   - airfare;
   - medical care;
   - injury and death compensation;
   - food and housing;
   - return of the body.

   **If there is no contract or translation, or it differs from your permission letter:** don't go to clearance. Then:

   - complain to BMET and the Ministry as in [Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself) <sup>[1](#o1), [104](#o104)</sup>;
   - call 16135 <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-3"></a>**BMET clearance (the smart card).** After the visa, the agency applies with, for Malaysia, the demand letter, power of attorney and skill certificates, for workers from BMET's database only; BMET's emigration branch clears within 3 working days <sup>[1](#o1), [30](#o30)</sup>. Your costs <sup>[10 (2022)](#o10)</sup>, paid only through the proper channel against a receipt <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>:

   - Tk 5,500 to the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (the government's welfare board for migrant workers) <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>;
   - a one-time Tk 1,000 insurance premium (5 years' cover, Tk 10 lakh) <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>; the Welfare Board's director general said in May 2024 that it is paid in the name of zero-cost BOESL workers too, giving them the five years' cover and the Board's other help <sup>[N74 (2024)](#n74)</sup>;
   - a Tk 250 clearance fee <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>;
   - source tax of Tk 800 (unskilled) or Tk 1,200 (skilled) on a group visa <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does the Tk 250 clearance fee still apply, given the Ministry reportedly abolished the smart card fee in December 2024 <sup>[N306 (2024)](#n306)</sup>?

   **If you got your visa yourself,** apply at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram DEMO with <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>:

   - an application;
   - the visa copy;
   - any contract;
   - an attested copy of your OEP card;
   - a declaration on Tk 300 stamp paper;
   - the original pay order for the welfare fee and insurance.

   **Check it is real** at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check; airport welfare desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet stop workers with no or a fake smart card <sup>[119](#o119), [1](#o1), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-4"></a>**The ticket.** Under the current MoU and permission letters the employer pays your air fare both ways, and BMET clears you only once that is ensured; an agency representative must fly with you or meet you in Malaysia <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29), [40 (2022)](#o40), [30](#o30)</sup>. Whether zero-cost BOESL workers pay airfare is disputed (see [Status](#status)) <sup>[14](#o14), [8](#o8)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported in May 2024 that in normal times the cheapest Dhaka-Kuala Lumpur ticket cost Tk 24,000 to 25,000, against Tk 14,000 to 15,000 from Kolkata, and that the flight from Dhaka takes 3 hours 55 minutes <sup>[N106 (2024)](#n106)</sup>; for Biman's later migrant fare, see Going home on leave and coming back <sup>[N307](#n307)</sup>.

   **Check the booking is real:** agencies have handed workers fake tickets minutes before the flight <sup>[N308 (2024)](#n308)</sup>. Enter the six-character PNR (booking reference) from your e-ticket on the airline's site:

   - Biman: Manage Booking, with your last name <sup>[N309](#n309)</sup>;
   - Malaysia Airlines: [Manage Booking](https://www.malaysiaairlines.com/ph/en/plan-trip/booking-and-services.html), with your last name <sup>[N310](#n310)</sup>;
   - US-Bangla: [Manage Your Booking](https://usbair.com/manage-your-booking), or use the ticket number <sup>[N311](#n311)</sup>;
   - AirAsia: [Check-in](https://www.airasia.com/check-in/en/gb), with your first name or surname <sup>[N312](#n312)</sup>.

   **Scam:** last-minute demands, such as Tk 50,000, for the ticket, which is the employer's cost <sup>[N308 (2024)](#n308), [29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>. **If asked for ticket money or the ticket is fake:** don't pay. Then:

   - complain to BMET and the Ministry as in [Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself) with the ticket and agency details <sup>[1](#o1), [104](#o104)</sup>;
   - call 16135 <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

5. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-5"></a>**Before you fly,** carry:

   - your passport, contract, visa, BMET clearance, medical report, ticket and receipts <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>;
   - the Bangladesh High Commission's contacts: No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur; 03-2604 0946, 48 or 49; labour wing +60 3 2604 0934 <sup>[125](#o125), [111](#o111)</sup>.

   Leave one set of copies at home and carry 3, and have a bank account in your name and one in a family member's <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. Keep money and papers in your cabin bag and the checked bag within 20 kg <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>. Never carry a packet whose contents you don't know <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

6. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-6"></a>**Register the free MDAC (Malaysia Digital Arrival Card)** within 3 days before arrival (the day you submit counts) at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main); workers on a VDR are not exempt, and fake MDAC sites charge for it <sup>[4](#o4), [126](#o126), [N313](#n313), [127 (2024)](#o127), [128](#o128), [129](#o129)</sup>. Fill it in, check it and show it as in [Tourist visa, Step 4: Fill in the Malaysia Digital Arrival Card (MDAC)](#step-4-fill-in-the-malaysia-digital-arrival-card-mdac).

<a id="scams"></a>

### 3.8 Scams

<a id="done-to-workers"></a>

#### 3.8.1 Done to workers

The main scams, each covered where it happens:

- overcharging and ghost jobs ([Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4); [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying") <sup>[N284 (2024)](#n284), [N11](#n11)</sup>;
- A-to-B and free visas, resale on arrival, and passports held for ransom ([Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "The trap") <sup>[N293](#n293), [N297 (2024)](#n297)</sup>;
- unauthorised medicals that take passports and money ([Step 2: Medical check in Bangladesh (Bio-Medical), once your VDR is approved](#step-2-medical-check-in-bangladesh-bio-medical-once-your-vdr-is-approved)) <sup>[12](#o12), [N207](#n207)</sup>;
- fake "zero-cost" or BOESL places: BOESL has no agents and takes no cash ([Step 1, item 3: Apply for government (BOESL) jobs](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)) <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>;
- fake tickets and last-minute ticket money ([Step 6, item 4: The ticket](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-4)) <sup>[N308 (2024)](#n308)</sup>.

Complain in Bangladesh as under [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money", and in Malaysia to the labour wing by email <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. Other scams:

- **Social media posts on Malaysia-approved agencies** have been false <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>; check the FWCMS list instead ([Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4)) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
- **The fee video.** Agencies make workers record a video at their Dhaka office saying they paid only the official fee when they paid far more <sup>[N206](#n206)</sup>; keep your own proof of every payment <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>.
- **Fake passes and permit "agents".** Rings sell fake e-PLKS (electronic work pass) copies for about RM150 and fake police reports for about RM70 <sup>[N277](#n277)</sup>; a Bangladeshi-run ring in Kulim, Kedah, sold fake work permits and fake UNHCR cards for RM80 to 250 <sup>[N314](#n314)</sup>. Only your employer deals with your pass; anyone else offering is breaking the law <sup>[90 (2021)](#o90), [9](#o9)</sup>. Check it yourself ([Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself)) <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>.
- **Sabah jobs.** There is no agreement for recruiting Bangladeshis to Sabah; rings have taken passports and cash for Sabah jobs <sup>[N77](#n77)</sup>.
- **Trafficking rings inside Malaysia.** Malaysia's Immigration Department said that in a coordinated operation at three places in Cheras, Kuala Lumpur, on the night of 11 December 2024 it rescued 11 Bangladeshis aged 30 to 38 from a trafficking ring believed to be led by three Bangladeshis, and arrested three main suspects aged 35 to 38 and a 32-year-old Bangladeshi woman with one of them <sup>[N315 (2024)](#n315)</sup>. It said it seized 17 Bangladeshi passports, 20 mobile phones, RM800 and USD 800 in cash and two cars, that the victims had been trafficked and exploited by the syndicate for financial gain, and that the rescued were in its care while the investigation continued; its Director General, Datuk Zakaria Shaban, confirmed the rescue of the 11 <sup>[N315 (2024)](#n315)</sup>.
- **"Free" Malaysia offers that end at the sea.** In November 2026 a young man from Feni was added to a WhatsApp group offering to take him to Malaysia free of cost, paying only after arrival, and ended up held by traffickers in the Teknaf hills awaiting a boat, he told Prothom Alo <sup>[N316](#n316)</sup>.
- **No job on arrival.** Employers sign a High Commission commitment to put workers to work at once; if broken, email the labour wing ([After you arrive, item 1: Wait for your employer at the airport](#after-you-arrive-item-1)) <sup>[25 (2023)](#o25), [130](#o130)</sup>.

<a id="tried-by-workers"></a>

#### 3.8.2 Tried by workers

Forged visas, passes and passports carry jail and caning (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)), including passports bought to resit a failed FOMEMA medical <sup>[55](#o55), [N317](#n317), [N318](#n318)</sup>.

<a id="after-you-arrive"></a>

### 3.9 After you arrive

1. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-1"></a>**Wait for your employer at the airport.** Enter only through official entry points with your VDR and visa; the employer must collect you within 6 hours, take you to the hostel and pay your transport to the workplace <sup>[5](#o5), [131 (2024)](#o131), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. **If no one comes or there is no job:**

   1. Call the agency representative who must meet the flight <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>.
   2. Then the High Commission's labour wing (+603 2604 0934, ministermalaysia@probashi.gov.bd, or +601 2290 3252, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd) <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>.

   Also call 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

   **If there is no job.** Don't take other work, which ends the pass (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>; file at the Labour Office ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 3) and get free help (see Free help in Malaysia). In one case of 161 Bangladeshis left without work or pay for seven months, the Labour Department told the employer to pay wages owed since arrival and either send the workers home or help them find new employers <sup>[N143 (2024)](#n143)</sup>. In December 2023, 171 Bangladeshis were arrested in Kota Tinggi, Johor, when they went to the police to report their agent <sup>[N140 (2023)](#n140)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can a worker whose pass was never issued report to the police without being arrested?

2. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-2"></a>**FOMEMA medical within 30 days.** FOMEMA appoints the clinics for foreign workers' medical checks <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. The employer must take you to a FOMEMA clinic within 30 days of arrival and pay the fee (RM207 for men, RM217 for women (which includes a pregnancy test), plus 8% sales and service tax) <sup>[5](#o5), [37 (2022)](#o37), [132](#o132)</sup>. It includes a chest X-ray, blood tests and a urine drug test for opiates, methamphetamine and cannabis; results usually come within 7 working days <sup>[133](#o133), [134](#o134)</sup>. Check yours on IMI's [FOMEMA Application Status](https://eservices.imi.gov.my/myimms/FomemaStatus?lang=en) with your passport number and BANGLADESH <sup>[135](#o135)</sup>; the employer checks it on the FOMEMA portal, and neither of you sees the report itself <sup>[136](#o136), [N227](#n227)</sup>.

   **If the employer hasn't taken you within 30 days:** without a FOMEMA fit result there is no work pass (PLKS) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Then:

   - complain to the Labour Department (WFW app or 03-8000 8000) <sup>[109](#o109), [110](#o110), [101 (2017)](#o101)</sup>;
   - tell the labour wing ([After you arrive, item 1: Wait for your employer at the airport](#after-you-arrive-item-1)) <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>;
   - call 16135 <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

   **If you are found unfit:** no PLKS. You are sent home with a Check-Out Memo (the employer's application for a foreign worker to leave), and the employer pays the fare <sup>[5](#o5), [9](#o9), [29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>. Only the employer or agency can appeal, for a fee, on portal.fomema.my within 2 weeks; the case closes if extra test results don't arrive within 4 weeks <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>. Confirmed tuberculosis, malaria, pregnancy, syphilis, HIV and hepatitis B can't be appealed <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>. Ask the employer in writing to appeal, and tell the labour wing <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>. Using a false passport to sit FOMEMA again carries 2 to 10 years' jail (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>.

3. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-3"></a>**PLKS sticker and i-Kad.** The sticker is printed once IMI approves your PLKS and the employer pays (CETAK on MyIMMs) <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>. Every PLKS holder gets an i-Kad (foreign worker identity card), free and without applying; the employer collects it from Immigration 7 days after IMI's email <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. Carry it always and show it when asked <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. If you don't get it, complain to the Labour Department (WFW app or 03-8000 8000), then the labour wing <sup>[109](#o109), [110](#o110), [101 (2017)](#o101), [111](#o111)</sup>.

4. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-4"></a>**Keep your own passport.** Your employer may not keep your passport; hand it over only for the medical, PLKS, i-Kad and renewal, and get it back <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. Holding someone else's passport without lawful authority carries a fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both <sup>[44 (1997)](#o44), [138](#o138)</sup>. **If your employer keeps it:**

   1. Ask for it back in writing and keep a copy <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>.
   2. Complain to the Labour Department (WFW app, 03-8000 8000 or jtksm@mohr.gov.my) <sup>[109](#o109), [110](#o110), [101 (2017)](#o101)</sup>.
   3. Tell the labour wing <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>.

   For unpaid wages, see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid).

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does a report to the Malaysian police or IMI get a passport back?
   > - Where does a worker make that report?

5. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-5"></a>**Wages into your own bank account** (the rules: Your rights at work, Pay) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. The employer is expected to open it <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>. At Maybank, for example, open it at a branch (the app needs a Malaysian identity card) with your passport and at least RM250, and perhaps your work permit, visa and an employer's letter <sup>[139](#o139)</sup>.

6. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-6"></a>**SIM card.** Register every prepaid SIM yourself, in person, at an operator's service centre or authorised dealer with your original passport and work-permit document <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>. The limit is 2 prepaid SIMs per operator; activation is confirmed within 12 hours <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>. Don't use a SIM that was active before you registered it <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>. Don't give documents or codes to anyone offering to "help"; each operator checks free which numbers are in your name <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>.

7. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-7"></a>**Social security, insurance and the retirement fund.** The employer must register you with PERKESO (SOCSO, Malaysia's social security organisation) for the Employment Injury Scheme (accidents, work diseases, death at work; employer pays 1.25% of wages) and the Invalidity Scheme (critical illness or death in Malaysia before 60 from any cause; employer 0.5%, you 0.5%) <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>. You get a 12-digit social security number; SOCSO: 1-300-22-8000 <sup>[141 (2019)](#o141), [142 (2022)](#o142)</sup>. Not registering or paying is an offence for the employer <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>.

   SPIKPA, the compulsory hospital and surgery insurance, covers admission and surgery at non-corporatised government hospitals and costs RM120 a year; the employer buys it with the PLKS application and may deduct it from wages with Labour Department permission, and cover starts once you are registered and it is paid <sup>[143](#o143), [N319](#n319), [144](#o144)</sup>. At a government hospital show your valid passport; you pay no deposit and only the part of a bill above the cover limit <sup>[144](#o144)</sup>. An unpaid government hospital bill blocks your PLKS renewal <sup>[144](#o144)</sup>.

   EPF (Employees Provident Fund, the retirement fund; 2% from you and 2% from the employer, Your rights at work) registers you automatically; visit any EPF office, no appointment, to update your details and give your thumbprint <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>. When you leave for good, withdraw your savings before you fly ([Leaving at the end of the job, item 4: Withdraw your EPF before you fly](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-4)) <sup>[145](#o145)</sup>.

8. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-8"></a>**If you came without BMET clearance,** join the Wage Earners' Welfare Board for its insurance and help: apply at [portal.wewb.gov.bd](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/) with your passport and work permit; the labour wing processes it, for the ringgit equivalent of Tk 6,500, life insurance included <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. Workers with BMET clearance are already covered (check at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check) <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>.

<a id="your-rights-at-work"></a>

### 3.10 Your rights at work

The rights in this section are Peninsular Malaysia's and Labuan's; Sabah and Sarawak have their own Labour Ordinances <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. For any broken right, use the routes under If you aren't paid.

<a id="contract"></a>

#### 3.10.1 Contract

You are entitled to your own copy of the contract from the employer named on your work permit <sup>[37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. The employer or its agent must explain the terms when you are selected, and the contract you sign must carry the same terms <sup>[37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. Under the Bangladesh-Malaysia agreement the contract is in Malay, Bangla and English, and the English counts <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. Your terms may be no worse than the Employment Act 1955 (the main labour law in Peninsular Malaysia): a worse term is void and replaced by the Act, and no contract may stop you joining a registered trade union <sup>[37 (2022)](#o37), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

<a id="pay-and-deductions"></a>

#### 3.10.2 Pay and deductions

**Pay**

At least RM1,700 a month or RM8.72 an hour (by the day: RM65.38 on a 6-day week, RM78.46 on 5 days, RM98.08 on 4), the same as a local worker for the same job <sup>[11 (2024)](#o11), [40 (2022)](#o40), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. The minimum applies to every employer, including for piece, task, trip or commission pay <sup>[11 (2024)](#o11)</sup>. Wages are paid monthly by the 7th of the next month into your own bank account ([After you arrive, item 5: Wages into your own bank account](#after-you-arrive-item-5)); overtime, rest-day and holiday pay by the end of the next wage period <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. The employer may not dictate where, how or with whom you spend it <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. It is to rise to RM2,000 a month from June 2027, with micro, small and medium businesses selling under RM50 million a year exempt <sup>[146](#o146)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the Minimum Wages Order setting RM2,000 been made?

**Deductions**

Only what the Employment Act allows <sup>[147](#o147), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Rent, food or payments to anyone else need your written request and Labour Department approval, given for rent and food only if they benefit you <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. All deductions together may not exceed half a month's wages <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34), [40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. In Sarawak nothing may be deducted for recruitment or repatriation, and foreign workers get local workers' rate <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>.

<a id="hours-rest-and-leave"></a>

#### 3.10.3 Hours, rest and leave

**Hours and overtime**

At most 8 hours a day and 45 a week, no more than 5 hours in a row without a 30-minute break, and never over 12 hours a day except in emergencies <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34), [148 (2022)](#o148)</sup>. Overtime is only at the employer's request with your agreement, paid at least 1.5 times the hourly rate (for monthly pay: the monthly wage divided by 26, then by your normal daily hours) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

**Rest days and holidays**

One rest day a week, on which you can't be made to work except in continuous shift work or emergencies <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Work on it within normal hours pays, on monthly pay, half a day's pay for up to half the normal hours and a full day's for more, on top of the monthly wage, and on daily pay one or two days' wages; beyond normal hours, at least 2 times the hourly rate <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. 11 paid public holidays a year; working one earns 2 extra days' wages, and overtime that day at least 3 times the hourly rate <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Absence without consent on the working day before or after a holiday loses that holiday's pay unless you have a reasonable excuse <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

**Eid in practice**

Migrants told Prothom Alo in June 2024 that a large share of workers in Malaysia get no holiday even on Eid day and go to work instead of to Eid prayers; Md Raihan Kashem, six to seven years in Malaysia and working at a supershop in Malacca, said he gets no leave on Eid and has never in any year been given time to pray <sup>[N148 (2024)](#n148)</sup>. On Eid day 2024 a migrant, Iftekhar Ahmed, wrote in the Facebook group "Malaysia Kajer Khobor" (Malaysia work news) that he was going to duty from 10 am to 10 pm and had been refused a single day off, and another, Md Ashraful Ahmed, replied that he worked from 6 am to 8 pm <sup>[N148 (2024)](#n148)</sup>.

**Leave**

Paid annual leave is 8, 12 or 16 days a year (under 2, 2 to 5, over 5 years' service) <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>; paid sick leave 14, 18 or 22 days on the same bands, plus up to 60 days if a doctor certifies hospital care <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34), [149 (2024)](#o149)</sup>; it needs a doctor's certificate and notice to the employer within 48 hours, or it counts as absence <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Maternity leave is 98 days <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>. A married man gets 7 days' paid paternity leave per birth, up to 5 births, after 12 months' service, if he tells the employer at least 30 days before the expected birth <sup>[148 (2022)](#o148)</sup>.

<a id="housing-food-and-health-care"></a>

#### 3.10.4 Housing, food and health care

**Housing**

The employer must give you reasonable housing with basic amenities, at the standard of Act 446 (the Employees' Minimum Standards of Housing, Accommodations and Amenities Act 1990) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. It needs a Labour Department Certificate for Accommodation, and men and women live separately <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. Each worker gets his own bed with a 4-inch mattress, pillow, blanket and lockable cupboard <sup>[N214 (2020)](#n214)</sup>, at least 3.6 square metres in a bedroom (3 in a dormitory), and no more than 6 share a bathroom and toilet (15 in a dormitory) <sup>[N214 (2020)](#n214)</sup>. On estates and other workplaces outside city, municipal and Federal Territory areas, employer housing must have free piped water and electricity, and no rent may be charged <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. Elsewhere rent can be taken from wages only within limits set by regulations, at most RM100 a month per worker <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150), [N214 (2020)](#n214)</sup>. Many employers still use hostels that break Act 446, and some leave workers to buy their own beds and kitchen equipment <sup>[N320](#n320)</sup>; the Labour Department can inspect at any time without notice <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. If the job ends with under 4 weeks' notice, you may stay up to 4 weeks <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the RM100 monthly rent limit changed since 2020?

**Food**

Food is usually at your own cost. Bangladesh Ministry permission letters for Malaysia jobs list food as paid by the worker, while housing and medical care are free <sup>[95 (2024)](#o95), [31 (2024)](#o31), [29 (2024)](#o29), [98 (2023)](#o98)</sup>. If the employer does provide food or meals, their cost can be taken from wages only at your written request and with Labour Department approval ([Pay and deductions](#pay-and-deductions)) <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does any Malaysian law or the Bangladesh-Malaysia agreement require the employer to provide food or a food allowance?

**Health care**

The employer must make sure workers get medical help, and the person in charge of employer housing must take a worker who reports illness to a clinic or hospital <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. A medical examination for sick leave is at the employer's expense <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Ministry permission letters list medical care as free <sup>[95 (2024)](#o95), [98 (2023)](#o98)</sup>. Hospital insurance and what you pay at a government hospital are in [After you arrive, item 7: Social security, insurance and the retirement fund](#after-you-arrive-item-7) <sup>[143](#o143), [144](#o144)</sup>.

<a id="your-passport"></a>

#### 3.10.5 Your passport

The employer may not keep it ([After you arrive, item 4: Keep your own passport](#after-you-arrive-item-4)) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [44 (1997)](#o44)</sup>; if it is kept, you can also complain to SUHAKAM (the Human Rights Commission of Malaysia; see Free help in Malaysia) <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>.

<a id="end-of-service-pay"></a>

#### 3.10.6 End-of-service pay

**Termination benefits.** After 12 months, if the employer ends the job (except for misconduct), you get at least 10 days' wages per year of service under 2 years, 15 days for 2 to 5 years, 20 days for 5 or more, pro rata; notice and dismissal rules are in If you resign or are dismissed <sup>[N321](#n321), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

**EPF savings.** EPF is the retirement fund ([After you arrive, item 7: Social security, insurance and the retirement fund](#after-you-arrive-item-7)). You withdraw all of it when you leave for good ([Leaving at the end of the job](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job)); it builds up because the employer pays 2% of your wage and deducts 2% from you, by the 15th of the next month <sup>[35](#o35), [145](#o145)</sup>.

**PERKESO pensions.** PERKESO (social security, [After you arrive, item 7: Social security, insurance and the retirement fund](#after-you-arrive-item-7)) also pays invalidity and survivors' pensions (see [If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job](#if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job)) <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>.

<a id="probash-pension-bangladesh"></a>

#### 3.10.7 Probash pension (Bangladesh)

Bangladesh's Probash pension, part of the Universal Pension Scheme, is open to Bangladeshis aged 18 to 50 working abroad <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>:

- a bank account;
- a mobile number;
- an email address;
- a foreign-currency card.

You can name a nominee, who gets the money if you die <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>.

1. Register yourself, in your own name, at [upension.gov.bd](https://www.upension.gov.bd/) under Probash, with no third person; your ID, rate and payment dates come by SMS and email, and any Sonali Bank branch is the front office <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>.
2. Pay Tk 1,000, 2,000, 5,000, 7,500 or 10,000 a month, 3, 9 or 12 months at a time, by card to Sonali Bank; 2.5% is added from abroad <sup>[153](#o153), [152](#o152)</sup>.

What you get back: after 10 years of unbroken payments, a monthly pension into your bank from age 60 without applying (Tk 1,000 a month for 20 years gives about Tk 4,927 a month); if you die, all you paid plus profit goes to your nominee <sup>[152](#o152), [153](#o153)</sup>. On returning home you can switch to the Progoti or Surokkha scheme <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What details does the Probash pension registration form ask for?
> - Does registering for the Probash pension cost anything?
> - How can a worker pay the Probash pension without a foreign-currency card?
> - What can a worker do if Probash pension registration or payment fails?

<a id="your-residence-permit"></a>

### 3.11 Your residence permit

<a id="how-long-you-can-stay-and-renewing"></a>

#### 3.11.1 How long you can stay and renewing

**12 months at a time, up to 10 years**

The PLKS lasts 12 months <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Only the employer renews it, from 3 months before expiry, and pays any late penalty <sup>[9](#o9), [40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. Renewal needs a passport valid over 12 months, a FOMEMA medical every year, and SPIKPA hospital insurance (except in plantations) <sup>[5](#o5), [134](#o134), [24](#o24)</sup>. A new 48-page, 5-year e-passport from the High Commission in Kuala Lumpur costs a worker RM172 (RM140 fee plus a RM32 ESKL charge for the appointment, enrolment and delivery) <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>; Bangladesh missions charge workers USD 50 for 10 years (express USD 45 for 5 years, USD 75 for 10) <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>. A renewal filed after expiry counts as overstay <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. The limit is 10 years in total, or 3 for workers registered under the 6P legalisation programme <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>.

**Renewing your passport in Malaysia**

- **Late 2024 backlog.** Asif Nazrul, then the interim government's law adviser, said on 20 November 2024 that he hoped delivery of renewed passports to migrant workers in Malaysia and other countries would start from 10 December, that officials had told him foil paper for printing passports was being brought in, and that passport problems affected many Middle Eastern countries too <sup>[N322 (2024)](#n322)</sup>. He said there was no crisis in issuing e-passports, but that migrant workers want MRP (machine-readable) passports rather than e-passports; the MRP is outdated, and the national aim is to move from MRP to e-passports <sup>[N322 (2024)](#n322)</sup>.
- **At the passport office.** A Bangladeshi who had lived 21 years in Malaysia wrote in Prothom Alo in November 2024 that migrants coming for services at the office in charge of e-passports in Malaysia were perhaps being lightly struck by security guards of the private company handling passports while many crowded and pushed; he guessed, without knowing for sure, that the pushing may have been the reason <sup>[N87 (2024)](#n87)</sup>. He wrote that because Bangladesh missions expect migrants to know all the rules and have no time to explain them, many migrants hire brokers and pay extra to avoid harassment over passports, travel passes and document attestation <sup>[N87 (2024)](#n87)</sup>.
- **Complaints about ESKL.** In a 13-point proposal handed to the foreign adviser on 21 May 2025, the AB Party (Amar Bangladesh Party) demanded that passport renewal be completed within seven days with courier delivery, and that the corruption and syndicate "in the name of business" at ESKL in Malaysia be stopped and the service decentralised under the High Commission's control <sup>[N323](#n323)</sup>. The proposal, as reported, does not say what ESKL is.

  > **Need to know**
  > - What is ESKL: the private company that runs passport services for the High Commission in Kuala Lumpur, and where is its office <sup>[N323](#n323)</sup>?

**If the employer doesn't renew,** you risk arrest <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. Check your pass on Immigration's ePass check ([Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself)) <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>; if it is near expiry and unrenewed, email the labour wing and contact Tenaganita ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) <sup>[130](#o130), [N324](#n324)</sup>. In June 2026 about 35 Aerofoam Manufacturing workers who had entered Malaysia legally had no valid PLKS because the company did not renew it on time, leaving them in fear of arrest or fines; their written complaint to the Labour Department (Jabatan Tenaga Kerja, JTK) asked that the 35 workers' visas and permits be regularised quickly <sup>[N325](#n325)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Labour Department act on a complaint about a pass that isn't renewed, and what came of the Aerofoam workers' June 2026 complaint <sup>[N325](#n325)</sup>?
> - Are Bangladeshi workers' renewals still done only at Immigration Putrajaya <sup>[90 (2021)](#o90)</sup> now that work-pass applications go through FWCMS <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>?

<a id="going-home-on-leave-and-coming-back"></a>

#### 3.11.2 Going home on leave and coming back

The yearly PLKS renewal includes a multiple-entry visa for Bangladeshi workers, with no separate application <sup>[N326](#n326)</sup>. Agree your return date with the employer before you go; missing it counts as running away <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24)</sup>.

**How the multiple-entry visa came in.** Asif Nazrul, the interim government's adviser for expatriates' welfare, said on 15 July 2025 that a circular signed on behalf of Malaysia's Immigration Director General and issued on 10 July 2025 gave Bangladeshi workers multiple-entry visas <sup>[N327](#n327)</sup>:

- workers already issued a single-entry visa and a PLKS (temporary employment visit pass) before the circular need not apply for a new multiple-entry visa <sup>[N327](#n327)</sup>;
- the multiple-entry visa is issued automatically when the PLKS is renewed <sup>[N327](#n327)</sup>;
- workers with a single-entry visa and a valid PLKS can travel home and back without a new multiple visa <sup>[N327](#n327)</sup>;
- instructions were sent to all of Malaysia's international airports and land ports <sup>[N327](#n327)</sup>.

He said that of the 15 countries Malaysia takes workers from, only Bangladeshi workers had been given single-entry rather than multiple-entry visas before then, causing them much hardship <sup>[N327](#n327)</sup>. Chief Adviser Muhammad Yunus, head of the interim government, had asked Malaysia's High Commissioner in Dhaka, Mohd Shuhada Othman, at a meeting reported on 13 January 2025, to give Bangladeshi workers multiple-entry visas so that they could go home easily when needed <sup>[N128](#n128)</sup>; on 15 May 2025 Asif Nazrul said he had asked Malaysia's home minister for them, and the minister told his officials to act quickly <sup>[N40](#n40)</sup>. The Ministry's first-year report of August 2025 said a multiple-entry visa had been introduced for Bangladeshi workers in Malaysia for the first time <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>.

**The fare.** In June 2026 the civil aviation minister, Afroza Khanom, told parliament that Biman Bangladesh Airlines had introduced a low-fare "T-class" ticket for migrant workers, bringing the Dhaka-Kuala Lumpur fare down from Tk 28,000 to Tk 21,000 <sup>[N307](#n307)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker check the multiple-entry visa was issued?
> - Is Biman's T-class fare one-way or return, and can a worker buy it from Kuala Lumpur <sup>[N307](#n307)</sup>?

<a id="bringing-your-family"></a>

#### 3.11.3 Bringing your family

**No.** PLKS holders can't bring family, may not marry a Malaysian or another foreigner while on the pass ([Spouse of a Malaysian citizen](#spouse-of-a-malaysian-citizen)), can't apply for permanent residence (PLKS years don't count toward it), and must go home when the pass ends <sup>[5](#o5), [9](#o9), [N328 (2021)](#n328), [N329 (2021)](#n329), [156](#o156)</sup>. For professionals' routes, see [Family passes](#family-passes); for registering a birth <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>, see [Having a child in Malaysia](#having-a-child-in-malaysia).

**Pregnancy and the work pass**

The yearly FOMEMA medical includes a pregnancy test for women <sup>[132](#o132), [134](#o134)</sup>. Confirmed pregnancy means unfit, with no appeal, and the worker is sent home with a Check-Out Memo <sup>[137](#o137), [5](#o5)</sup>. This holds even though a pregnant worker can't be dismissed except for wilful breach of contract, misconduct or closure of the business <sup>[149 (2024)](#o149), [148 (2022)](#o148)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What happens to a worker who becomes pregnant between yearly medicals?

<a id="what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported"></a>

### 3.12 What can get you jailed or deported

<a id="no-valid-pass-or-passport"></a>

#### 3.12.1 No valid pass or passport

Overstaying an expired or cancelled pass: fine of at least RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both, or a compound (out-of-court fine) up to RM3,000 <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39), [46](#o46), [55](#o55)</sup>. Entering illegally, or failing to show a valid passport and pass when asked: fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both, and up to 6 strokes of the cane, or a compound up to RM3,000 <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. Carry your i-Kad at all times <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.

A pass ends when the employer doesn't renew it ([Your residence permit](#your-residence-permit)), when Immigration cancels it after the employer reports you absent (If your employer reports you absent (lari)), or at once when you break a pass condition, such as working for anyone but the employer named on the pass <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39), [N229 (2021)](#n229), [5](#o5)</sup>. PLKS holders may not work as frontliners (customer-facing staff) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

**Raids and detentions, in date order:**

- **August 2025:** Malaysia's Home Ministry said 790 Bangladeshis who had overstayed their visas or whose work permits had expired had been detained <sup>[N81](#n81)</sup>.
- **20 November 2025:** Immigration raided commercial areas, construction sites and vegetable farms in four areas of Cameron Highlands, Pahang, checked 1,886 foreigners and arrested 468, 174 of them Bangladeshi, for offences such as expired passports and no travel documents; some showed temporary work permits that appeared fake, Immigration's Director General, Zakaria Shaaban, said <sup>[N330](#n330)</sup>. He said intelligence showed Cameron Highlands had become an attractive work centre for foreigners, drawn by its hills and distance from towns, that local businesses often prefer to hire them, and that the raids followed complaints from local people <sup>[N330](#n330)</sup>.
- **December 2025:** citing Malaysian media, Prothom Alo reported that 182 Bangladeshis were detained in November alone and 377 in one raid in September, and that Bangladeshis are detained in almost every Malaysian Immigration raid and are later sent home <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.

<a id="after-arrest"></a>

#### 3.12.2 After arrest

Someone whose pass has expired or been cancelled is removed whether or not prosecuted, held as long as removal takes, with no appeal <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. On entering an Immigration depot your biometrics are taken and you are blacklisted <sup>[N234 (2022)](#n234)</sup>. Depots have held more people than their capacity, and former detainees describe medical care denied or delayed <sup>[N274](#n274)</sup>. Those aged 20 to 54 among the people arrested in the November 2025 Cameron Highlands raid were to be sent to depots in Kelantan, Perak and Selangor; Immigration figures showed 83,994 foreigners detained across Malaysia from the start of 2025 to 18 November <sup>[N330](#n330)</sup>. Family can call the High Commission's consular line, +603 2604 0949, or email the labour wing; its officials visit camps <sup>[125](#o125), [130](#o130), [N331](#n331)</sup>. For visiting a detainee and the flight home, see [If you are detained](#if-you-are-detained).

> **Need to know**
> - Does Immigration's permission letter to visit a detainee cost anything?
> - How long does Immigration take to issue the permission letter to visit a detainee?

<a id="false-papers"></a>

#### 3.12.3 False papers

Making, forging or using a forged visa or pass: fine of at least RM30,000, 5 to 10 years' jail, up to 6 strokes <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. A false passport: RM15,000 to RM100,000, 2 to 10 years, up to 6 strokes <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. Letting someone else hold your passport, intending or knowing it may be used by another person, is also an offence <sup>[44 (1997)](#o44)</sup>.

<a id="drugs"></a>

#### 3.12.4 Drugs

**Carrying drugs in or out.** Morphine, heroin, opium, ganja and other dangerous drugs may not be brought in or taken out <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>. Trafficking means death or life imprisonment, plus at least 12 strokes if not sentenced to death <sup>[N332](#n332), [159 (2023)](#o159), [158](#o158)</sup>. Never carry a packet for someone else if you don't know what is inside <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

**Testing positive at the FOMEMA medical.** The FOMEMA urine test checks for opiates, methamphetamine and cannabis (see [After you arrive, item 2: FOMEMA medical within 30 days](#after-you-arrive-item-2)) <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>. A worker FOMEMA finds unfit gets no work pass and is sent home with a Check-Out Memo <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does a positive FOMEMA drug test by itself make a worker unfit, and can the employer appeal it?
> - What are the penalties under the Dangerous Drugs Act 1952 for having or using a small amount of drugs (not trafficking), and is a foreign worker convicted of it deported?

<a id="militancy-and-terrorism-charges"></a>

#### 3.12.5 Militancy and terrorism charges

**What was alleged.** Malaysia's home minister, Saifuddin Nasution Ismail, said on 27 June 2025 that police had arrested 36 Bangladeshis on allegations of direct involvement in a "radical militant movement", in three phases of operations in Selangor and Johor from 24 April 2025 <sup>[N333](#n333)</sup>. He said police found the group, inspired by Islamic State ideology, had formed a cell to spread extremism within its own community, radicalise people, raise money for terrorist activities and work to overthrow the legitimate government of its home country <sup>[N333](#n333)</sup>. Malaysia's police chief, Khalid Ismail, said on 4 July 2025, citing intelligence, that the ring targeted other Bangladeshi workers to recruit members and spread extremist ideology through social media and online messaging platforms, that those detained sent money to Islamic State in Syria and Bangladesh, and that 100 to 150 people might be involved <sup>[N334](#n334)</sup>. He said all those detained had been working in factories, construction and services in Malaysia <sup>[N335](#n335)</sup>.

**What happened to those arrested:**

- **Malaysia's account (27 June 2025):** five were charged under 6A of Malaysia's Penal Code, which applies to terrorism-related offences, at the Shah Alam and Johor Bahru sessions courts; removal orders were issued for 15; 16 were still under investigation <sup>[N333](#n333)</sup>. Malaysia's police chief said on 4 July 2025 that those less involved would be sent home and those deeply involved dealt with under Malaysian law <sup>[N334](#n334)</sup>.
- **The High Commission's account (July 2025):** sources at the Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur said the Royal Malaysia Police counter-terrorism division had arrested 35, not 36, Bangladeshis between April and June 2025; 14 had overstayed their visas and were handed to immigration, six of whom had been sent back to Bangladesh; five were charged with terrorism offences; 16 were under further investigation <sup>[N336](#n336)</sup>.
- **In prison:** the five charged were held in two prisons, two in Selangor and three in Johor Bahru; High Commission officials were due to visit them on 9 July 2025, after the prison authorities gave permission, to learn whether they needed legal or other basic help <sup>[N336](#n336)</sup>.
- **Two Bangladeshis charged (August 2025; Prothom Alo did not link them to the 36):** in a Malaysian sessions court, Md Mamun Ali, 31, was charged under section 130J(1)(a) of the Penal Code with supporting Islamic State through a Facebook account from July 2023 to April 2024, which carries life or up to 30 years in prison and a fine; Rifat Bishat, 27, was charged under section 130JB(1)(a) with keeping a picture of the Islamic State flag on his mobile phone at a house in the Larkin Industrial Estate area, which carries up to seven years in prison or a fine, and forfeiture of the items <sup>[N337](#n337)</sup>. No lawyer represented either, and the hearing was put off to 12 September 2025 to appoint an interpreter <sup>[N337](#n337)</sup>.
- **Back in Bangladesh:** Asif Nazrul, the interim government's adviser for expatriates' welfare, said on the night of 4 July 2025 that three of the arrested workers had already returned and were being questioned, that everyone returned on this allegation would be questioned, and that those against whom proof of involvement was found would be put on trial <sup>[N334](#n334)</sup>.

**What the High Commission could do.** Bangladesh's foreign ministry said on 5 July 2025 that the High Commission contacted Malaysian authorities right after the arrests, that Bangladesh had formally asked for the identities of those held and the charges against them, that it would cooperate fully with Malaysia's investigation, and that the High Commission would give Bangladeshis the help they needed <sup>[N335](#n335)</sup>. A diplomatic source said in July 2025 that the High Commission had contacted Malaysia's foreign, home and human resources ministries from the start for help over the 35 but had not received the expected response, and had not been told at the time that a counter-terrorism operation was under way <sup>[N336](#n336)</sup>.

<a id="banned-goods"></a>

#### 3.12.6 Banned goods

Obscene pictures or films on any media, daggers, flick knives and cloth printed with Quran verses may not be brought in; declare anything dutiable and expect your bags to be opened <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>.

<a id="if-something-goes-wrong"></a>

### 3.13 If something goes wrong

<a id="if-you-arent-paid"></a>

#### 3.13.1 If you aren't paid

**Complain through any of these four routes, alone or several at once:** the WFW app, the High Commission's labour wing, the Labour Court and, from Bangladesh, the Ministry. In Sabah and Sarawak, go to the state's own Labour Department instead (In Sabah and Sarawak, at the end of this section) <sup>[109](#o109), [130](#o130), [101 (2017)](#o101), [104](#o104), [160](#o160)</sup>. For free help, see Free help in Malaysia <sup>[N324](#n324)</sup>.

Photograph your contract, payslips, attendance records, passport and pass, and note the employer's registered name and address, from SSM e-Info if you don't have them ([Step 1, item 7: Check the employer exists](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-7)) <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101), [N229 (2021)](#n229), [49](#o49)</sup>. Without a written contract you are still presumed an employee if the other person controls your work or hours, provides tools, or pays you regularly as most of your income <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. If you work for a subcontractor, the main contractor is also liable for up to 3 months' unpaid wages if you complain within 90 days of the due date <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. If the employer seems about to run off to avoid paying, complain to a Magistrate, who can require a bond or order arrest <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker reach a Magistrate to complain about an employer about to run off?

**Cases:**

- **January 2024:** Malaysia's human resources minister said 751 Bangladeshi migrant workers had filed cases with the Labour Department claiming unpaid wages of about Tk 5.11 crore (at RM1 = Tk 23.26) <sup>[N85 (2024)](#n85)</sup>.
- **June 2026:** all workers at Aerofoam Manufacturing (1969) Sdn Bhd, a Malaysian maker of bedding products employing Bangladeshis, had gone three months without wages, according to documents of the Migrant Welfare Network (MWN), a rights platform for Bangladeshi migrants; they could not meet daily costs or send money home, and many who had borrowed at high interest to migrate could not pay their instalments <sup>[N325](#n325)</sup>. Their complaint to the Labour Department is under If you are abused, denied food or medical care, or your passport is taken <sup>[N325](#n325)</sup>.

**1. Working for Workers (WFW) app**, the Ministry of Human Resources' complaint channel <sup>[109](#o109), [110](#o110)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[N338 (2021)](#n338)</sup>:

- a smartphone;
- your name, mobile, passport number, gender and nationality;
- the company's name, phone and workplace address (state, district and postcode).

1. Install "WFW" from Google Play or the App Store (official page: [workforworkers.com.my](https://www.workforworkers.com.my/sapn-portal/index)) and register <sup>[110](#o110), [161](#o161), [N338 (2021)](#n338)</sup>.
2. Choose the complaint type (unpaid, late or below-minimum wages, no overtime pay, unlawful deductions, housing) and describe what happened <sup>[N338 (2021)](#n338), [109](#o109)</sup>.

What you get back: the complaint goes to the labour authorities, your identity is kept confidential, and you track it in the app; no Labour Office visit is needed <sup>[110](#o110), [161](#o161)</sup>. At launch the ministry said complaints would be dealt with within 3 to 7 days <sup>[N338 (2021)](#n338)</sup>.

**In practice:** claims without payslips are hard to prove; complainers have been warned off, falsely reported absent or had visas cancelled (If your employer reports you absent (lari)) <sup>[45](#o45), [N246 (2024)](#n246), [N206](#n206)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the WFW app cost anything?
> - Does the WFW app work in Bangla?
> - What can a worker do if a WFW complaint gets no response?

**2. The High Commission's labour wing**, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>. You'll need <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>:

- what happened;
- proof you are a migrant worker (passport, work permit, BMET smart card, Malaysian ID number);
- the employer's name and address;
- what you are owed.

1. Email counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, ss.lab.kl@gmail.com or fsl.bhc.kl@gmail.com, or call +601 2290 3252; a group can send one email <sup>[130](#o130), [111](#o111), [N339](#n339)</sup>.
2. If it doesn't act, apply to the Ministry ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 4), which tells it to <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

What happens: it calls the employer in, visits the workplace and attends Labour Department hearings, and takes ignored cases to the Ministry of Human Resources <sup>[N339](#n339), [N340 (2023)](#n340)</sup>.

**In practice:** it recovered compensation on 2,857 claims in 2025 <sup>[N341](#n341)</sup>; other workers say complaints to it and the Labour Department brought nothing until a migrant rights group stepped in (see Free help in Malaysia) <sup>[N342](#n342), [N215](#n215)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does the labour wing take to reply?
> - Does the labour wing charge anything?

**3. Labour Court claim**, the Labour Department's (JTK's) free wage tribunal, with no upper limit <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. The employer may not stop you going to it <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101), [N229 (2021)](#n229), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>:

- your passport (or a copy);
- the employer's name and address;
- payslips;
- contract or appointment letter;
- any termination letter;
- arrival date;
- witnesses' names.

1. For free advice call 03-8000 8000. Then give a sworn statement to an officer at the nearest Labour Office (Pejabat Tenaga Kerja), or complain by letter or to jtksm@mohr.gov.my <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101), [34 (2023)](#o34), [N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>. JTK's complaint pages and hotline are not in Bangla <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>.
2. The officer sets a mention date (a first short hearing), summons the employer and tells you when and where; bring your witnesses <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.
3. Attend every date; an absent employer can lose by default <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Workers with the same claim can file together through representatives <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. No lawyers, but a trade union can help <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>.

What you get back: a written order to pay, with up to 8% yearly interest, enforced through the courts (for example by seizing property) if unpaid <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34), [101 (2017)](#o101)</sup>. Either side can appeal to the High Court within 14 days <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101)</sup>.

Time: about a month if settled, about 3 months if heard <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>. Challenge a misconduct dismissal within 60 days of being told of it <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

If your pass ends mid-case: take a Labour Office letter showing the pending case to Immigration's Foreign Workers Division for a Special Pass (one month, extendable; usually granted with at least two extensions; decided within 3 working days; RM100). It doesn't let you work <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229), [100](#o100), [138](#o138), [162](#o162)</sup>.

**In practice:** about half of claims settle at the mention and about 85% of those heard are decided for the worker, but 38% are withdrawn or dismissed for non-attendance, usually because the pass expired <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>; employers often stall until the pass runs out, hence the Special Pass above <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>. An order is not money: Kawaguchi's workers were awarded over RM3 million in wages but got only RM251,000 after the company held back pay for months and closed <sup>[N216](#n216)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where is the official list of Labour Office addresses?
> - Can a worker apply for the Special Pass himself?
> - Does each Special Pass extension cost RM100?

**4. Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, Bangladesh** (you or your family). You'll need the same details as for the labour wing ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 2) <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

Send it free to the Welfare-1 branch: saswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030251 <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

What you get back: within 7 working days the Ministry asks the labour wing or the Wage Earners' Welfare Board to act, and tells you by letter <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>. Advice any time: 16135 in Bangladesh (free) or +880 9610102030 from abroad <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

**In Sabah and Sarawak** complain to the state's own Labour Department, under its Labour Ordinance <sup>[160](#o160), [163](#o163)</sup>.

- **Sarawak.** Complain to the labour office nearest the workplace, any of these ways <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>:
  - in writing or on its Borang Aduan Pekerja form, by post, fax or email;
  - in person;
  - by phone;
  - on SISPAA (the Malaysian government's online complaints system);
  - on the WFW app.

  Give your and the employer's name, address and phone, the issues, and your contract and payslips <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>. A Labour Court claim is made in person by sworn statement, with your passport, the employer's name and address, contract, pay details and the amount claimed <sup>[163](#o163)</sup>. Headquarters: Tingkat 13, Bangunan Sultan Iskandar, Jalan Simpang Tiga, 93532 Kuching; 082-242261; jtknsarawak@mohr.gov.my <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>.
- **Sabah.** Complain to JTK Sabah, jtksabah.gov.my <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - What are JTK Sabah's office addresses and phone numbers?
  > - Does JTK Sabah take WFW complaints?

<a id="if-you-are-abused-denied-food-or-medical-care-or-your-passport-is-taken"></a>

#### 3.13.2 If you are abused, denied food or medical care, or your passport is taken

**In danger now:** call Tenaganita's 24-hour emergency lines ([Where to complain](#where-to-complain-in-malaysia-and-from-bangladesh), in Malaysia and from Bangladesh) and email the labour wing ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 2) <sup>[N343](#n343), [130](#o130)</sup>. SUHAKAM also takes complaints ([Where to complain](#where-to-complain-in-malaysia-and-from-bangladesh), in Malaysia and from Bangladesh) <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>.

- **Beaten or threatened:** you may leave the job at once, without notice, if you or your dependants face immediate violence ([If you resign or are dismissed](#if-you-resign-or-are-dismissed)) <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Report it at any police station, even outside the area where it happened, either orally (the officer writes it down and reads it back) or on form Pol. 55, free at the counter, in Malay or English only; buy a certified copy for RM2 at once or within 3 days <sup>[165](#o165)</sup>. In December 2023, 171 Bangladeshis were arrested in Kota Tinggi, Johor, when they went to the police to report their agent <sup>[N140 (2023)](#n140)</sup>.
- **Food denied:** food is usually at your own cost; ministry permission letters list it as paid by the worker (Housing, food and health care) <sup>[95 (2024)](#o95), [29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>. If the employer provides food or meals, their cost can be taken from wages only at your written request and with Labour Department approval <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>; complain about a breach through the routes under If you aren't paid <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Where does a worker complain if an employer stops food the contract says it will provide?
- **Ill and refused care:** the person in charge of employer housing must take a worker who complains of illness to a clinic or hospital (Housing, food and health care) <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>; complain through the routes under If you aren't paid.
- **Passport taken:** ask for it back, then complain to the Labour Department ([After you arrive, item 4: Keep your own passport](#after-you-arrive-item-4)) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [109](#o109)</sup>.

**Forced labour**

An employer who threatens, deceives or forces you to work and stops you leaving can be fined up to RM100,000, jailed up to 2 years, or both <sup>[148 (2022)](#o148)</sup>.

**Case:** in June 2026 the Aerofoam workers ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) alleged that the company held most of their passports, forced them to work overtime against their will without enough rest, gave no transport to the workplace and had no working grievance mechanism; their complaint to the Labour Department (JTK) demanded payment of arrears, return of their passports, an end to forced overtime, a safe workplace, an investigation of high recruitment fees and no retaliation against them <sup>[N325](#n325)</sup>.

**Sexual harassment**

Report it to the Labour Department's Director General, who can direct the employer to inquire <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. A woman worker or a close relative in Bangladesh can also apply free to the Ministry, aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, with passport, smart card and residence-permit copies, the agency's details and the accused person's contacts; it acts within 7 working days <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker file a sexual harassment complaint with the Labour Department's Director General?

<a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill"></a>

#### 3.13.3 If you are injured or fall ill

1. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-1"></a>**Get treatment.** Treatment is free at a government clinic or hospital, or at a PERKESO panel clinic <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>. At a government hospital show your valid passport; SPIKPA hospital insurance means you pay no deposit ([After you arrive, item 7: Social security, insurance and the retirement fund](#after-you-arrive-item-7)) <sup>[143](#o143), [144](#o144)</sup>. If you live in employer housing, the person in charge must take you to a clinic or hospital when you report illness <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-2"></a>**Tell the employer within 48 hours, with a doctor's certificate,** or the days count as absence; sick-leave days are in Hours, rest and leave <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-3"></a>**Claim from PERKESO (SOCSO).** Its Employment Injury Scheme covers accidents and diseases from work, and its Invalidity Scheme critical illness or death in Malaysia before 60 from any cause; SOCSO: 1-300-22-8000 <sup>[36](#o36), [142 (2022)](#o142)</sup>. The form, documents, where to file and what you can get: [If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job](#if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job).
4. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-4"></a>**If the employer won't report it or pay you,** file a case at the Labour Office with payslips, contract and attendance record, and give a copy to the High Commission <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>.

<a id="if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-lari"></a>

#### 3.13.4 If your employer reports you absent (lari)

**What counts**

Leaving the workplace without telling the employer, intending to escape, or not returning on the agreed date after a trip home <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24)</sup>.

**What follows**

The employer reports you to Immigration with its ID, your passport copy, the runaway form and a police report, and tells the Labour Department within 14 days <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24), [148 (2022)](#o148), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. You are blacklisted and its security bond is forfeited <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24)</sup>. Immigration normally cancels a quitting worker's pass at once, leaving you without a valid pass <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>. False reports are filed near the contract's end to avoid the fare home, or after a complaint <sup>[45](#o45)</sup>.

**What you can do**

1. Keep photos of your pass, payslips and attendance records, and details of any WFW or Labour Office complaint <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101), [110](#o110)</sup>.
2. If you leave over abuse or unpaid wages, file at the Labour Office and email the labour wing <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131), [130](#o130)</sup>; the pending-case letter usually gets you a Special Pass ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 3) <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>.
3. If you are arrested, family can call Tenaganita's 24-hour line and email the labour wing <sup>[N343](#n343), [130](#o130)</sup>.

A reported worker is blacklisted, and blacklisted workers can't use PRM 2.0, Malaysia's repatriation programme for people without valid papers ([Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers), [Step 3: Go to Immigration](#step-3-go-to-immigration), Who can't use PRM 2.0) <sup>[5](#o5), [N239](#n239)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker find out about an absence report or blacklisting, given Immigration's online check is for citizens only <sup>[167](#o167)</sup>?
> - How does a worker clear a false absence report?

<a id="where-to-complain-in-malaysia-and-from-bangladesh"></a>

#### 3.13.5 Where to complain, in Malaysia and from Bangladesh

Official complaint routes, in Malaysia and from Bangladesh: If you aren't paid, routes 1 to 4; agency cases: [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money" <sup>[109](#o109), [104](#o104), [1](#o1)</sup>. The labour wing's Minister (Labour), Md Siddiqur Rahman, said at a community event in Putrajaya on 1 June 2026 that any Bangladeshi in trouble anywhere in Malaysia should tell the High Commission by writing on its Facebook page, by email or by phone, and it would try to solve the problem <sup>[N344](#n344)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the address of the High Commission's Facebook page that the labour wing reads <sup>[N344](#n344)</sup>?

**Free help in Malaysia:** Tenaganita (cases and a 24-hour emergency line), Bar Council legal aid, SUHAKAM and MTUC, listed after this paragraph <sup>[N324](#n324)</sup>. Unrepresented workers in the 2024 Pengerang mediation settled for about half their RM2.1 million claim, while 74 workers helped by Tenaganita won a Labour Office order for RM1.54 million <sup>[N345 (2024)](#n345), [N346](#n346)</sup>. The Welfare Board has no safe house in Malaysia <sup>[168](#o168)</sup>.

- Tenaganita (an NGO that takes migrant workers' cases, and helps gather the documents a Labour Department claim needs): 12 Jalan 6/11, 46000 Petaling Jaya, Selangor, +603 7770 3671 or 3691, Monday to Friday 9:30 to 6:00, general@tenaganita.net; 24-hour emergency lines for abuse, rescue, arrest or detention +6012 335 0512 and +6012 339 5350 <sup>[N324](#n324), [N343](#n343), [N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>.
- Bar Council Legal Aid Centres give non-citizens free legal advice in every Peninsular state, walk-in or by appointment; bring your documents <sup>[N347](#n347)</sup>. Kuala Lumpur: Unit 3.03, Level 3, Wisma Badan Peguam Malaysia, 2 Leboh Pasar Besar, 50050 Kuala Lumpur, +603 2691 1121 or 2692 1122, lac.kl@malaysianbar.org.my <sup>[N347](#n347)</sup>. They advise and help negotiate; representation is rare, criminal only, and may cost money <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229), [N347](#n347)</sup>.
- SUHAKAM investigates complaints from anyone, including non-citizens, about exploitation or unpaid wages, and recommends action to the authorities <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>. Email aduan@suhakam.org.my, call +603-2612 5635 or 5636, write to 17th Floor, Menara Aras Raya, Jalan Raja Laut, Kuala Lumpur, or use "New Complaint" on suhakam.org.my and keep the Ticket ID <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>.
- MTUC (the Malaysian Trades Union Congress) has run a migrant worker centre at 10-5 Jalan USJ 9/5T, Subang Business Centre, 47620 Subang Jaya, Selangor, +603 8024 2953 or +6017 680 9384 <sup>[N348 (2018)](#n348)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Does the MTUC migrant worker centre still take cases?

<a id="changing-employer-or-leaving"></a>

### 3.14 Changing employer or leaving

<a id="if-you-want-to-change-employer"></a>

#### 3.14.1 If you want to change employer

**You can't apply yourself**

PLKS holders may not change employer or sector on their own, and the agreement contract bars it <sup>[5](#o5), [40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. Only the employer or its appointed company representative deals with Immigration; using an agent is an offence <sup>[90 (2021)](#o90)</sup>.

**When it is allowed**

Only if all of these hold:

- the employer is bankrupt, closed, has suffered a disaster such as a fire or economic crisis and can't keep its workers, has been restructured or renamed, or has died <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>;
- the new employer is in the same sector and allowed foreign workers <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>;
- your PLKS has 3 months left <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.

Not open in your 10th year or later, to workers legalised through 6P, RTK (Labour Recalibration) or other programmes, or to domestic helpers <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>. Malaysia's home minister said in January 2024 that the government would also allow workers to be transferred between sectors in line with quotas <sup>[N85 (2024)](#n85)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are cross-sector moves now possible, given Immigration says they need Home Ministry (KDN) permission <sup>[90 (2021)](#o90)</sup> but they have been reported as allowed <sup>[N349](#n349), [N85 (2024)](#n85)</sup>?

**How the employers do it**

1. Both employers register on ePPAx (eppax.gov.my, the Labour Department's employer system); the old employer applies naming the new one, which uploads its documents within 7 days <sup>[169 (2023)](#o169), [170 (2023)](#o170)</sup>.
2. The Labour Department investigates and issues a support letter <sup>[169 (2023)](#o169), [41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.
3. The old employer (or heir) submits the free Ministry of Human Resources form and documents at the foreign workers division, Level 5, Setia Perkasa 3, Kompleks Setia Perkasa, 62546 Putrajaya, 03-8886 6501, Monday to Thursday 8.30 am to 4.30 pm, queue numbers 8.30 am to noon only <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does a change of employer take?

<a id="if-you-resign-or-are-dismissed"></a>

#### 3.14.2 If you resign or are dismissed

**If you resign**

Immigration normally cancels your pass at once, and you pay your own ticket home <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229), [40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. Under the agreement contract, either side gives 2 months' notice or 2 months' wages <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. Not paying wages counts as the employer breaking the contract; either side may end it without notice for a wilful breach by the other, and you may leave at once if you or your dependants face immediate violence or disease you didn't agree to risk <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Being absent more than 2 working days in a row without leave or a reasonable excuse told to the employer counts as you breaking it <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

**If you are dismissed**

Dismissal for misconduct needs an inquiry first; suspension during it lasts at most 2 weeks on at least half pay <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. The employer pays your ticket home unless you are dismissed for misconduct ([Leaving at the end of the job, item 2: Ticket home](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-2)) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. In a redundancy, foreign workers in a similar job go before any local worker <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Termination benefits after 12 months are in End-of-service pay <sup>[N321](#n321)</sup>. When wages are due: Leaving at the end of the job, item 3 <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

<a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job"></a>

#### 3.14.3 Leaving at the end of the job

1. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-1"></a>**Check-Out Memo** (Memo Periksa Keluar, Immigration's approval to leave for home). The employer applies to Immigration with the form, your original passport and a ticket; you leave on the date and through the exit point on the memo, and the employer is responsible for you until then <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. The Home Minister announced in May 2025 that employers must also be present at the exit point <sup>[N231](#n231)</sup>.
2. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-2"></a>**Ticket home.**
   - The employer pays when the contract ends, when it ends the contract early (unless you breached it), when you leave because it broke the contract, or after serious work injury or illness <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>.
   - You pay if you resign or are dismissed for misconduct <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>.
3. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-3"></a>**What you are owed:**
   - wages by the last day of the job, or within 3 days if you end it without notice <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>;
   - all outstanding wages if sent home early, untaken annual leave (unless dismissed for misconduct), and termination benefits if the employer ended the job after 12 months ([Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [34 (2023)](#o34), [N321](#n321)</sup>.
4. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-4"></a>**Withdraw your EPF before you fly.** You'll need <sup>[145](#o145), [171](#o171)</sup>:
   - Form KWSP 9K (AHL) and checklist;
   - a copy of your valid passport (or a High Commission travel permit);
   - the Check-Out Memo;
   - a bank document for an active account at an EPF panel bank in the passport details EPF holds.

   1. Apply as soon as you have the memo <sup>[171](#o171)</sup>.
   2. Apply in person at the self-service terminal in an EPF office, with copies certified by an EPF officer, the High Commission or your employer; helpline +603-8922 6000 <sup>[145](#o145)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Where is EPF's official list of offices?
      > - Does EPF withdrawal cost anything?
      > - How long does EPF take to pay out?

   What you get back: all your savings in ringgit into that account, so keep it open, or a banker's cheque if payment fails <sup>[145](#o145), [171](#o171)</sup>.

   Already left? Use EPF's overseas application: post the forms, certified by a Malaysian high commission or notary, to EPF, Jabatan Pengurusan Transaksi, Menara KWSP, No.1 Persiaran Kwasa Utama, Kwasa Damansara, Seksyen U4, 40150 Shah Alam, Selangor; the money is sent by bank transfer <sup>[145](#o145)</sup>.

   The EPF account then closes; returning workers register again <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>.
5. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-5"></a>**To leave** you need a passport valid 6 months and a valid pass <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>.

<a id="work-visa-domestic-worker"></a>

## 4 Work visa: domestic worker

This visa is not open to Bangladeshis. Immigration's approved source countries for domestic helpers are Indonesia, Thailand, Cambodia, the Philippines, Sri Lanka, India, Vietnam, Laos and Nepal <sup>[19](#o19), [9](#o9)</sup>.

<a id="student-visa-student-pass"></a>

## 5 Student visa (Student Pass)

A Student Pass lets you stay in Malaysia to study full time at an approved institution; it is not a work visa <sup>[53](#o53), [52](#o52)</sup>. You apply through EMGS (Education Malaysia Global Services, a company under Malaysia's Ministry of Higher Education, KPT), for public and private institutions alike <sup>[173](#o173), [52](#o52), [174](#o174)</sup>; the Immigration Department of Malaysia (IMI) gives final approval <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. More than 10,000 Bangladeshi students were studying at Malaysian universities in June 2026, Prothom Alo reported; Bangladeshi diplomats put it at at least 11,000, the second-largest group of foreign students after the Chinese (about 62,000) <sup>[N54](#n54), [N80](#n80)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-check-the-institution-and-the-course"></a>

### 5.1 Step 1: Check the institution and the course

Check before you pay anyone: agents earn commission from institutions and push the colleges paying most <sup>[N251](#n251)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-1-check-the-institution-and-the-course-item-1"></a>**Check the institution may take foreign students.** A private institution needs KPT approval, an accredited programme and the Home Ministry's (KDN's) permission to take international students; a public university needs no separate check <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. KPT's Department of Higher Education (JPT) lists private institutions with that permission, updated monthly <sup>[175](#o175), [176](#o176)</sup>.
   1. On [jpt.mohe.gov.my, Senarai Daftar dan Statistik IPT Swasta](https://jpt.mohe.gov.my/portal/index.php/ms/ipt/ipt-swasta/25-senarai-daftar-dan-statistik-ipt-swasta), click the download icon beside "Senarai Daftar IPTS Kelulusan KDN" <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>.
   2. Open "Tahun 2026", then the "AKTIF" (active) file with the highest number (in November 2026, file 7, the list up to 31 July 2026, updated 20 August 2026) <sup>[177](#o177), [178](#o178), [179](#o179)</sup>.
