Hand in two copies at any state Immigration headquarters with <sup>[259 (2022)](#o259)</sup>:

- a copy of the partner's identity card;
- two passport photos of each of you;
- a copy of the foreigner's passport with the pass pages.

An officer certifies the pass and its expiry on the form <sup>[259 (2022)](#o259)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the pass-confirmation form have a fee?
> - How long does it take?

The Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur asks a Bangladeshi for <sup>[254 (2024)](#o254)</sup>:

- an unmarried certificate from the Deputy Commissioner's office, attested by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in Dhaka;
- the parents' affidavit or consent, attested by the Ministry of Law and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs;
- the consular fee receipt.

The marriage must be registered under Malaysian law before the spouse can get permission to work or apply for permanent residence <sup>[260](#o260), [156](#o156)</sup>.

<a id="the-spouses-pass"></a>

#### 13.2.2 The spouse's pass

The foreign spouse of a Malaysian gets the LTSVP, issued for 6 months to 5 years at a time <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>. You apply together with the Malaysian spouse at the Immigration office nearest your home <sup>[261](#o261), [262](#o262)</sup>, and a decision takes 25 working days under IMI's client charter <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

You'll need, as originals with copies <sup>[196](#o196), [262](#o262)</sup>:

- IMI's forms, listed on IMI's [new spouse application page](https://www.imi.gov.my/index.php/permohonan-baru-isteri-atau-suami-kepada-warganegara/):
  - [Form Imm.12](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/IM.12.pdf);
  - [Form Imm.38](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/IM38.pdf) (2 copies);
  - [Form Imm.55](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Imm55.pdf) (2 copies);
  - [sponsor's statement](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borang_Kenyatan_Penaja.pdf);
  - [security bond](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Security-Bond.pdf);
  - [sworn declaration](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Surat_Akuan_Sumpah.pdf);
- the Malaysian spouse's identity card (MyKad), birth certificate and passport (all pages);
- the marriage registration certificate;
- any divorce or death certificate from an earlier marriage;
- a postcard-size wedding photo;
- children's birth certificates;
- the Malaysian spouse's employer letter (or business licence if self-employed);
- proof of income (3 months' payslips, bank book or tax Form J);
- a utility bill.

1. Stamp the security bond with a RM10 revenue stamp <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>; have the sworn declaration, with your photo, stamped by a Commissioner for Oaths <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.
2. Hand in the application together at that office; Immigration checks every original and accepts only complete applications <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the fee for the spouse LTSVP?
> - Can a refusal be appealed?

**Checks and scams.** Immigration refuses a marriage made only for immigration status and looks closely at large age gaps <sup>[N372](#n372)</sup>; it may visit your address, interview people and check documents before granting or extending the pass <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>. Anyone selling a spouse pass or marriage registration is a syndicate <sup>[N372](#n372)</sup>; report them (see [If Immigration mishandles any family-pass application](#if-immigration-mishandles-any-family-pass-application)) <sup>[263](#o263), [264](#o264)</sup>. A false statement in the application can bring a fine of up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail, or both <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.

<a id="permission-to-work"></a>

#### 13.2.3 Permission to work

Permission to work, once endorsed in the passport, lets the spouse work, run a business or practise a profession on the LTSVP <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.

You'll need:

- the Malaysian spouse in person with their MyKad;
- your passport and pass;
- a sworn statement;
- the marriage certificate;
- the job offer;
- an employment contract with a RM10 revenue stamp stating the job, period and monthly pay <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>;
- IMI's [Applicant, Citizen Spouse and Employer Information Form](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borang_Maklumat.pdf);
- the employer's latest company registration form from MyData SSM (the online records of the Companies Commission of Malaysia) <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.

Apply at the Visa, Pass and Permit Division of Immigration in the state where you'll work, with the LTSVP application or any time after a job offer <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can the work permission also be applied for at Immigration headquarters in Putrajaya or the nearest state office?
> - What is its fee?
> - How long does it take?

<a id="permanent-residence"></a>

#### 13.2.4 Permanent residence

A wife can apply for an Entry Permit after 3 years of marriage and over 12 months on the spouse LTSVP; a husband after 5 years of marriage and 5 continuous years on it <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>. See [Permanent residence (Entry Permit)](#permanent-residence-entry-permit).

<a id="other-relatives-of-malaysians-and-of-permanent-residents"></a>

### 13.3 Other relatives of Malaysians and of permanent residents

IMI gives an LTSVP in other family categories <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>:

- A foreigner aged 60 or over with no family at home, sponsored by a child, child-in-law or sibling in Malaysia earning RM2,000 or more <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>, with a personal bond stamped RM10 at LHDN <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.
- An abused, divorced or widowed wife of a citizen, with a local sponsor earning RM2,000 or more <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>; a widow's sponsor is a close relative of her late husband who comes with her <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>; an abused wife adds a police or hospital report, a security bond stamped RM10, and the sponsor's declaration and identity card <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.
- The wife of a permanent resident, married over 6 months <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.
- A citizen's child or stepchild aged 7 or under, on the citizen parent's application <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.
- Close family of a foreign student, with the student's letter from the institution and an embassy letter proving the relationship <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>; see [Student visa (Student Pass), Bringing your family](#bringing-your-family-1).
- A patient at a government or specialist hospital, with one companion (see [Medical visit, How long you can stay](#how-long-you-can-stay-1)) <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>:

- all originals;
- Forms Imm.12 (or Imm.55) and Imm.38;
- the marriage, divorce or birth certificates that apply.

Apply at the Immigration office nearest your home; only complete applications are accepted, and the charter gives 25 working days <sup>[261](#o261), [196](#o196), [100](#o100)</sup>. A dependant's or guardian's LTSVP costs RM90 <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. A false statement is an offence <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - May these pass holders work?
> - What is the fee for the other categories?
> - Can a refusal be appealed?

<a id="residence-pass"></a>

#### 13.3.1 Residence Pass

The Residence Pass (Pas Residen: RM500 for 5 years) allows work, study and business <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>. Who can get it <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>:

- relatives of citizens (biological, step or adopted children; a widowed or divorced spouse with a citizen child; parents and parents-in-law), after 3 years on a valid long-term pass;
- children and spouses of permanent residents, after 5.

You'll need:

- Form IMM 16A <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>;
- 2 passport photos and a copy of the sponsor's identity card <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>;
- old and new passports and original supporting documents, with copies <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>;
- copies of Bangladeshi documents attested by the Bangladesh High Commission <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>.

1. A Malaysian citizen aged 21 or over sponsors you and comes with you to Immigration headquarters in Putrajaya or a nearby Immigration office <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>.
2. Pay RM500 plus a multiple-entry visa fee set by nationality <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>.
3. Track it on myOnlinePasResiden at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main) <sup>[190](#o190)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does a Residence Pass decision take?
> - Can a refusal be appealed?

<a id="if-immigration-mishandles-any-family-pass-application"></a>

#### 13.3.2 If Immigration mishandles any family-pass application

- **Complain online** on IMI's SISPAA at [imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do](https://imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do) <sup>[263](#o263)</sup>:
  1. Choose "Aduan/Maklum Balas Baharu" (new complaint).
  2. Fill in every field. For example: "I applied for a [pass] at [office] on [date], reference [number]. After [N] working days I have no decision, beyond IMI's client charter time (14 working days for a Dependant Pass, 25 for an LTSVP). Please tell me the status." <sup>[234](#o234), [100](#o100)</sup>
  3. Track it under "Semak Aduan/Maklum Balas" <sup>[263](#o263)</sup>.
- **An officer asks for money or misbehaves:** report to IMI's Integrity Division: Tingkat 4 (Menara), Persiaran Perdana, Presint 2, 62550 Putrajaya; aduanintegriti@imi.gov.my; 03-8880 1246. Giving your name and contact protects you under the Whistleblower Protection Act 2010 <sup>[264](#o264)</sup>.
- **Immigration headquarters:** No. 15, Persiaran Perdana, Presint 2, 62550 Putrajaya; 03-8000 8000 <sup>[266 (2022)](#o266)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long do SISPAA complaints take to answer?
> - Does the Bangladesh High Commission help with family-pass cases?

<a id="mm2h-malaysia-my-second-home"></a>

## 14 MM2H (Malaysia My Second Home)

A long-stay pass for people with substantial savings, run by MOTAC (the Ministry of Tourism, Arts and Culture), for citizens of any country with diplomatic relations with Malaysia <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>, aged 25 or over (21 for the SEZ/SFZ, Special Economic Zone / Special Financial Zone, tier) <sup>[267](#o267), [268](#o268)</sup>. Whether the deposit can legally leave Bangladesh: see [How to apply](#how-to-apply), item 1.

Malaysia's tourism minister told its parliament that 3,604 Bangladeshis held MM2H passes on 31 January 2024, the fifth-largest group after China, Australia, South Korea and Japan, out of 56,066 active pass holders including dependants; Bangladeshis had once been third <sup>[N373 (2024)](#n373)</sup>. Prothom Alo gave the same 3,604 in September 2024 and in December 2025, when it ranked Bangladeshis fourth <sup>[N374 (2024)](#n374), [N45](#n45)</sup>.

**Tiers** <sup>[269](#o269), [270](#o270)</sup>:

- **Silver:** USD 150,000 fixed deposit and a home of at least RM600,000.
- **Gold:** USD 500,000 and RM1,000,000.
- **Platinum:** USD 1,000,000 and RM2,000,000.
- **SEZ/SFZ:** USD 65,000 (age 21 to 49) or USD 32,000 (50 and over) and a home in Forest City, Johor <sup>[269](#o269), [268](#o268)</sup>.

Prothom Alo reported in March 2024 that under the December 2023 restructuring applicants had to be 30 or older, hold a fixed deposit of RM5 million (Platinum), RM2 million (Gold) or RM500,000 (Silver), and spend 60 days a year in Malaysia <sup>[N373 (2024)](#n373)</sup>. In December 2025 it reported that a 5-year visa comes with an investment of Tk 3 crore 60 lakh and a 20-year visa with more, on condition of investing in property <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which terms apply now: age 25 <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>, USD deposits <sup>[269](#o269)</sup> and 90 days a year for under-50s <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>, or the age 30, ringgit deposits and 60 days a year reported in March 2024 <sup>[N373 (2024)](#n373)</sup>?

**Family** <sup>[267](#o267), [269](#o269)</sup>:

- a spouse;
- children under 21 (biological, step or adopted);
- unmarried, unemployed children aged 21 to 34;
- disabled children of any age;
- parents and parents-in-law.

<a id="how-to-apply"></a>

### 14.1 How to apply

1. <a id="how-to-apply-item-1"></a>**Check the money can legally leave Bangladesh.** Bangladesh Bank controls money leaving Bangladesh under the Foreign Exchange Regulation Act 1947 <sup>[271](#o271)</sup>; in 2024 it was reported not to have approved sending money abroad for MM2H <sup>[N375 (2024)](#n375)</sup>; a Malaysian MM2H licence doesn't by itself mean an agent meets Bangladesh's rules <sup>[N376](#n376)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported in March 2024 that Bangladesh Bank had permitted only a small number of institutions to invest abroad, and that there were allegations that money laundered out of Bangladesh was being invested in Malaysian "second homes" <sup>[N373 (2024)](#n373)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What is Bangladesh Bank's written rule on sending money abroad for MM2H?
   > - How do you ask Bangladesh Bank for permission?

2. <a id="how-to-apply-item-2"></a>**Choose a licensed agent.** Every application goes through an MM2H operator licensed by MOTAC <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>. Check at [mm2h.gov.my/agencies](https://www.mm2h.gov.my/agencies) that the company name, licence number (MM2H plus digits) and licence dates match what the agent shows <sup>[272](#o272)</sup>. No agent can guarantee approval <sup>[N377 (2024)](#n377)</sup>; for agents who vanished with clients' money, see [Scams](#scams-8).

3. <a id="how-to-apply-item-3"></a>**The agent applies** through the One Stop Centre MM2H on MOTAC's online system; the Ministry of Home Affairs approves through Immigration <sup>[267](#o267), [273](#o273)</sup>. Fees:
   - processing: RM5,000 for the main applicant and RM2,500 per dependant <sup>[269](#o269)</sup>;
   - participation: RM1,000 (Silver, SEZ/SFZ), RM3,000 (Gold) or RM200,000 (Platinum) <sup>[269](#o269)</sup>;
   - MOTAC's system also lists RM500 plus SST (sales and service tax) per application <sup>[273](#o273)</sup>.

   You need a Certificate of Good Conduct (in Bangladesh, a police clearance certificate; see Work visa: company worker (PLKS), [Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates)) and Malaysian police screening <sup>[N378 (2024)](#n378)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is the RM500 in MOTAC's system extra to the processing and participation fees?
   > - What is the full document list?
   > - What is the processing time?

4. <a id="how-to-apply-item-4"></a>**After approval.**
   1. Everyone has a medical check at a MOTAC panel clinic or hospital <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>.
   2. Open the fixed deposit in a licensed Malaysian bank; up to half can later be withdrawn for a home, education, medical care or tourism in Malaysia <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>.
   3. Buy a home within a year of endorsement and keep it 10 years unless selling to buy a dearer one <sup>[274](#o274), [267](#o267)</sup>.

   Immigration's client charter gives 1 working day to endorse the pass <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-you-can-stay-2"></a>

### 14.2 How long you can stay

The pass lasts 5 years (Silver), 15 (Gold), 20 (Platinum) or 10 (SEZ/SFZ) <sup>[269](#o269), [270](#o270), [268](#o268)</sup>.

- Under 50, spend at least 90 days a year in Malaysia <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>.
- Renewal is RM500 a year plus a visa fee of RM0 to RM50 by nationality <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>.
- MM2H years don't count toward permanent residence <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.

<a id="can-you-work-7"></a>

### 14.3 Can you work?

Only Platinum holders may work or run a business <sup>[275](#o275), [270](#o270), [276](#o276), [268](#o268)</sup>.

<a id="if-the-visa-is-refused-7"></a>

### 14.4 If the visa is refused

The Ministry of Home Affairs makes the final decision through the Immigration Department, and any appeal, if one is allowed at all, goes to the Ministry <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a refused MM2H applicant appeal to the Ministry of Home Affairs, or only apply again?
> - If an appeal exists, how is it made (form, address, fee) and by when?

<a id="scams-8"></a>

### 14.5 Scams

Agents have taken tens of thousands of ringgit, then stopped answering, and applicants couldn't recover it <sup>[N377 (2024)](#n377)</sup>. Before paying, check the agent's MOTAC licence ([How to apply, item 2: Choose a licensed agent](#how-to-apply-item-2)). If cheated:

- Complain on MOTAC's SISPAA at [motac.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do](https://motac.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do) <sup>[277](#o277)</sup>:
  1. Choose "Aduan/Maklum Balas Baharu".
  2. Give the agent's name and licence number, and what you paid and when. For example: "I paid RM[amount] on [date] to [agent], licence MM2H[number], for an MM2H application. The agent has not applied or answered since [date]. Receipts attached. Please investigate and help me recover the money."
  3. Attach receipts.
  4. Track it under "Semak Aduan/Maklum Balas" <sup>[277](#o277)</sup>.
- Or call the MM2H office: +603 8891 7568 or +603 8891 7434; MOTAC, No. 2, Tower 1, Jalan P5/6, Presint 5, 62200 Putrajaya <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has MOTAC ever recovered money for an applicant cheated by an agent?

<a id="sarawaks-s-mm2h"></a>

### 14.6 Sarawak's S-MM2H

Sarawak runs its own scheme, on these terms since 1 January 2025 <sup>[N379](#n379)</sup>:

- age 30 or over;
- RM500,000 fixed deposit in a Sarawak bank;
- 30 days a year in Sarawak;
- a 5-year pass;
- applied for through the state's one-stop centre or licensed agents.

Bangladesh Bank controls money leaving Bangladesh <sup>[271](#o271)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a Bangladeshi legally move RM500,000 from Bangladesh for the S-MM2H deposit (the same open question as for MM2H, [How to apply, item 1: Check the money can legally leave Bangladesh](#how-to-apply-item-1))?

<a id="de-rantau-nomad-pass"></a>

## 15 DE Rantau Nomad Pass

New applications were paused from 1 to 30 November 2026, to resume on 1 December 2026 <sup>[278](#o278)</sup>. The pass is for remote workers and freelancers paid from outside Malaysia, run by MDEC (the Malaysia Digital Economy Corporation), for all nationalities except Israel, main applicant over 18 <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>. Minimum income: USD 36,000 a year in tech, USD 60,000 otherwise <sup>[279](#o279), [280](#o280)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Did DE Rantau applications reopen on 1 December 2026, and what changed?

**Family** <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>:

- a spouse or common-law partner;
- children under 18 (including adopted and stepchildren);
- a special-needs child of any age;
- parents.

1. <a id="de-rantau-nomad-pass-item-1"></a>**Prepare the documents** in English or with a certified translation, as clear PDFs, with the same name and income figures throughout <sup>[279](#o279), [281](#o281)</sup>.

   You'll need, as a remote employee:

   - a passport with 6 empty pages, valid over 14 months <sup>[281](#o281), [279](#o279)</sup>;
   - a CV;
   - a signed contract showing remote work, title, duration and salary, with an employer not registered in Malaysia, starting at least 3 months before you apply <sup>[281](#o281), [279](#o279)</sup>;
   - 3 months' payslips and bank statements showing the same deposits <sup>[281](#o281)</sup>;
   - an income statement or tax return;
   - a Letter of Good Conduct (police clearance; see Work visa: company worker (PLKS), [Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates));
   - the personal bond form;
   - your highest education certificate <sup>[281](#o281)</sup>;
   - a medical insurance enrolment certificate valid in Malaysia for at least 3 months, covering dependants; it can be handed in after approval, before the pass is issued <sup>[281](#o281)</sup>.

   You'll need, as a freelancer <sup>[282](#o282), [279](#o279)</sup>:

   - the passport, CV, Letter of Good Conduct, bond form, certificate and insurance;
   - up to 3 signed client contracts showing scope, duration and payment terms;
   - 3 months' invoices;
   - proof of payment (PayPal, Stripe, Wise or bank records);
   - 3 months' bank statements above the threshold.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What is the minimum insurance cover?
   > - Which insurers are accepted?
   > - Do Malaysian clients count?

2. <a id="de-rantau-nomad-pass-item-2"></a>**Apply** at [mdec.my/derantau](https://mdec.my/derantau) and pay RM1,080 plus RM540 per dependant online, not refunded <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>. A decision takes 6 to 8 weeks after a complete application; track it at [malaysiadigital.mdec.my](https://malaysiadigital.mdec.my) <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>. If refused, see If the visa is refused below.
3. <a id="de-rantau-nomad-pass-item-3"></a>**After approval: enter and get the pass endorsed.** The approval letter is valid 6 months, not extendable; finish every step within it <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>.

   1. Get an eVISA (a visa applied for online) or, if the letter says so, a VDR (visa with reference) <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>. Apply for the eVISA yourself on MyVISA, with the fees and times in [Tourist visa, Step 2: Apply on MyVISA](#step-2-apply-on-myvisa) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. A tourist or social visit pass can't be converted: leave and re-enter <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - What does a VDR for DE Rantau cost?
      > - What documents does it need?
      > - How long does it take?

   2. Use a normal Immigration counter, not the automatic gate, to get an entry stamp <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>.
   3. Within 1 month, upload on the DE Rantau portal, under your approved application <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>:
      - the personal bond with a RM10 revenue stamp, endorsed by LHDN;
      - all passport pages;
      - the entry stamp;
      - the eVISA or VDR;
      - Malaysian health insurance;
      - an LHDN tax registration slip from mytax.hasil.gov.my.
   4. Pay Immigration RM90 per 3 months or RM360 a year, any multiple-entry visa charge, and a RM750 bond for a Bangladeshi, refunded when the pass expires <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>.
   5. Stay in Malaysia until the e-Pass is issued, about 1 week; leaving can cancel the application <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-you-can-stay-3"></a>

### 15.1 How long you can stay

Up to 12 months at a time and 48 months in all <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>; holders at the two-year limit will get two more years under conditions to be set <sup>[258](#o258)</sup>. It gives no route to permanent residence <sup>[280](#o280)</sup>. The pass covers Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan; Sabah or Sarawak need a tourist pass <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>.

<a id="can-you-work-8"></a>

### 15.2 Can you work?

Only remote work for your employer or clients outside Malaysia; a spouse can't work <sup>[279](#o279), [281](#o281)</sup>.

<a id="if-the-visa-is-refused-8"></a>

### 15.3 If the visa is refused

No appeal; you can reapply <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>. For questions, contact MDEC's Expats Service Centre (expatctr@mdec.com.my, 03-8315 3106 / 3157) <sup>[278](#o278)</sup> or the form at [mdec.my/contact-us](https://mdec.my/contact-us), enquiry type "Foreign Knowledge Workers" <sup>[283](#o283)</sup>. For a visa or endorsement problem, complain on IMI's SISPAA at [imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do](https://imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do) <sup>[263](#o263)</sup>.

<a id="scams-9"></a>

### 15.4 Scams

<a id="done-to-applicants"></a>

#### 15.4.1 Done to applicants

An agent or consultant who says your approval is guaranteed is wrong: no agent, and not MDEC itself, can guarantee it <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>. The RM1,080 fee is not refunded if you are refused <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>, so apply yourself at [mdec.my/derantau](https://mdec.my/derantau) and track it at [malaysiadigital.mdec.my](https://malaysiadigital.mdec.my) <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>. Anyone telling you to enter on a tourist pass and switch later is wrong too: a tourist pass can't be converted <sup>[279](#o279)</sup>. Get the eVISA only on malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my; one bought through another site can't be checked <sup>[3](#o3)</sup> (see [Tourist visa, Step 3: Check your visa is real](#step-3-check-your-visa-is-real)).

> **Need to know**
> - Is there any official warning about DE Rantau scams?

<a id="tried-by-applicants-1"></a>

#### 15.4.2 Tried by applicants

Getting a pass by a false statement, such as an invented contract or payslip, carries a fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years' jail <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>.

<a id="other-passes"></a>

## 16 Other passes

<a id="residence-pass-talent-rp-t"></a>

### 16.1 Residence Pass-Talent (RP-T)

A 10-year pass for experienced professionals from TalentCorp (Talent Corporation Malaysia, a government agency for skilled workers) <sup>[284](#o284)</sup>. You need <sup>[284](#o284)</sup>:

- 3 consecutive years' work in Malaysia and an Employment Pass with over 3 months left;
- at least RM15,000 basic a month;
- Malaysian income tax paid for 2 years;
- a degree, diploma or professional certificate with 5 years' experience.

The holder can change employer, and the spouse can work without an Employment Pass <sup>[284](#o284)</sup>.

1. Register an account on the RP-T Portal at [rpt.talentcorp.com.my](https://rpt.talentcorp.com.my/), complete the application and upload the documents yourself; no agent may apply for you <sup>[285](#o285)</sup>. Name a local contact: a Malaysian citizen aged 21 or over working and living in Malaysia, with a copy of both sides of their MyKad <sup>[285](#o285)</sup>.
2. Pay TalentCorp online RM540 per person on applying, not refunded; if approved, RM5,400 for you or your spouse and RM4,860 per other dependant; then Immigration RM500 per pass (RM90 for an LTSVP) plus the visa fee <sup>[286](#o286)</sup>.

3. Wait for the pass <sup>[285](#o285)</sup>:
   - approval: 21 working days from a complete application;
   - result: within 10 more;
   - endorsement: 7;
   - delivery by Pos Malaysia: within 7 more.

No appeal; reapply after 1 year <sup>[285](#o285)</sup>.

Questions: RPTCare@myxpats.com.my, 03-7839 7171 <sup>[285](#o285)</sup>; TalentCorp +603 7839 7000, info@talentcorp.com.my <sup>[284](#o284)</sup>.

<a id="malaysia-tech-entrepreneur-programme-mtep"></a>

### 16.2 Malaysia Tech Entrepreneur Programme (MTEP)

For tech founders, through MDEC, at [mdec.my/mtep](https://mdec.my/mtep). You need RM50,000 in personal bank statements, a certificate of good conduct and a sponsor <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>. The two passes <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>:

- **1 year, for new founders** (from RM2,700): the sponsor is a Malaysia Digital Hub or MTEP recognised partner, which signs a personal bond and gives a letter of offer or tenancy agreement.
- **5 years, for experienced founders** (from RM5,400, dependants allowed): the sponsor is a Malaysian sponsor or individual who signs a declaration.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the MTEP processing time?
> - Can a refusal be appealed?
> - Does any nationality restriction apply to Bangladeshis?

<a id="investor-pass"></a>

### 16.3 Investor Pass

For foreign investors committed through Malaysia's investment agencies; 6 months, extendable by 6, no dependants <sup>[288](#o288)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which investment agencies qualify an investor?
> - What investment must the investor have made or committed?

Apply from outside Malaysia on Xpats Gateway ([xpatsgateway.com.my](https://xpatsgateway.com.my/), the platform for professional passes), after the company is fully registered in ESD Online <sup>[288](#o288), [289](#o289)</sup>. Fees <sup>[288](#o288)</sup>:

- RM1,200 (RM1,296 with tax);
- a RM90 pass fee for 6 months;
- RM500 processing;
- the visa fee.

A complete application is processed within 5 working days, and the ePASS appears in Xpats Gateway 1 working day after payment <sup>[288](#o288)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can an Investor Pass refusal be appealed?
> - Does any nationality restriction apply to Bangladeshis?

<a id="premium-visa-programme-pvip"></a>

### 16.4 Premium Visa Programme (PVIP)

IMI's visa of up to 20 years for investors, entrepreneurs and talent, no age limit, allowing work and study <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>. You need offshore income of RM40,000 a month or RM480,000 a year and a RM1 million fixed deposit in a licensed Malaysian bank <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>. Fees:

- participation: RM200,000, plus RM100,000 per dependant <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>, RM2,000 of it paid at online registration <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>;
- the pass: RM2,000 a year <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>.

Dependants <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>:

- a spouse;
- children under 25 (biological, step or adopted);
- disabled children of any age;
- parents and parents-in-law;
- a domestic helper, under the domestic helper rules.

Only IMI-appointed PVIP agencies apply, on [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main) <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>, with your written power of attorney <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>; check the agency is registered with IMI before paying <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>. PVIP years don't count toward permanent residence <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where does IMI publish the list of appointed PVIP agencies?
> - How long does a PVIP application take?
> - Can a refusal be appealed?

<a id="plsxpats"></a>

### 16.5 PLS@XPATS

IMI's online permission for professionals to work up to 30 days on a social visit pass <sup>[291](#o291), [222 (2022)](#o222)</sup>, approved in 3 working days <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>; the company applies as below <sup>[292](#o292)</sup>. Emergency work may be considered by incident, need and policy <sup>[221](#o221)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a PLS@XPATS refusal be appealed?

You'll need (the company) <sup>[222 (2022)](#o222)</sup>:

- its request letter;
- a copy of every page of your passport, valid 6 months or more;
- its SSM registration;
- the work or project schedule;
- your return ticket.

1. The company applies free at [mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats/main](https://mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats/main), without ESD (IMI's Expatriate Services Division) registration; it takes 3 working days, for anyone 18 or over, not blacklisted and not Israeli <sup>[222 (2022)](#o222)</sup>.
2. You still need the eVISA, and must carry the approval letter or be refused entry for this work <sup>[222 (2022)](#o222)</sup>.

The approval is valid 30 days from approval for one entry and at most 30 days' work, not extendable, in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan only, with no family pass <sup>[222 (2022)](#o222)</sup>. Questions: eapp.imi.gov.my enquiry form <sup>[292](#o292)</sup>.

<a id="permanent-residence-entry-permit"></a>

### 16.6 Permanent residence (Entry Permit)

Lets a foreigner live in Malaysia without time limit and work, study and run a business without another pass <sup>[293](#o293), [156](#o156)</sup>; it can be cancelled for harm to public order, security, health or morality <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.

<a id="who-can-apply-1"></a>

#### 16.6.1 Who can apply

Those who can apply: a Malaysian citizen's wife, husband or biological child under 18, and "fully foreign" nationals <sup>[293](#o293), [156](#o156)</sup>: investors, experts and professionals in fields recognised by a Malaysian regulating agency, with 5 continuous years on an Employment, Student, Professional Visit or Residence Pass and no break over 6 months <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>. PLKS, MM2H and PVIP holders can't apply, and time on those passes doesn't count <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>. Check your points first at [app.imi.gov.my/epc](https://app.imi.gov.my/epc) <sup>[190](#o190)</sup>.

<a id="documents"></a>

#### 16.6.2 Documents

You'll need, as originals with copies <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>:

- a passport valid 12 months;
- Form IM.4 (any Immigration office);
- old and new passports (data page and endorsed pages);
- the sponsor's MyKad and declaration;
- 2 white-background passport photos each of you and the sponsor.

Each kind of applicant adds <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>:

- **a spouse:**
  - the information form;
  - the marriage certificate registered in Malaysia;
  - a declaration that the marriage continues;
  - MyKad copies of 2 Malaysian referees;
  - children's birth certificates (and MyKad from 12);
  - proof of address (a tenancy or purchase agreement or a utility bill);
  - the spouse LTSVP deposit receipt;
- **a child under 18:**
  - the foreign parent's passport;
  - the foreign birth certificate;
  - any Malaysian birth registration;
  - the parents' marriage certificates;
  - proof of address;
  - the sponsor's proof of income;
- **a fully foreign applicant:**
  - the information form;
  - 2 referees' MyKad copies.

Copies of Bangladeshi documents (passport, marriage and birth certificates) need attestation by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs or the Bangladesh High Commission, translation into English or Malay, then attestation by a Malaysian mission or Malaysia's Foreign Ministry <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.

<a id="how-to-apply-1"></a>

#### 16.6.3 How to apply

1. Download IMI's Entry Permit guideline e-book from [imi.gov.my/index.php/perkhidmatan-utama/permit-masuk](https://www.imi.gov.my/index.php/perkhidmatan-utama/permit-masuk/) <sup>[293](#o293)</sup>.
2. Go with your sponsor and all originals to the nearest Immigration office <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>. A fully foreign Bangladeshi pays a RM750 deposit, refundable after 2 years on the permit <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.
3. You and the sponsor are interviewed (not a child), and police run a security check <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.
4. Six months after the acknowledgement, check at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/myPermit?semakStatusPermit](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/myPermit?semakStatusPermit); the letter goes to the sponsor <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.
5. If approved, go with your sponsor to the nearest Immigration office with their identity card, your passport (valid over 12 months) and the letter to amend your current pass <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.
6. Within 30 days, go together to the Visa, Pass and Permit Division at Immigration headquarters, Putrajaya, with the checklist documents, pay RM120 by card, and collect the permit, which never expires <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>. Uncollected within 6 months of the letter without good reason, the approval lapses <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.
7. With each new passport, get a new Entry Permit notice stamp at the nearest Immigration office; after more than 6 months abroad it is held while Immigration investigates <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.

<a id="if-refused"></a>

#### 16.6.4 If refused

Within 30 days of the refusal letter, write to the Home Minister through the Chief Secretary, Ministry of Home Affairs, Bahagian Hal Ehwal Imigresen, Aras 4, Setia Perkasa 2, Kompleks Setia Perkasa, 62546 Putrajaya; questions to 03-8886 3316, 3319 or 3170 or rayuanpermitmasuk@moha.gov.my. You can reapply after 1 year <sup>[156](#o156), [294](#o294)</sup>.

<a id="macs"></a>

### 16.7 MACS

A fast lane at two Johor checkpoints (Bangunan Sultan Iskandar, Johor Bahru; Kompleks Sultan Abu Bakar, Tanjung Kupang) for frequent Malaysia–Singapore travellers; it doesn't replace any visa or pass <sup>[295](#o295)</sup>. A foreigner on a long-term pass (Employment, Professional Visit, Student or PLKS) involved in developing Iskandar Malaysia (the Iskandar Regional Development Authority's region) can get a Category B sticker <sup>[295](#o295), [246](#o246)</sup>. To apply <sup>[295](#o295)</sup>:

1. Book on IMI's appointment system at [sto.imi.gov.my/STO/home.php](https://sto.imi.gov.my/STO/home.php).
2. Fill in the [MACS form](https://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/pdf-NEW-MACS-FORM-1.pdf).
3. Apply at the MACS counter, Level 1, Wisma Sunway Big Box, Iskandar Puteri, Johor (+607-2333016).

> **Need to know**
> - What is the MACS fee?
> - How long does it take?

<a id="changing-visa-type"></a>

## 17 Changing visa type

- **Visit pass (tourist or family visit) to PLKS (company worker's pass):** You can't switch inside Malaysia. The employer gets the VDR and PLKS while you are outside Malaysia; you enter only after approval <sup>[9](#o9), [5](#o5)</sup> (see [Work visa: company worker (PLKS)](#work-visa-company-worker-plks)).
- **Visit pass to Longer visit pass:** You can get a longer pass only for illness, an accident or war at home, with proof and a ticket home or onward <sup>[296](#o296)</sup>.
- **Visit pass to DE Rantau Nomad Pass:** You can't switch. A tourist pass can't be converted <sup>[279](#o279)</sup> (see [DE Rantau Nomad Pass](#de-rantau-nomad-pass)).
- **Visit pass to Student Pass:** You can't switch. You must be outside Malaysia when the application is submitted <sup>[52](#o52)</sup> (see [Student visa (Student Pass)](#student-visa-student-pass)).
- **Visit pass to Professional job up to 30 days:** This can only be arranged before you travel. The company gets PLS@Xpats approval (see [PLS@XPATS](#plsxpats)) <sup>[222 (2022)](#o222)</sup>; without the approval letter you won't be let in for this work <sup>[222 (2022)](#o222)</sup>. For longer work, see [Professional Visit Pass](#professional-visit-pass).
- **Student Pass to Student Pass for a higher level:** You can switch. Apply for Progression (for example bachelor's to master's) within 3 months of finishing; you may stay while it is processed <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>.
- **Student Pass to Work pass:** You can switch only through an employer. The employer applies, for example for an Employment Pass <sup>[297](#o297)</sup>. Bangladeshis can't get the Graduate Pass <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>.
- **PLKS to Another employer or sector:** You can move only with the Home Ministry's permission <sup>[9](#o9)</sup> (see [If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer)).
- **Dependant Pass to Work:** You can work only on an Employment Pass. The employer applies for an Employment Pass for the dependant <sup>[234](#o234), [252](#o252)</sup> (see [Family of an Employment Pass holder](#family-of-an-employment-pass-holder)).
- **Long-Term Social Visit Pass, family of an Employment Pass holder to Work or study:** You can work or study only by converting to another pass. An employer applies for an Employment Pass, or you get a Student Pass <sup>[234](#o234)</sup> (see [Employment Pass](#employment-pass); see [Student visa (Student Pass)](#student-visa-student-pass)).
- **Long-Term Social Visit Pass, spouse of a Malaysian to Work:** You can work. Get the work endorsement in your passport <sup>[196](#o196)</sup> (see [Spouse of a Malaysian citizen](#spouse-of-a-malaysian-citizen)).
- **Any pass, applicant outside Malaysia to Employment Pass:** You can get one. The employer applies for the VDR with the Employment Pass <sup>[239](#o239)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a Student Pass holder switch to an Employment Pass without leaving Malaysia?
> - Can a PLKS worker switch to an Employment Pass without leaving Malaysia?

<a id="asylum"></a>

## 18 Asylum

Asylum gives no right to stay or work in Malaysia <sup>[298](#o298), [299](#o299)</sup>. There is no national asylum system and the Immigration Act has no provision on refugees; UNHCR (the UN refugee agency) decides claims under its own mandate, and its recognition gives no legal immigration status <sup>[298](#o298), [39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. Refugees and asylum seekers are treated as undocumented migrants: they can be arrested, detained, fined and deported, and may not work <sup>[299](#o299)</sup>. UNHCR can't grant work permits or stop an arrest; its document is not a visa and at most may reduce the risk of arrest <sup>[299](#o299), [300](#o300)</sup>.

<a id="who-can-get-it"></a>

### 18.1 Who can get it

Refugee status is only for someone who fled persecution or serious harm because of race, religion, nationality, social group or political opinion, or who can't return because of war or serious disorder; someone who left for work or study is a migrant <sup>[298](#o298)</sup>.

<a id="how-to-apply-2"></a>

### 18.2 How to apply

1. Apply only on UNHCR's My Services portal, reached from help.unhcr.org/malaysia ("Registering with UNHCR"): create a personal account and submit the request; you get a reference number to quote in every contact, and letters are not accepted <sup>[300](#o300)</sup>.
2. Wait to be called for an interview; don't go to the Reception Centre without an appointment <sup>[300](#o300)</sup>. UNHCR deals first with requests from 2022 or earlier and the most vulnerable, and can't say when anyone will be interviewed <sup>[300](#o300), [301](#o301), [298](#o298)</sup>.
3. At the interview you must tell the truth; being coached or misleading UNHCR harms the case <sup>[298](#o298)</sup>.

An unregistered pregnant asylum seeker pays full foreigners' fees for antenatal care and childbirth at government facilities <sup>[301](#o301)</sup>.

UNHCR Malaysia, 570 Jalan Bukit Petaling, 50460 Kuala Lumpur, 03 2118 4800; detention hotline +6012 630 5060 <sup>[302 (2024)](#o302)</sup>.

<a id="if-it-is-refused"></a>

### 18.3 If it is refused

You can appeal once, within 30 days; if you don't, or the appeal fails, the case closes and UNHCR's help and documents end <sup>[298](#o298)</sup>.

<a id="scams-10"></a>

### 18.4 Scams

All UNHCR services are free; anyone asking money for an interview or letters is lying <sup>[300](#o300), [298](#o298)</sup>. Fake UNHCR cards and work permits are sold <sup>[N314](#n314)</sup>. Scan a card's large QR code with the free UNHCR Verify Plus app (Google Play or App Store); "Incompatible QR Code" means it is not genuine <sup>[302 (2024)](#o302)</sup>.

<a id="having-a-child-in-malaysia"></a>

## 19 Having a child in Malaysia

**PLKS workers** may not marry in Malaysia or bring family, and a worker who marries may lose the pass; a pregnancy confirmed at the FOMEMA medical means being sent home (Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Bringing your family) <sup>[9](#o9), [37 (2022)](#o37), [40 (2022)](#o40), [24](#o24), [137](#o137)</sup>.

The steps below are for parents lawfully in Malaysia with family, such as an Employment Pass holder with dependants or the spouse of a Malaysian <sup>[252](#o252), [196](#o196)</sup>.

1. <a id="having-a-child-in-malaysia-item-1"></a>**Register the birth with JPN** (Jabatan Pendaftaran Negara, the National Registration Department) within 60 days: free, then RM50 as a late registration <sup>[157](#o157), [303](#o303)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>:
   - form JPN.LM01 (original; take it at the JPN office, as the website copy is only a guide);
   - the hospital's birth confirmation form, or a doctor's letter for a home birth;
   - the mother's antenatal card, both passports and the marriage certificate (originals and copies);
   - an AM80 statutory declaration if there was no antenatal care;
   - a utility bill if you live away from the address on your documents.

   1. Find your office at [jpn.gov.my/hubungi-kami/direktori-cawangan](https://www.jpn.gov.my/hubungi-kami/direktori-cawangan/) (choose your state) <sup>[304](#o304)</sup>. Counters open Monday to Friday 8 am to 4.45 pm in Johor, Melaka, Negeri Sembilan, Pahang, Penang, Perak, Perlis, Selangor, Kuala Lumpur and Putrajaya; in Kedah, Kelantan and Terengganu, Sunday to Wednesday 8 am to 4.45 pm and Thursday 8 am to 3.15 pm <sup>[305](#o305)</sup>.
   2. A parent, the guardian, or someone who knew of or saw the birth hands in the documents and gets the birth certificate <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

   A child born before the parents' marriage was registered takes the mother's status <sup>[306](#o306)</sup>. The child is not Malaysian unless a parent is a citizen or permanent resident at the birth, or the child would otherwise be stateless <sup>[307](#o307)</sup>; JPN decides from the parents' status <sup>[306](#o306)</sup>. Act A1752, not yet in force, will limit this to a citizen parent <sup>[307](#o307)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does a birth in a hospital need a police report before JPN registers it?
   > - How long does JPN take to issue the birth certificate?
   > - How can parents check that a JPN birth certificate is genuine?
   > - Is Act A1752 in force yet?

2. <a id="having-a-child-in-malaysia-item-2"></a>**Register the birth with Bangladesh,** which Bangladeshi law requires for a child born abroad <sup>[308](#o308)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[308](#o308)</sup>:
   - the JPN certificate attested by Wisma Putra (Malaysia's foreign ministry);
   - copies of both parents' passports;
   - the marriage certificate (kabinnama), e-apostilled (authenticated online by Bangladesh's foreign ministry), or if older, attested by the Law and then Foreign Ministry in Dhaka;
   - the signed printed application;
   - 2 recent passport-size colour photos on white.

   1. **Attest the JPN certificate** at Wisma Putra's Consular Division (address and hours under [Family visit sponsored by a Malaysian citizen (VDR), Step 1, item 1](#step-1-send-your-papers-to-the-sponsor-item-1)): you or someone with your authorisation letter brings an identity card or passport, and usually collects it the same or next working day <sup>[214 (2023)](#o214)</sup>.
   2. **E-apostille the marriage certificate** through the Ministry of Foreign Affairs' e-Apostille service on MyGov, [mygov.bd](https://www.mygov.bd/services?category=sector&data=service-sector-67): up to five days, with a QR code for checking <sup>[309](#o309)</sup>.
   3. **Apply** at [bdris.gov.bd](https://bdris.gov.bd/) (Bangladesh's birth and death registration system) with your permanent Bangladesh address, uploading the child's foreign birth certificate and a parent's passport or NID; without uploads it is rejected <sup>[310](#o310), [308](#o308)</sup>.
   4. The child and both parents take the printed, signed application and documents to the Bangladesh High Commission, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[308](#o308), [125](#o125)</sup>, and pay RM5 by card at the consular counter or by deposit to its Maybank account 564427102268 <sup>[308](#o308)</sup>.
   5. **Check it is real** at [everify.bdris.gov.bd](https://everify.bdris.gov.bd/), which shows whether a certificate is digital (17 digits) <sup>[310](#o310)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does Wisma Putra charge to attest the JPN certificate?
   > - What does the e-Apostille of the marriage certificate cost?
   > - How long does the High Commission take to register the birth?
   > - How is the Bangladeshi birth certificate collected?

3. <a id="having-a-child-in-malaysia-item-3"></a>**Get the child a Bangladeshi passport:** a 48-page e-passport valid 5 years for anyone under 18 <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[311 (2024)](#o311), [154](#o154)</sup>:
   - the child in person;
   - the JPN certificate (original, plus a copy attested by JPN);
   - the Bangladeshi birth registration in English showing Malaysia as birthplace, with its online verification copy;
   - both parents' NID numbers, NID copies and online-verified NID copies;
   - copies of both parents' passports and visas;
   - the marriage certificate attested by the Law Ministry, the Foreign Ministry and the High Commission;
   - a photo of each parent;
   - under age 6, a 3R or 4R lab-printed photo on grey.

   1. Book the compulsory appointment with ESKL (Expat Services (Kuala Lumpur) Sdn. Bhd., the High Commission's passport company) at [expatservicesmy.com/#/book-appointment](https://www.expatservicesmy.com/#/book-appointment) or 03-92120267 <sup>[311 (2024)](#o311), [154](#o154)</sup>, then go to Southgate Commercial Centre, Level 2, Block E, No 2, Jalan Dua, off Jalan Chan Sow Lin, 55200 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.
   2. Pay RM545 (RM485 government fee plus RM60 ESKL charge) <sup>[311 (2024)](#o311), [154](#o154)</sup>; a Maybank cash deposit goes to 564427102268 with the child's name on the receipt <sup>[311 (2024)](#o311)</sup>.
   3. Track it at [epassport.gov.bd/authorization/application-status](https://www.epassport.gov.bd/authorization/application-status) with the Application or Online Registration ID and the child's date of birth <sup>[312](#o312)</sup>; questions to the passport WhatsApp, +60104303020 <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How long does the child's passport take to issue?
   > - How is the child's passport collected?
   > - How can parents spot a fake passport agent?

<a id="sending-money-home"></a>

## 20 Sending money home

**Use a licensed remitter, never hundi** (sending through an unlicensed agent) <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. In 2022 Bangladesh Bank was reported to warn that hundi is a punishable offence, and money received in about 480 bKash and Nagad agent accounts suspended over hundi could not be cashed <sup>[N221 (2022)](#n221)</sup>. A money-sending shop must display its Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM, the central bank) licence or its certificate as a licensed company's agent; without one it is not authorised <sup>[313](#o313)</sup>. Licensed remitters such as MoneyGram, Western Union, NBL Money Transfer, Agrani Remittance House, Merchantrade and CBL Money Transfer cost about 5% to 5.5% to send RM610; banks about 9% to 16% <sup>[314](#o314)</sup>.

You'll need:
- the receiver's bKash number (a Bangladeshi mobile-money account) and full name as registered with bKash <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>;
- for Touch 'n Go eWallet (a Malaysian wallet app), a passport valid 12 more months and a valid work visa <sup>[N380](#n380)</sup>.

1. <a id="sending-money-home-item-1"></a>**Check the licence** in BNM's List of MSB (money services business) Licensees, [bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/msb-licensees](https://www.bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/msb-licensees), which also lists head offices and branches, or for an agent shop the List of Approved MSB Agents, [bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/approved-msb-agents](https://www.bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/approved-msb-agents) <sup>[315](#o315), [316](#o316), [317](#o317)</sup>.
2. <a id="sending-money-home-item-2"></a>**Send.** Ria, CBL Money Transfer, Merchantrade, NBL Money Transfer, WorldRemit, Wise, Touch 'n Go eWallet (no Malaysian bank account needed), Western Union, MoneyGram and others send to bKash <sup>[38](#o38), [N380](#n380)</sup>. At a partner bank, exchange house or agent, give the bKash number and registered name and hand over the cash <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>. Up to Tk 250,000 per transfer, any number of times <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>.
3. <a id="sending-money-home-item-3"></a>**The family** gets the government's 2.5% incentive on legal remittances <sup>[38](#o38), [N220](#n220)</sup> and can cash out at BRAC Bank, City Bank and Q-Cash ATMs for Tk 7 per thousand <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>. Money can also be paid out through Bangladesh Krishi Bank, which has 1,038 branches and 5 sub-branches across Bangladesh, more than any other bank in remote areas <sup>[N381](#n381)</sup>. Krishi Bank, the second-largest collector of remittances in January to November 2025 (USD 277 crore), receives the most from Malaysia's Merchantrade, and also from CBL Money and NBL Money in Malaysia; its general manager said in December 2025 that families can now receive the money directly into their own account, 24 hours a day through Bangladesh Bank, instead of collecting it with a secret PIN <sup>[N381](#n381)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does the remittance receipt show, such as a tracking reference?
> - How long does money sent to bKash from Malaysia take to arrive?

<a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive"></a>

### 20.1 If the money doesn't arrive

1. Complain to the remitter's or bank's Complaints Unit by phone (note the officer's name, date and time), then in writing with your name, account number and receipt copies; keep a copy. BNM won't take a complaint that skipped this step <sup>[316](#o316), [318](#o318)</sup>.
2. No reply in 14 days: go to BNMLINK, BNM's customer centre, at [bnmlink.bnm.gov.my](https://bnmlink.bnm.gov.my/) or Ground Floor, D Block, Bank Negara Malaysia, Jalan Dato' Onn, 50480 Kuala Lumpur (9 am to 5 pm, Monday to Friday), or call BNMTELELINK, 1-300-88-5465 <sup>[318](#o318), [316](#o316)</sup>. For a bank, also ABMConnect (Association of Banks in Malaysia), 1-300-88-9980 <sup>[316](#o316)</sup>.

Report hundi businesses to BNMTELELINK, 1-300-88-5465 <sup>[316](#o316)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does BNMLINK take complaints against a licensed remitter?
> - Which Bangladesh Bank circular sets this year's 2.5% incentive?
> - How can a worker without a valid pass send money home legally?

<a id="saving-and-pension-in-bangladesh"></a>

### 20.2 Saving and pension in Bangladesh

Bangladesh's Probash pension takes monthly payments from a worker abroad and pays a monthly pension from age 60; the rates and how to join are under [Your rights at work, Probash pension (Bangladesh)](#probash-pension-bangladesh) <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>. Your EPF retirement savings in Malaysia come back in full when you leave for good ([Leaving at the end of the job, item 4: Withdraw your EPF before you fly](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-4)) <sup>[145](#o145)</sup>.

Bangladesh Krishi Bank has run several savings schemes for migrants, which held about Tk 220 crore by December 2025 <sup>[N381](#n381)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which Bangladeshi savings schemes or bonds for migrants can a worker in Malaysia buy, and how?
> - What are Krishi Bank's savings schemes for migrants, and can a worker in Malaysia join one from abroad <sup>[N381](#n381)</sup>?

<a id="going-home-without-valid-papers"></a>

## 21 Going home without valid papers

Without a valid pass you can go home under Malaysia's repatriation programme, PRM 2.0 (Program Repatriasi Migran 2.0), 1 May 2026 to 31 May 2027, at Immigration offices in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan <sup>[319](#o319), [47](#o47)</sup>, paying a compound (a fixed fine instead of prosecution) <sup>[47](#o47), [N382](#n382)</sup>. Who is excluded is under [Step 3: Go to Immigration](#step-3-go-to-immigration), Who can't use PRM 2.0. Otherwise it is arrest, detention in an immigration depot until removal, and deportation (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39), [N383](#n383)</sup>.

PRM 2.0 only lets you leave; no legalisation programme is open (see [What running away or an expired pass does to you](#what-running-away-or-an-expired-pass-does-to-you)) <sup>[319](#o319), [N235](#n235)</sup>. Anyone but an employer offering to get you a pass is breaking the law (see [Going home without valid papers, Scams](#scams-11)) <sup>[90 (2021)](#o90)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Will Malaysia open a new legalisation round, now that Bangladesh asked it in August 2026 to register undocumented Bangladeshis <sup>[N384](#n384)</sup>?

<a id="help-from-bangladesh"></a>

### 21.1 Help from Bangladesh

A close relative can ask the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment to help bring a worker home; the Ministry arranges the return through the High Commission, the Welfare Board or BMET, free, within 7 working days of receiving the letter <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>:
- proof the person is a migrant worker;
- their workplace and home address in Malaysia;
- the recruiting agency's name and address;
- the employer's mobile number.

1. Write to the Ministry, Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000, or secretary@probashi.gov.bd; phone +88-02-41030444 <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.
2. No reply: the grievance officer, Joint Secretary (Admin and Finance), 02-41030224, 01712992086, adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd, has 30 working days <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Ministry acknowledge a family's application?
> - How can the family check the application's progress?
> - Does the Ministry pay the ticket, the PRM 2.0 compound or the Travel Permit fee, or only contact the High Commission?

**Case: a worker found by other migrants.** Amir Hossain, who went to Malaysia through a broker in 1996 after selling inherited farmland and worked as a painter, was traced about six months before he came home in April 2026 after two Bangladeshis in Malaysia, an expatriate named Dipu and an expatriate journalist, Bappi Kumar Das, found him in a tin hut in a Penang forest, rescued him and posted video on social media, from which his wife recognised him <sup>[N385](#n385)</sup>. How his papers were made is under [Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit](#step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit) <sup>[N385](#n385)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit"></a>

### 21.2 Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit

With a valid passport, go to [Step 2: Buy a ticket home](#step-2-buy-a-ticket-home). If it has expired or been lost or damaged, get a Travel Permit (a one-way "out pass" home) from the Bangladesh High Commission <sup>[308](#o308)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[308](#o308)</sup>:
- a copy of your old passport;
- a copy of your NID or online birth registration;
- two colour photos;
- your phone number.

Without an NID, a citizenship certificate from your UNO (Upazila Nirbahi Officer, the sub-district's chief officer), or the Deputy Commissioner for Cox's Bazar, emailed to the High Commission's labour wing at counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, ss.lab.kl@gmail.com and fsl.bhc.kl@gmail.com <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.

1. Go yourself (no one can go for you) to the High Commission, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur, on an office day, 9:30 am to 1 pm <sup>[308](#o308), [125](#o125), [130](#o130)</sup>.
2. Pay RM38, the only fee, to the High Commission itself, by card at the consular counter or by deposit to Maybank account 564427102268 <sup>[320](#o320), [308](#o308)</sup>.
3. Collect it, usually the same day after 4 pm; it is valid 3 months <sup>[130](#o130), [308](#o308)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker in Malaysia get the UNO citizenship certificate?
> - What does the UNO certificate cost?
> - How long does the UNO certificate take?
> - Does the Travel Permit take 1 to 2 working days rather than the same day, which matters before you date your ticket <sup>[321 (2023)](#o321)</sup>?

**Case: proving who you are without a passport or NID.** Amir Hossain of Naria, Shariatpur, was found mentally unwell in a forest in Penang after 27 years out of contact. The Bangladesh mission in Malaysia sent his photo and the information he gave to the Naria UNO, who verified it with the family; Chamta Union Parishad, with the upazila administration's help, issued him a birth registration; that and family members' documents were sent to the mission, which issued a travel pass; he reached Dhaka in the night after 21 April 2026, about six months after he was found <sup>[N385](#n385)</sup>.

**If it fails:** the consular section, +60326040949 <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>, or the High Commission's labour wing, which has two Ministers (Labour): Syed Shariful Islam, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, +601 2290 3252, and Md. Siddiqur Rahman, ministermalaysia@probashi.gov.bd, +603 2604 0934 <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-lose-your-passport-in-malaysia"></a>

#### 21.2.1 If you lose your passport in Malaysia

You'll need <sup>[162](#o162)</sup>:
- the passport number or a copy;
- your new passport or Travel Permit ([Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit](#step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit));
- a police report;
- a plane, bus or ferry ticket out dated within 7 days.

1. Report the loss at any police station, orally to the officer (who writes it and reads it back) or on form Pol. 55, free at the counter; any report must be in Malay or English; buy a certified copy for RM2, at once or within 3 days <sup>[165](#o165)</sup>.
2. Report at once, yourself, without agents, to the Enforcement Division at your state Immigration office ([Step 3: Go to Immigration](#step-3-go-to-immigration)) <sup>[162](#o162)</sup>.
3. Pay RM100 for a Special Pass to leave within 7 days <sup>[162](#o162)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does Immigration take to issue the Special Pass after a lost passport?
> - If you also have no valid pass, do you pay this RM100 as well as the PRM 2.0 compound and RM20 Special Pass ([Step 3, item 3](#step-3-go-to-immigration)), or instead of them?

<a id="step-2-buy-a-ticket-home"></a>

### 21.3 Step 2: Buy a ticket home

You'll need: your passport or Travel Permit, and the fare.

1. Buy a confirmed ticket to Bangladesh dated 5 to 14 days after the day you go to Immigration <sup>[47](#o47), [N386](#n386), [N387](#n387)</sup>.
2. **Check it is real** on the airline's website with the six-character PNR (booking reference) and your last name; on Biman, "Manage Booking" <sup>[N309](#n309)</sup>.
3. **If you'll miss the date,** change it beforehand: on Biman, Manage Booking, Exchange, new date, pay any fare difference or fee <sup>[N309](#n309)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where can a worker buy the ticket home safely?
> - What is the usual fare from Malaysia to Bangladesh?
> - Does Immigration accept a ticket whose date was changed?

<a id="step-3-go-to-immigration"></a>

### 21.4 Step 3: Go to Immigration

You'll need <sup>[N386](#n386), [47](#o47)</sup>:
- your original passport or Travel Permit;
- a copy of the photo page;
- a copy of your last Malaysian pass;
- old and new passports if you have both;
- the confirmed ticket;
- a debit or credit card or Touch 'n Go eWallet with enough for the compound.

1. Go yourself, without appointment, to one of the 14 Immigration Enforcement offices (headquarters and state offices, listed under Offices below); no one can apply for you <sup>[319](#o319), [47](#o47), [N386](#n386), [N387](#n387)</sup>. Contacts: [imi.gov.my/index.php/hubungi-kami-2/hubungi-kami](https://www.imi.gov.my/index.php/hubungi-kami-2/hubungi-kami/).
2. Arrive early: at Seremban, queues start before the 8 am opening <sup>[N388](#n388)</sup>.
3. Pay the compound by debit card, credit card or Touch 'n Go eWallet only <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>:
   - RM500 for entering or staying without a valid pass or for overstaying;
   - RM300 for breaking pass conditions.

   Plus RM20 for the Special Pass (Pas Khas, a pass to leave within a set time); under-18s pay only the RM20 <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.
4. Leave on your ticket within the Special Pass's time <sup>[47](#o47), [130](#o130)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What are the opening hours of the other Immigration offices?

**Who can't use PRM 2.0** <sup>[N239](#n239), [N240](#n240)</sup>:
- anyone who registered in an earlier programme but didn't leave;
- anyone blacklisted by Immigration;
- anyone wanted or under an arrest warrant.

A worker reported absent (lari) by the employer is blacklisted (see If your employer reports you absent (lari)) <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24)</sup>, and blacklisted people are excluded <sup>[N239](#n239), [N240](#n240)</sup>, so a lari worker appears not to qualify. An overstaying spouse of a Malaysian goes instead to Immigration's Visa, Pass and Permit Division <sup>[N240](#n240)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do these rules still apply in the PRM 2.0 extension to May 2027?
> - How can a worker reported as lari leave Malaysia legally?

**Offices:**

- Putrajaya (headquarters): No. 15, Tingkat 1-7, Blok 2G4 (Menara & Podium), Persiaran Perdana, Presint 2, 62550 Putrajaya, 03-8000 8000 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Kuala Lumpur: Kompleks KDN, No 69, Jalan Sri Hartamas 1, off Jalan Duta, 50550 Kuala Lumpur, 03-62057400 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Selangor: Tingkat 2, Kompleks PKNS, 40550 Shah Alam, 03-5650 0000 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Negeri Sembilan: Wisma Persekutuan, Jalan Dato' Abdul Kadir, 70000 Seremban, 06-7620000 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Johor: Blok 1, Kompleks KDN, Taman Setia Tropika, 81200 Johor Bahru, 07-2338400 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Penang: Jalan Kelasah, 13700 Seberang Jaya, 04-3973011 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Perak: Kompleks KDN, Persiaran Meru Utama, Bandar Meru Raya, 30020 Ipoh, 05-5017100 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Pahang: Kompleks KDN, Bandar Indera Mahkota, 25200 Kuantan, 09-5717999 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Melaka: Kompleks KDN, Jalan Seri Negeri, Hang Tuah Jaya, 75450 Ayer Keroh, 06-2322662 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Kedah: Bangunan KDN, Bandar Mu'adzam Shah, 06550 Alor Setar, 04-7333302 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Perlis: Kompleks KDN, Persiaran Wawasan, 01000 Kangar, 04-9762636 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Terengganu: Wisma Persekutuan, Jalan Sultan Ismail, 20200 Kuala Terengganu, 09-6221424 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Kelantan: Kompleks Imigresen Negeri Kelantan, Jalan Hospital, 15000 Kota Bharu, 09-7482120 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- Labuan: Unit E002, Tingkat 1, Aras Podium, Kompleks Ujana Kewangan, Jalan Merdeka, 87008 Labuan, 087-425 307 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.

<a id="scams-11"></a>

#### 21.4.1 Scams

PRM is run by Immigration alone, with no agents; don't pay any broker or anyone asking money for special treatment <sup>[N387](#n387), [N386](#n386)</sup>.

**If something goes wrong:**

- PRM line, 03-8880 1269 <sup>[319](#o319)</sup>.
- The High Commission's labour wing: either Minister (Labour) ([Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit](#step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit), "If it fails") <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>.
- Family in Bangladesh: Probashi call centre (Wage Earners' Welfare Board), 16135 free, +8809610102030 from abroad, 24 hours <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.
- Tenaganita (see [Where to complain](#where-to-complain-in-malaysia-and-from-bangladesh), in Malaysia and from Bangladesh, Free help in Malaysia): +603 7770 3671; emergency +6012 335 0512 and +6012 339 5350 <sup>[N343](#n343)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-detained"></a>

### 21.5 If you are detained

With a valid passport a detainee needs only a ticket, and the depot takes them to the airport and clears them <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. Without a passport, the camp sends the Travel Permit application and the High Commission issues the Travel Permit to the camp <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. High Commission officers visit prisons as well as detention camps <sup>[N331](#n331)</sup>. For family contact with a detainee, see [What can get you jailed or deported, After arrest](#after-arrest).

> **Need to know**
> - How does family visit a detainee in an Immigration depot?

<a id="bans-after-you-leave"></a>

### 21.6 Bans after you leave

How long a ban follows leaving under PRM 2.0 isn't published (see the Need to know below). Deported people are blacklisted for 1 to 5 years by offence, or for life in serious cases, and the blacklist records fingerprints (BioSL) <sup>[N389](#n389), [N390](#n390), [N276](#n276), [N298 (2017)](#n298), [N391 (2024)](#n391)</sup>. Immigration's Director General can bar a person for a set period or for life, and a person whose pass is cancelled is removed and barred <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. A ban or pass cancellation can be appealed within 7 days by a written petition, with the grounds in detail, to the Minister of Home Affairs <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>: Blok D1 & D2, Kompleks D, Pusat Pentadbiran Kerajaan Persekutuan, 62546 Putrajaya, 03-8886 8000 <sup>[322](#o322)</sup>. Removal for an expired or cancelled pass can't be appealed <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. Returning unlawfully after removal can bring a fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail and whipping <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long is the ban after leaving under PRM 2.0?
> - Does the petition go to the Ministry of Home Affairs directly or through Immigration?
> - What does the petition cost?

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job"></a>

## 22 If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

PERKESO (SOCSO, see [After you arrive, item 7](#after-you-arrive-item-7)) pays for injuries, disability and death in Malaysia, and the labour wing chases employers for compensation <sup>[166](#o166), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>. In Bangladesh the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (the Welfare Board; Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Eskaton Garden, Ramna, Dhaka) pays grants and handles the insurance bought at BMET clearance, which Jiban Bima Corporation, the state life insurer, pays out <sup>[323](#o323), [237](#o237), [324](#o324), [N392](#n392)</sup>.

On 5 October 2024 the Welfare Board and PERKESO signed a cooperation memorandum on the health, occupational safety and welfare of Bangladeshi workers in Malaysia, under which every Bangladeshi worker there is to be registered with PERKESO <sup>[N34 (2024)](#n34)</sup>. As stated at the signing, 1,215,536 Bangladeshi workers had registered with PERKESO, 711,655 of them active; by August 2024, 11,439 had received temporary disablement benefit totalling about RM15 million (Tk 37 crore), 364 permanent disablement benefit totalling RM1.27 million, and 175 funeral benefit totalling RM870,000 <sup>[N34 (2024)](#n34)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1"></a>

### 22.1 If you are injured or fall ill

1. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-1"></a>**Get treatment.** Free at a government clinic or hospital, or at a PERKESO panel clinic (a private clinic PERKESO appoints), which after a workplace accident checks your cover itself on PERKESO's SEHATi system <sup>[166](#o166), [325](#o325)</sup>. Panel clinics are listed by state (Selangor: perkeso.gov.my/images/Klinik_Panel/Senarai_Klinik/SENARAI_KLINIK_PANEL_SELANGOR.pdf); PERKESO's line is 1-300-22-8000 <sup>[326](#o326), [325](#o325)</sup>. Non-panel clinic costs can be claimed back from PERKESO <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>. At government hospitals SPIKPA covers you without a deposit (see [After you arrive, item 7](#after-you-arrive-item-7)), and sick leave must be reported within 48 hours (see [Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[144](#o144), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Where is a current list of PERKESO panel clinics for each state?

2. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-2"></a>**Make sure the accident is reported and the claim filed.** The employer must report it to PERKESO at once, within 30 days at the latest, or face a fine or prosecution <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>. Covered: accidents at work, between home and work and during an emergency, work diseases, and since 1 June 2026 accidents outside work <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>. The claim is on Form 34-PA (perkeso.gov.my/images/borang/Borang34/290626-BORANG_34-PA.pdf); the worker or dependant signs as claimant, and the employer fills in its sections if you still work there <sup>[328](#o328), [329](#o329)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>:
   - Form 34-PA;
   - passport and PLKS copies;
   - your medical or sick-leave certificate;
   - for a road accident, the original police report or a copy certified by the issuing police station.

   Quote your 12-digit FWSS (Foreign Workers Social Security) number in every dealing with PERKESO <sup>[141 (2019)](#o141)</sup>.

   - Where and when: hand it in at a PERKESO counter and keep the stamped slip, or post it to the nearest PERKESO office (the form asks which office you want to deal with); head office: Menara PERKESO, 281 Jalan Ampang, 50538 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[327](#o327), [329](#o329), [330](#o330)</sup>. File within 12 months of the benefit falling due (the appeal board can extend this for good reason); incomplete, unclear or late claims can be rejected <sup>[331 (2019)](#o331), [327](#o327)</sup>.
   - Cost: free; don't use agents or middlemen who charge <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>. A false claim can bring up to 2 years' jail, a fine of up to RM10,000 or both <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>. Progress: 1 300 22 8000 or perkeso@perkeso.gov.my <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>.
   - If PERKESO refuses or underpays, appeal to the Social Security Appellate Board within 3 years; a Medical Board's disability assessment goes to the Appellate Medical Board <sup>[331 (2019)](#o331)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does PERKESO accept Form 34-PA without the employer's sections when the employer refuses to sign?
   > - How does a worker find the nearest PERKESO branch's address?
   > - How long does PERKESO take to decide a claim?
   > - How is an appeal filed to the Social Security Appellate Board?

3. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-3"></a>**What you can get.**
   - Temporary Disablement Benefit for medical leave of at least 4 days, counting the accident day <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.
   - For permanent disability, assessed by a Medical Board in Malaysia: up to 20% is paid as a lump sum; above 20%, one fifth can be taken as a lump sum and the rest monthly for life <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.
   - A 100% disabled worker needing constant care gets RM500 a month more, and PERKESO pays for physiotherapy, artificial limbs, wheelchairs and similar aids <sup>[166](#o166), [36](#o36)</sup>.
   - A worker certified invalid who hasn't paid Invalidity Scheme contributions long enough gets a one-time Invalidity Grant of both sides' contributions with interest <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.

   Payments can go to a bank outside Malaysia: give the account holder, bank, address, branch, account number and SWIFT code on the form, with a bank verification letter <sup>[329](#o329), [327](#o327)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-4"></a>**If the employer won't report it or pay you while you are off work,** file a case at the nearest Labour Department office (offices by state at jtksm.mohr.gov.my/ms/hubungi-kami/alamat-ibu-pejabat-dan-cawangan) with your payslips, contract and attendance record, and give a copy to the labour wing (Minister (Labour) Syed Shariful Islam, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, +60 12-290 3252), which pursues compensation claims <sup>[332](#o332), [131 (2024)](#o131), [111](#o111), [N341](#n341)</sup>. The Labour Court works as in route 3 (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)); if the employer ignores its Order, the Labour Department can seize and sell its property, take the money from its bank or prosecute it <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101)</sup>. Sample message to the labour wing: "I am [name], passport [number], PLKS with [employer, address]. I was injured at work on [date] at [place]. My employer has not reported it to PERKESO. Please help me claim." Tenaganita and the Bar Council's Legal Aid Centres help with cases (see [Where to complain](#where-to-complain-in-malaysia-and-from-bangladesh), in Malaysia and from Bangladesh, Free help in Malaysia) <sup>[N324](#n324), [N347](#n347)</sup>; the ILO (International Labour Organization) does not take complaints from individual workers <sup>[333](#o333)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does Tenaganita charge workers for its help?

5. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-5"></a>**Going home injured.** On your or your family's application, with proof of BMET clearance or Board membership and a passport copy, the Welfare Board asks the High Commission to bring you home <sup>[334](#o334)</sup>. The Board's ambulance at Dhaka and Chattogram airports is free on request <sup>[335](#o335)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-6"></a>**Treatment money in Bangladesh.** A returnee with BMET clearance or Board membership who is injured, sick or disabled can get up to Tk 2 lakh <sup>[336](#o336)</sup>. Within 1 year of return, the family applies to the Board's Director General via its web portal, the district probashi kallyan (migrant welfare) centre or the Board <sup>[336](#o336), [323](#o323)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[336](#o336), [323](#o323)</sup>:
   - medical reports and prescriptions;
   - a bank statement;
   - a passport copy and a copy of its arrival stamp;
   - proof of clearance or membership;
   - one 6R (6 by 8 inch) colour photo of the worker.

   A Board committee checks the case by WhatsApp video call and recommends an amount; free, up to 60 working days after all papers are in <sup>[336](#o336), [323](#o323)</sup>.
7. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-7"></a>**Insurance.** It pays Tk 10 lakh for total permanent disability and 10% to 90% of that for partial permanent disability <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>; see [Claiming the insurance](#claiming-the-insurance).

**Case:**

- **February 2026:** Md Jahangir Alam, 40, of Satkhira, who went to Malaysia through a broker at great cost in 2018, fell seriously ill there with a severe stomach problem and lost everything to brokers in the name of treatment in Malaysia without getting proper care; he came home on 17 February 2026 and died on 19 February at Dhaka Medical College Hospital, where no ICU bed could be found for him <sup>[N393](#n393)</sup>. His son, Afzal Hossain, 18, said the family had to mortgage land and sell cattle to pay for his treatment in Malaysia and his hospital costs after he came home, and that he had been unable to send money home regularly <sup>[N393](#n393)</sup>.

<a id="if-a-worker-dies"></a>

### 22.2 If a worker dies

These steps are for the family in Bangladesh.

1. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-1"></a>**Ask the Welfare Board to bring the body home.** Call 16135 (from abroad +8809610102030) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>. Then apply in one of these ways, even if the worker had no BMET clearance or Board membership <sup>[N394](#n394)</sup>:
   - on myGov (the government's online services portal), service "Application to bring home a migrant worker's body", https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1638167134, with a myGov account (register with your mobile number at idp-v2.live.mygov.bd/registration), the worker's passport and any clearance or membership; a tracking number comes by SMS and you can follow the case on myGov; free; up to 35 working days <sup>[337](#o337)</sup>; or
   - to the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, which also handles undocumented workers, describing what happened and the cause of death, with proof the person was a migrant worker (passport, work permit, BMET smart card, Malaysian ID, death certificate); free <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

   Sample: "My [relation], [name], passport [number], worked for [employer, phone] in [town], Malaysia, and died on [date]. We want the body brought home to [district]. The receiver is [name, phone]."
2. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-2"></a>**Home or burial in Malaysia.** The Board passes the family's choice to the High Commission, and the family sends the High Commission a claim or consent letter naming the receiver and their phone number <sup>[338](#o338), [339](#o339), [131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>. For burial in Malaysia, apply on myGov with the service "Application for permission to bury a migrant worker's body in the destination country", https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1638246958, free, 3 working days <sup>[340](#o340)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[340](#o340)</sup>:
   - the worker's passport or visa copy;
   - any clearance or membership;
   - the district office's investigation report;
   - a certificate signed by all heirs on the union parishad (rural local council) chairman's letterhead.
3. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-3"></a>**The Malaysian side.** The employer writes to the High Commission, appoints a funeral company and sends its letters, the insurance claim and compensation letters, the hospital letter, the death certificate, the Malaysian police report and the passport <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>. It pays the funeral and repatriation <sup>[37 (2022)](#o37)</sup>. For its no-objection certificate (NOC) the High Commission also needs <sup>[341 (2023)](#o341)</sup>:
   - the employer's PERKESO death report;
   - for an abnormal death (hanging, fall, accident, murder), a police report with NOC;
   - for an undocumented worker, the original passport (or a police report of its loss), the receiver's details, a police report with NOC, the original death certificate, the funeral company's letter and the employer's name and phone.
