   The Board forwards the request within 3 to 5 working days, and the labour wing takes about 30 <sup>[337](#o337)</sup>. An undocumented worker's body can take much longer to come home <sup>[N394](#n394)</sup>.

   If there is no employer or it refuses, the Welfare Board pays <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124), [131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>. PERKESO repays whoever paid (a person, company or the High Commission) up to RM4,500 of repatriation, even if the death isn't otherwise covered, on its PPJ (Funeral Repatriation Cost Claim) form, on which the employer states whether it paid; hand it in at a PERKESO counter or post it, with <sup>[166](#o166), [342 (2022)](#o342), [343 (2020)](#o343)</sup>:
   - a certified copy of the death certificate and of the claimant's ID or company registration;
   - the original receipt;
   - bank proof;
   - proof the body was sent home (the Ministry of Health's export permit or the airline's air waybill).

   Malaysian authorities trace the family through the permanent address and emergency contact in the worker's passport, so write a full name and a working phone number in the passport's emergency contact field; one body waited 15 days in a Kuala Lumpur morgue, at RM75 a day, because that field held only "0088" <sup>[316](#o316), [N395](#n395)</sup>. If no family is found, the worker may be buried in Malaysia as an unclaimed person <sup>[N395](#n395)</sup>.

   Death papers usually give the cause as illness or natural death <sup>[N394](#n394)</sup>; migrants say deaths are recorded as stroke or heart attack to avoid workplace-accident compensation, and Bangladesh doesn't check the cause <sup>[N394](#n394), [N396](#n396)</sup>. If the family doubts it, apply to the Welfare Board for legal help abroad: myGov https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1638179893, at the Board, or through the expatriate welfare branch of the Deputy Commissioner's office (the district administration) or the DEMO, with proof of clearance or membership and a passport or travel permit copy with arrival and departure stamps; free; sent to the labour wing within 1 to 2 days; up to 20 working days <sup>[344](#o344), [345](#o345)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can a family ask the Malaysian police for a post-mortem or inquest?
   > - How does the family make that request?

4. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-4"></a>**At the airport.** The Board's desk gives a Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial, collectable within 3 months, and a free ambulance <sup>[346](#o346), [335](#o335)</sup>. You'll need <sup>[347](#o347), [346](#o346)</sup>:
   - the worker's passport copy;
   - a heirship (warish) certificate from the union parishad chairman, mayor or city councillor;
   - the NID of the wife, father or mother;
   - the death certificate;
   - the NOC;
   - the original air waybill;
   - proof of clearance or membership;
   - a photo and NID or passport copy of the named receiver, and of the collector if different.
5. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-5"></a>**The Tk 3 lakh Welfare Board grant,** for the family of a worker with clearance or membership who died in Malaysia, the country of the clearance <sup>[237](#o237)</sup>. If the family took the airport cheque, no application is needed: the Board writes asking for the papers; after burial in Malaysia, apply within 1 year of the death to the Board's Director General or through the district office <sup>[237](#o237)</sup>. The grant goes to the spouse, children and parents; for an unmarried worker with no living parents, to full siblings <sup>[237](#o237)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[237](#o237), [323](#o323)</sup>:
   - a family-members certificate on the letterhead of the union parishad chairman, mayor or city councillor, countersigned by the UNO or, in a city corporation, the regional executive officer;
   - an indemnity, undertaking and authorisation on Tk 400 non-judicial stamp paper, countersigned the same way (samples on wewb.gov.bd);
   - the NOC or death certificate copy;
   - a bank certificate or original statement for the receiver's account with the routing number (minor children's own account where they apply);
   - each family member's colour photo, attested by the chairman and the district welfare centre;
   - the worker's passport copy.

   Paid by bank transfer (BEFTN), with an SMS, within 60 working days of all papers reaching the Board <sup>[237](#o237), [323](#o323)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-6"></a>**The insurance:** Tk 10 lakh for a death within 5 years of the smart card date <sup>[348](#o348), [21](#o21)</sup>; who is covered: see [Claiming the insurance](#claiming-the-insurance).
7. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-7"></a>**Malaysian compensation.** PERKESO pays:
   - a Dependants' Benefit for a work-related death <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>;
   - a funeral benefit against receipts of up to RM7,500 for burial in Bangladesh (up to RM4,500 to whoever paid repatriation, RM3,000 to the dependants) or up to RM3,000 for burial in Malaysia <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>;
   - if the worker died in Malaysia before 60 from any cause, a Survivors' Pension of at least RM550 a month, if Invalidity Scheme contributions were paid for at least 24 of the 40 consecutive months before the claim or two-thirds of the months since they became due <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.

   Claim within 12 months <sup>[331 (2019)](#o331)</sup>. You'll need <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>:
   - Form 34-PA;
   - the worker's passport and work pass copies;
   - the death certificate or burial permit;
   - proof of relationship (marriage certificate, widow's ID and children's birth certificates; or, with no wife or child, the worker's birth certificate and parents' IDs);
   - bank proof;
   - Bangladeshi papers translated into English by an accredited translator and certified by the High Commission or a Bangladesh authority <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>.

   For an accident, the Labour Department sends its Form H to the heirs, who fill it in and return it through the High Commission <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>.

   How it works: when the High Commission reports that compensation may be due, the Welfare Board asks the family for a power of attorney and the papers, with free help from its legal cell, sends them to the High Commission and follows the case; the labour wing recovers compensation and unpaid wages, and the Board pays them to the heirs within 60 working days of having the papers and the money <sup>[349](#o349), [323](#o323), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>. The family can also ask the Ministry (aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd), which writes to the High Commission and the Board within 7 working days <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How long does PERKESO take to pay a death claim filed through the High Commission?

**Cases:**

- **October 2024:** an explosion at a chemical factory in Malaysia at about 11:30 am on the Thursday before 14 October 2024 burned three Bangladeshi workers from the same Ramzanbeg area of Munshiganj municipality over most of their bodies; Jabbar Ali (42) died on the Friday, Abu Taher (32) at dawn on the Saturday and his nephew Abdus Salam (24) at about 5 pm on the Sunday while under treatment <sup>[N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>. Jabbar Ali's nephew Khairul Islam said the three lay on the road for an hour and a half after the blast, and that some of them might have survived if treatment had started in time <sup>[N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>. On 14 October the Munshiganj Deputy Commissioner, Fatema Tul Jannat, said the district was in contact with the High Commission on how to bring the bodies home quickly, while the families demanded that the government bring them quickly <sup>[N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>.
- **July 2025:** Farhad Hossain alias Rony, 31, of Baguri village, Kayba union, Sharsha upazila, Jashore, who went to Malaysia in January 2023 at a cost of Tk 5 lakh, which his father borrowed, and worked as a labourer for a construction company in Seremban, was killed on 5 July 2025 when a crane chain snapped and he was crushed under it; his family said Bangladeshi workers there were trying to bring the body home, and his father said the loan had not yet been fully repaid <sup>[N226](#n226)</sup>.
- **August 2026:** on Monday 3 August 2026 three Bangladeshi men were killed in a fight among Bangladeshi plantation workers who all lived in the same house in Taman Linggi, Port Dickson, Negeri Sembilan; police arrested a 40-year-old Bangladeshi with no previous record and filed a murder case under section 302 of the Penal Code, the Negeri Sembilan police chief, DCP Aljafni Ahmed, said <sup>[N397](#n397)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-lose-the-job"></a>

### 22.3 If you lose the job

1. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-1"></a>**The employer must** tell the Labour Department within 30 days <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>. Under the MoU's standard contract it gives 2 months' notice or 2 months' wages, pays all money owed and pays your fare home unless you broke the contract <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. It sends you home on a Check-Out Memo <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>; with under 4 weeks' notice you may stay in its housing up to 4 weeks <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. You can't simply move to another employer ([If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer)) <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-2"></a>**To claim money owed,** go to the Labour Court (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 3) <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101)</sup> with:
   - your passport;
   - the workplace's name and address;
   - payslips and income details;
   - appointment letter and terms of service;
   - any termination letter.
3. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-3"></a>**If you were dismissed without just cause,** file a written representation to the Director General of Industrial Relations, by hand at the Industrial Relations Department (JPP) office nearest the workplace, within 60 days of the dismissal or the end of the notice period, and keep the acknowledgement; union membership isn't needed and you can represent yourself <sup>[350 (2021)](#o350), [N398](#n398)</sup>. JPP tries to settle, then sends the case to the Industrial Court, which can award compensation only if you asked for reinstatement <sup>[350 (2021)](#o350), [N398](#n398)</sup>. A letter showing the case is pending usually gets you a Special Pass (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 3) <sup>[N229 (2021)](#n229)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How does a worker find the address of the JPP office nearest the workplace?
   > - Can the representation be filed online?
   > - Is there a fee to file it?

4. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-4"></a>**Insurance:** coming home within 6 months of going after losing the job pays Tk 50,000 <sup>[21](#o21), [348](#o348)</sup>; see [Claiming the insurance](#claiming-the-insurance).

<a id="claiming-the-insurance"></a>

### 22.4 Claiming the insurance

The insurance bought at BMET clearance covers workers who left with clearance on or after 1 January 2023 <sup>[348](#o348), [21](#o21)</sup>. Cover for those who left between 19 December 2019 and 31 December 2022 lasted 2 years and has ended <sup>[N395](#n395)</sup>. What it pays: see [If a worker dies](#if-a-worker-dies), item 6; [If you are injured or fall ill, item 7: Insurance](#if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-7); [If you lose the job, item 4: Insurance](#if-you-lose-the-job-item-4).

> **Need to know**
> - Are workers who left between 1 January and 11 March 2023 covered, since BMET's leaflet gives the start as 12 March 2023 <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>?

1. <a id="claiming-the-insurance-item-1"></a>**Call 16135** (+8809610102030 from abroad); the Welfare Board helps claim from Jiban Bima Corporation <sup>[80](#o80), [324](#o324)</sup>.
2. <a id="claiming-the-insurance-item-2"></a>**Apply.** For a death, nothing is needed if the body came through the airport desk; after burial in Malaysia or death after return, apply at portal.wewb.gov.bd or at the Board <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>. For disability or a lost job, apply to the Board's welfare section <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>:
   - disability: Deputy Director, +88 02 48317085, 01321-158354, dd.welfare2@wewb.gov.bd;
   - lost job: Assistant Director, 01712-107301, jahanwewb@gmail.com.
3. <a id="claiming-the-insurance-item-3"></a>**You'll need:**
   - proof of clearance or membership and a passport copy <sup>[324](#o324)</sup>, with departure and arrival stamps for a lost job <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>;
   - for disability, medical papers and a 6R colour photo of the disabled part of the body <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>;
   - for a death, the papers in [If a worker dies, item 5: The Tk 3 lakh Welfare Board grant](#if-a-worker-dies-item-5).

   Free; 30 working days <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does Jiban Bima Corporation pay the insurance by bank transfer or cheque?

4. <a id="claiming-the-insurance-item-4"></a>**If it stalls** (families have waited months <sup>[N392](#n392)</sup>), complain in this order <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>:
   1. the Board's grievance officer, +88 02 55138267, 01684-501891, azizwelfare@gmail.com (30 working days);
   2. the Ministry's Joint Secretary (Administration), +88 02 41030261, jsadmin@probashi.gov.bd (20 working days);
   3. grs.gov.bd, the Cabinet Division's grievance system.

   The Ministry's complaint cell also takes email at minister.expat@gmail.com <sup>[199](#o199)</sup>.

<a id="coming-home"></a>

## 23 Coming home

- **Leaving Malaysia with cash.** You may carry any amount of foreign currency, and ringgit up to the equivalent of USD 10,000; above USD 10,000 in total, declare it on Customs form No. 22, available before the customs checkpoint, or face a fine of up to RM1 million, up to a year in jail or both <sup>[351](#o351), [352 (2009)](#o352)</sup>.
- **Customs in Bangladesh.**
  - Duty-free: 65 kg of baggage, 2 used phones and 1 new phone a year, and once a year, if declared, 100 g of gold or 200 g of silver jewellery (no more than 12 pieces of one type) <sup>[353](#o353)</sup>.
  - Once a year you may bring a declared gold bar up to 117 g (10 tola; duty Tk 5,000 per tola) or silver bar up to 234 g; a bigger or hidden bar is confiscated <sup>[353](#o353), [354](#o354)</sup>.
  - No alcohol on a Bangladeshi passport <sup>[353](#o353)</sup>.
  - Declare foreign currency over USD 10,000 on the FMJ form <sup>[354](#o354)</sup>.
  - Customs emergency line: +88 01319-056591 <sup>[354](#o354)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - May BMET card holders who have been abroad at least 6 months bring 2 new phones a year duty-free?

- **Airport help.** The Welfare Board's desks help with boarding cards, customs and lost luggage, take complaints of abuse or cheating, and send sick returnees to hospital by ambulance; Dhaka desk +8802 8901040, 01404 413388 <sup>[347](#o347), [335](#o335)</sup>. Women returning within a year after abuse or fraud get Tk 5,000 cash <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **A place to stay.**
  - The Wage Earners' Centre (Lonjonipara, Borua, Khilkhet, Dhaka-1229, near Shahjalal airport): Tk 100 admission, Tk 200 a night; book at wec.wewb.gov.bd or in person with proof of clearance or membership, a passport copy and the ticket; 01310 350555, 01754 715720 (24 hours) <sup>[355](#o355)</sup>.
  - The Probashi Lounge at Shahjalal airport gives free wifi and phone calls and food at up to 30% off when you show proof of clearance or membership <sup>[356](#o356)</sup>.
- **If your family is threatened or its property seized,** apply to the Welfare Board for legal help (myGov https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1638181581, the Board, the expatriate welfare branch of the Deputy Commissioner's office or the DEMO) with the worker's passport copy and any clearance or membership; free, up to 3 working days; the Board writes to the local administration, such as the Deputy Commissioner or Superintendent of Police <sup>[357](#o357), [358](#o358), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.
- **A business loan.** Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB, the government bank for migrants) lends returnees who start a project up to Tk 50 lakh, up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral, at 9% simple interest over up to 10 years; apply at the PKB branch for the business's area on its free form <sup>[359](#o359)</sup>.

  You'll need <sup>[359](#o359)</sup>:
  - 3 passport photos;
  - passport and NID copies;
  - a certificate of your present and permanent address from the pourashava (municipality) or union parishad;
  - a current trade licence;
  - project details with 2 years' income and expenses;
  - the premises' lease or ownership papers;
  - a declaration of your own investment and other loans;
  - copies of your return papers;
  - 3 signed cheque leaves from the business account;
  - a guarantor (a relative or anyone able to repay) with 2 photos, ID copy, address certificate, a clean CIB report (Bangladesh Bank's credit report showing no unpaid loans) and 3 signed cheque leaves.

  Fees: 0.50% processing and 0.50% documentation, each capped at Tk 15,000, each plus VAT, paid when you take the loan; no service charge <sup>[359](#o359)</sup>. Decision within 15 working days of a complete application <sup>[359](#o359)</sup>. Helpline 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.
- **BRAC's help.** BRAC's Migration Programme gives returnees and trafficking survivors airport help at Dhaka through its Migration Welfare Centre, counselling, money or materials to start earning, and legal help <sup>[360](#o360), [N399](#n399)</sup>. In one case, Amir Hossain, a worker found in a Penang forest ([Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)), was received at Dhaka airport on the night of 21-22 April 2026 by BRAC's Migration Programme and his family, with the help of Civil Aviation Security (AVSEC) and the Probashi Kallyan desk <sup>[N385](#n385)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - How does a returnee contact BRAC's Migration Programme?

- **Children's scholarships.** The Welfare Board pays migrants' children scholarships (Tk 27,500 a year at SSC level, Tk 34,000 at HSC) and reserves 0.5% of HSC admission seats for them <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - How does a family apply for the Welfare Board's children's scholarships?

<a id="contacts"></a>

## 24 Contacts

- **Bangladesh High Commission, Kuala Lumpur:** No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur; consular +603 2604 0949; passport service centre, 30 Jalan Ampang, +6010 430 3020 <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>. Labour wing: +60 12-290 3252 (counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd), +603 2604 0929, ministermalaysia@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>. Selim Reza of North South University's Centre for Migration Studies wrote in a Prothom Alo column in June 2026 that most consular services require Bangladeshis to travel to Kuala Lumpur, and that coming from Sabah, Sarawak, Penang or Johor to renew a passport or get other services is time-consuming and costly <sup>[N101](#n101)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Does the High Commission offer passport or other consular services outside Kuala Lumpur, such as camps in Sabah, Sarawak, Penang or Johor <sup>[N101](#n101)</sup>?
- **High Commission of Malaysia, Dhaka:** House 19, Road 6, Baridhara Diplomatic Enclave; +88 02 4108 1892; visa section dhaka@imi.gov.my <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>.
- **Probashi call centre (Welfare Board), 24 hours:** 16135, or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.
- **Welfare Board airport desks:** Dhaka +8802 8901040, 01404 413388 <sup>[335](#o335)</sup>; Chattogram 01913631797, pkdctg@gmail.com; Sylhet 01558277904, pkdsylhet@gmail.com <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Welfare Board grievance officer:** +88 02 55138267, 01684-501891, azizwelfare@gmail.com <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>.
- **Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare:** complaint cell minister.expat@gmail.com <sup>[199](#o199)</sup>; deaths and compensation aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>; Malaysia focal point Md. Hedayetul Islam Mondal, +880 241-030-241, +880 171-604-8115, dsemployment1@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[N400](#n400)</sup>.
- **BMET emigration:** Director (Emigration), +8802222222415, diremi@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. OEP hotline 09614445271 <sup>[75](#o75)</sup>.
- **PKB:** 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.
- **PERKESO:** 1 300 22 8000, perkeso@perkeso.gov.my; Menara PERKESO, 281 Jalan Ampang, 50538 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[327](#o327), [330](#o330)</sup>.
- **Labour Department of Peninsular Malaysia (JTKSM):** 03-8000 8000, jtksm@mohr.gov.my; offices by state at jtksm.mohr.gov.my/ms/hubungi-kami/alamat-ibu-pejabat-dan-cawangan <sup>[101 (2017)](#o101), [332](#o332)</sup>.
- **Tenaganita:** +603 7770 3671; 24-hour emergency lines for abuse, rescue, arrest and detention +6012 335 0512 and +6012 339 5350 <sup>[N343](#n343)</sup>.
- **Malaysia's repatriation programme (PRM 2.0, to 31 May 2027):** 03-8880 1269 <sup>[319](#o319)</sup>.
- **Bank Negara Malaysia (the central bank), BNMTELELINK:** 1-300-88-5465, for complaints when a bank refuses you an account without a reason or doesn't resolve a complaint, and to report or get advice about illegal money schemes and scams <sup>[348](#o348), [316](#o316)</sup>.
- **Migrants' own Facebook groups:** the group "Malaysia Kajer Khobor" (Malaysia work news) and the page "Malaysiaprobashi Bangladeshi", one of whose admins is Rakib Rana, are used by Bangladeshi migrants in Malaysia, Prothom Alo reported in June 2024 <sup>[N148 (2024)](#n148)</sup>.
